# Arcelle — UI/UX, view quality and simplification

_2026-08-17 · companion to `full-review-2026-08-17-defects.md`_


332 items from 13 review lenses, each passed through a grounding check that
verified the described control actually exists in the source and rewrote any
proposal too vague to act on. Items marked `PARTLY` had their description
corrected by that pass — the file is right, the original reading overstated it.

House constraints respected throughout: no ambient motion (reverted twice before,
in writing), no telemetry, no cloud sync. Proposals that would have added surface
area for its own sake were struck.


---

## Simplification — things to delete or merge


- **[high impact · small]** 'I saved it' walks straight past the guard that 'Skip for now' carries
  `src/screens/RecoveryModal.tsx:89`
  _Today:_ skip() (RecoveryModal.tsx:24-36) shows a native confirm — 'This code is shown once and never again… Continue without saving it?' — whenever recoveryCopied is false. But 'I saved it' (89-91) is className='primary' and calls onDismiss() with no check at all. 'Skip for now' (93-95) is a .subtle button sitting BELOW the button row, so the guarded answer is also the quietest-looking one. Once Copy has been pressed the row holds two primaries at once (73 and 89).
  _Do:_ Delete the 'Skip for now' button (lines 93-95) and change 'I saved it' (89) to onClick={() => void skip()}. Four controls become three, the guard becomes unavoidable for anyone who neither copied nor printed, and Escape (line 41) already goes the same way. Keep exactly one primary in the row: 'I saved it'.

- **[high impact · small]** SIMPLIFICATION: delete the e2e suite that runs nowhere, and give its name to the one that runs
  `e2e/wdio.conf.mjs:15`
  _Today:_ `npm run e2e` (package.json:22) points at e2e/wdio.conf.mjs (143 lines), which refuses on macOS immediately — no macOS tauri-driver, and the Linux build is impossible because whisper-rs is pinned to Apple's `metal` feature outside any target gate (e2e/README.md:130-138). README:11 lists the suite as running "nowhere — see below" and then spends its longest section, :119-152, saying why. Its only spec is e2e/specs/smoke.e2e.mjs (87 lines).
  _Do:_ Delete `e2e/wdio.conf.mjs` and `e2e/specs/`. Keep `e2e/mock-ollama.mjs` and `e2e/fixtures/` — they still serve `npm run e2e:mock`, which works on a Mac and is how the app is driven by hand with no model loaded (README:154-163). Rename `e2e:qa` → `e2e` in package.json, keeping `e2e:qa` as an alias for one release. Cut README's table (:6-11) from four rows to three and replace the HLT-8 section (:119-152) with three lines under `e2e:mock`.

- **[high impact · medium]** Two different front pages for the same room, and new users never see the real one
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:484`
  _Today:_ frontPageView is null unless fileCount, chatCount or memories are non-zero (ViewerPane.tsx:484-487). Below that threshold the centre renders the 'Your room is sealed' sheet (1263-1305); above it, FrontPage (1262). The two share no structure, headings or actions. The sealed sheet is the only place in the app offering Add a file / Summarize room / Ask the room, and it becomes unreachable the moment the first file lands. FrontPage's own empty state (468-471) then reads 'Nothing here yet — add a file or ask the room a question' while offering no control that does either.
  _Do:_ Change ViewerPane.tsx:484-487 to `const frontPageView = s.fp;` (keeping the null guard, dropping the count thresholds), delete the viewer-empty branch (1263-1305), and move its three buttons into FrontPage's Continue section as that section's empty state, replacing the dead sentence at 468-471. Keep the 'Encrypted on your Mac' note (1298-1304) as that section's footer. One page, one masthead, one set of starting actions that stay reachable.

- **[high impact · medium]** Three per-kind sets live outside the registry that says it is the only per-kind table
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:69`
  _Today:_ registry.tsx:19-31 states the design: one row per kind, 'exactly ONE place per concern.' Three more per-kind sets are maintained by hand elsewhere: FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS (ViewerPane.tsx:69-82), READER_KINDS (ReaderShell.tsx:17-24) and QUOTABLE_KINDS (quoteSelection.ts:92-97). FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS is already stale for five kinds: `.sk-page` asks `height: 100%` (sketch.css:12-17), `.rec-view` asks `height: 100%; min-height: 0` (recording.css:33-37), `.audio-view` the same (composer.css:595-601, used by both the audio and video rows, registry.tsx:351/368) and `.img-scroll` asks `flex: 1; min-height: 0` (viewer-formats.css:548-552) — the exact contract the set exists to satisfy — and sketch, recording, audio, video and image are all absent from it. Without `fill`, `.viewer-body` is a padded `overflow:auto` block (viewer.css:88-96), so `height:100%` resolves against auto and the promised height never arrives (the mechanism is documented at viewer.css:345-357).
  _Do:_ Move all three onto FormatEntry in registry.tsx as `fill?: boolean`, `reader?: boolean`, `quotable?: boolean`. ViewerPane reads `FORMATS[kind].fill`, ReaderShell exports nothing but its card and the drift test reads `.reader`, quoteSelection reads `.quotable`. Delete the three Sets. Adding a format then means filling in one row, which is what the file already promises. Note quoteSelection.test.mjs:28 textually parses READER_KINDS out of ReaderShell.tsx and would need repointing at the registry.

- **[high impact · medium]** Re-cut the Privacy page: 'Privacy' is really lock+keys, and holds two room-maintenance tools
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/PrivacySection.tsx:110`
  _Today:_ The 'Privacy & recovery' page stacks three sections: Cloud privacy (CloudPrivacySection.tsx:121), a section whose h3 is also 'Privacy' (PrivacySection.tsx:110) and Recovery key (RecoverySection.tsx:25). The middle one contains auto-lock (113-125), change password (128-207), Touch ID (210-235), Duplicate room (238-270) and Compact room (273-313). Settings.tsx:39-46 records that this section's retired id-label was 'Lock & password'. The password sheet and the Recovery section issue one-time codes for the same sidecar and need cross-hook wiring so the two never contradict (Settings.tsx:518-524, 555-559). Meanwhile 'History & storage' is a whole page holding a single section (Settings.tsx:619-621), whose own comment already places it 'with the other whole-room safety ops (duplicate/compact)' (CheckpointsSection.tsx:39-41).
  _Do:_ Three moves. (1) Rename PrivacySection's h3 (line 110) to 'Lock & keys' and inline RecoverySection's body under the password block — one section, one recovery-code sheet, and the cross-wiring at Settings.tsx:518-524 and 555-559 deletes itself along with RecoverySection.tsx and the set-recovery id. (2) Move the Duplicate room block (PrivacySection.tsx:238-270) and the Compact room block (273-313) into CheckpointsSection's page, retitled 'Room copies & storage' — its own comment already says they belong together. (3) The privacy page is then 'Privacy & keys' with exactly two sections: Cloud privacy, Lock & keys. Update SETTINGS_GROUPS (Settings.tsx:66-69, 75) accordingly.

- **[high impact · medium]** Merge 'AI helpers' into 'Model' — they are the same downloader with two headings
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/HelpersSection.tsx:46`
  _Today:_ HelpersSection (157 lines) offers two buttons: 'Download a local vision helper' and 'Turn on semantic search'. It shares pullPercent, pullStatus, stopPull, stoppingPull and pulling with ModelSection — Settings.tsx threads the same values from one useModelManagement hook into both (415-446 and 471-486) — and renders a second, near-identical .pull-progress block (HelpersSection.tsx:123-148 vs ModelSection.tsx:201-213), differing only in that the helpers copy carries the Stop button (140-146) and ModelSection's does not (its Stop is in the pull-row at 195-199, gated on `pulling`). Its own copy sends the user back to the other section: 'Any model with the "vision" badge in the Model section does this' (81-86).
  _Do:_ Fold HelpersSection's two rows into ModelSection under a 'Helpers' label after the download row, and unify the progress renderer into ONE block that is shown when (pullStatus || pullPercent != null) and carries the Stop button whenever (pulling || pullingSpecial) — that is strictly more capable than either block today and is what makes the merge safe. Then delete HelpersSection.tsx, set-helpers from SETTINGS_GROUPS (Settings.tsx:55) and the duplicated prop threading at Settings.tsx:471-486. The AI page drops from six sections to five (four with item 8).

- **[high impact · medium]** Draw the agent graph only in the overlay; leave a roster line in the transcript
  `src/workspace/AgentGraph.tsx:300`
  _Today:_ `AgentGraph` renders `<GraphCanvas/>` inline inside the chat bubble (line 300): an SVG edge layer measured from the laid-out DOM with a ResizeObserver (lines 393-449), hub and child NodeCards with label + instruction subtitle + elapsed, `N in parallel` band tags, and a click-to-open `Inspector` that grows the bubble past the viewport (hence its `autoScroll` prop, line 594). `.agraph-mini-chip` (line 665) already exists but as a plain `<span>` — glyph + label, no elapsed, not clickable — inside the hub inspector's "Delegations" row.
  _Do:_ In the inline render, drop `<GraphCanvas/>` (line 300) and keep `.agraph-head` (caption, `N running` / `N/M done`, Expand — lines 277-292) plus one row of per-specialist chips: promote `.agraph-mini-chip` from a `<span>` to a `<button>`, add `elapsedFor(c.key)` beside its glyph + label, and wire `onClick` to the same `toggle(key)` that opens the existing `Inspector`. `GraphCanvas` stays untouched inside `agraph-modal` behind Expand (line 349). This removes the inline SVG measurement path, the band rails and the edge-label logic from every message in the transcript.

- **[high impact · medium]** shell.css carries 215 lines of Studio/Activity CSS that is shadowed in every instance
  `src/styles/shell.css:1812`
  _Today:_ shell.css:1812-1893 (.studio-tab-view through .studio-note strong), 2029-2034 (.studio-shelf) and 2038-2181 (.activity-view through .activity-empty) style the AI column's two non-chat tabs. aiPane.css re-states every one of those selectors scoped to `.pane-ai` (104-330 for studio, 335-580 for activity) and wins on specificity regardless of load order; aiPane.css:31-36 says exactly that, and adds 'the scope is also simply true: nothing here renders anywhere else'. The markup agrees: .studio-tab-view and .activity-view appear only in AiPane.tsx (322, 332, 479), .studio-shelf only in StudioShelf.tsx:33, all inside `.pane-ai`. A selector-by-selector diff of the three bands against aiPane.css finds no shell class without a .pane-ai counterpart. Two things in the band are NOT shadowed and are load-bearing: `.activity-progress span` (2093-2099), the only rule giving the fill span `display: block; height: 100%; width: 0`, and `.activity-progress span.indeterminate` plus @keyframes activity-indeterminate and its reduced-motion block (2100-2121), which AiPane.tsx:570/596/631 actually render.
  _Do:_ Move to aiPane.css, rescoped to `.pane-ai`: shell.css:2093-2099 (`.activity-progress span` — keep display/height/width, drop `transition: width 0.3s ease`, which aiPane.css:461 already supersedes with --dur-slow/--ease-pen) and shell.css:2100-2121 (the indeterminate rule, its keyframe and its reduced-motion block, whose comment about keeping the 40% stub must travel with it). Only then delete shell.css:1812-1893, 2029-2034, 2038-2092 and 2122-2181. That is ~215 lines, and it takes 10 of shell.css's 28 sub-floor font sizes with it. Leave .activity-rail (shell.css:401-590) alone — different component, same prefix.

- **[high impact · medium]** paper.css ships 35 primitives with no consumers, five of them decorative draw-in animations
  `src/styles/paper.css:1358`
  _Today:_ paper.css is 1469 lines and defines 90 top-level .nb-* selectors. 35 are worn by no element in any .tsx: .nb-annot-l, .nb-axes, .nb-bar, .nb-bar-h, .nb-bar-track, .nb-bar-v, .nb-card-soft, .nb-connect-h, .nb-frame--b/--c/--d, .nb-gridlines, .nb-hand, .nb-hand-lg, .nb-ico-bookmark/-clip/-file/-mic/-star, .nb-lead-ico, .nb-list-groups, .nb-press, .nb-progress, .nb-progress-fill, .nb-ringed, .nb-rule-v, .nb-swash, .nb-tilt, .nb-tilt-b, .nb-tilt-set, and the five draw-in classes .nb-mark-draw (1358), .nb-swash-draw (1367), .nb-trace-in (1402), .nb-check-draw (1420) and .nb-underline-draw::after (1459) with their keyframes nb-mark-in, nb-swash-in, nb-trace, nb-check and nb-draw. .nb-float-in and .nb-float-draw DO have consumers (QuickActions.tsx:150, SkillsView.tsx:662, RecoveryModal.tsx:58, plus five animation references in CSS). Six of the 35 are named as recipes in comments elsewhere (tokenBudget.css:8, sidebar.css:119, settings.css:1446, aiPane.css:40 and :420, composer.css:167, skills.css:1260), and .nb-press and .nb-progress-fill appear in paper.css's own reduced-motion block (1450-1461).
  _Do:_ Delete the 35 rules and the five orphan keyframes, and in the same commit (a) remove the dead names from the reduced-motion lists at paper.css:1440-1445 and 1450-1461, and (b) fix the six comments that point at them — tokenBudget.css:8, sidebar.css:119, settings.css:1446, aiPane.css:420, composer.css:167, skills.css:1260 — to describe the recipe rather than cite a class that no longer exists. Keep .nb-float-in and .nb-float-draw (real consumers, and the motion communicates a surface arriving). Add one line to paper.css's header: 'This library is what the app uses. A primitive with no consumer is deleted, not kept warm.' Expect 250-300 lines out of 1469.

- **[high impact · medium]** Ten modals say aria-modal="true" and let Tab walk out behind them
  `src/workspace/CompareModal.tsx:59`
  _Today:_ `useFocusTrap` (src/settings/useFocusTrap.ts) does Tab/Shift+Tab cycling, Escape, focus-in on open and focus restored to the trigger on close, and has four callers: Settings.tsx:120, CustomizeSidebar.tsx:145, Overlays.tsx:195 (ApproveCard), RecoveryModal.tsx:41. Ten other dialogs declare `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` without it: CompareModal.tsx:59, StudioModal.tsx:44, FeedbackModal.tsx:129, AiActionModal.tsx:49, UnsavedEditsDialog.tsx:85, AgentGraph.tsx:329, Overlays.tsx:373 (ShortcutsSheet), PicturePicker.tsx:53, DocumentPicker.tsx:54, FilmReview.tsx:78. Six of them hand-roll the same `useEffect` + `window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey, true)` + `e.stopPropagation()` block with its own comment explaining the same capture-phase reasoning (CompareModal.tsx:33-44, StudioModal.tsx:14-27, UnsavedEditsDialog.tsx:36-47, AiActionModal.tsx:16-30, AgentGraph.tsx:188-196, Overlays.tsx:357-365); the remaining four have no key handling whatsoever.
  _Do:_ Replace the six hand-rolled Escape effects with `useFocusTrap`, wiring `ref={modalRef} tabIndex={-1} onKeyDown={...onModalKeyDown}` onto the dialog div exactly as ApproveCard does (Overlays.tsx:195-215, which also shows the `if (e.key === "Escape") e.stopPropagation()` line that keeps the app-level handler from closing the file underneath). Net deletion of roughly eighty lines and six duplicated comments, and the four Escape-less dialogs get Escape, the trap and focus-in for one line each. Where a dialog owns a nested Escape (StudioModal's and AiActionModal's @-file list), keep the existing guard in the textarea's own onKeyDown — it already runs first and returns — rather than re-listening on window.

- **[high impact · large]** Four sources of truth for "where am I", reconciled by hand in eleven files
  `src/Workspace.tsx:205`
  _Today:_ The destination is not one value. `s.area` holds eight of the eleven, and Workflows, Scripts and Room Map are three separate booleans that outrank it: `const area: WorkArea = s.showWorkflows ? "workflows" : s.showScripts ? "scripts" : s.showMap ? "map" : s.area` (Workspace.tsx:205-211). Because the flags win, every navigation has to clear the other three by hand — `showArea` does it four times (Workspace.tsx:236-260), `leaveAreas` in the palette does it (Overlays.tsx:235-240), `goArea` does it (FrontPage.tsx:409-413), `revealMemory` and `revealBrowser` each do it (miscActions.ts:339-343, 381-385), `showFileHere` does it (Workspace.tsx:586-590). There are 41 write sites across 11 files (`setShowWorkflows`/`setShowScripts`/`setShowMap`), plus a whole effect whose only job is to notice a flag going up and deselect the tab strip (`areaFlagsRef`, Workspace.tsx:603-620), plus an Escape ladder in effects.ts:855-876 that must close them in the right order.
  _Do:_ Fold the three flags into `s.area`. `showWorkflows` → `area === "workflows"`, `showScripts` → `area === "scripts"`, `showMap` → `area === "map"`; delete the derivation at Workspace.tsx:205-211 and the `areaFlagsRef` effect at Workspace.tsx:603-620; `wfDetailId` and the map's own state stay as they are. The reads are the cheap half (22 sites) and the writes collapse into `goToArea`. Do it after the `goToArea` lift, and keep `s.showMapRef`-style refs only where the Escape ladder needs them (effects.ts:855-876).

- **[high impact · large]** Delete the deferred-Save model and the whole unsaved apparatus it required
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/Settings.tsx:324`
  _Today:_ Settings runs three save idioms at once. Most controls apply on change (useBehaviorSettings.ts:91-115, usePrivacy.ts:58-61, useModelManagement.ts:270-277, AppearanceSection.tsx:27-30, useInterfaceSettings.ts:85-105). Four sections defer to a Save button: Behavior (BehaviorSection.tsx:109-113, covering only the creativity slider and custom instructions), Spoken voice (VoiceSection.tsx:195-197), Online features (OnlineSection.tsx:162-168), Remote AI (RemoteAiSection.tsx:58-60). One saves on blur with no confirmation (CloudPrivacySection.tsx:296). Those four buttons are the sole reason Settings.tsx carries unsavedRef (111), requestClose (113-119), the confirmClose strip (373-387), the dirtyPages set (334-340), the nav 'Unsaved' flag (405-409), and four dirty flags computed in four hooks (useBehaviorSettings.ts:126-128, useVoiceSettings, useOnlineSearch.ts:37, useRemoteAi.ts:65) — plus OnlineSection's duplicate 'Not saved yet' note (172-184), .settings-unsaved in src/styles/settings.css:217-243, and one whole test in e2e/page-script/settingsPages.test.mjs.
  _Do:_ Make all four apply on change, then delete the machinery. (a) OnlineSection: drop Save, call api.setSetting from each setter in useOnlineSearch.ts, keep 'Test search'. (b) RemoteAiSection: save on blur as well as Enter (the Enter path is already at RemoteAiSection.tsx:38), drop Save. (c) VoiceSection: call save() from setNeuralVoiceId and pickArchetype directly, and for the two sliders from onPointerUp/onKeyUp — NOT from setParam, which fires per pixel — keep Preview. (d) BehaviorSection: save instructions on blur and temperature on pointer-up, keep the transient 'Saved' tick at line 111. Then remove Settings.tsx 111-119, 321-340, 373-387, 405-409; the four dirty flags; OnlineSection.tsx:172-184; .settings-unsaved from src/styles/settings.css; and rewrite e2e/page-script/settingsPages.test.mjs's 'closing Settings with unsaved work asks first' test into 'every Settings control persists on change' (assert no `Save` button strings remain in the four sections). Net: four buttons, one modal strip, one nav badge, four state pairs and one test gone.

- **[medium impact · small]** Delete the AI-written subtitle on Room Home
  `src/workspace/FrontPage.tsx:433`
  _Today:_ FrontPage builds homeDekFacts from the file count, chat count, running-job count and the newest timeline entry (424-432), then calls useAdaptiveText to have a model write a 20-word sentence from them (433-442). If it returns within 400ms (adaptiveText.ts:89, 191-193) it replaces the static subtitle (449-456): 'Recent work, current background activity, and everything this room can do — nothing here leaves this Mac on its own.' The facts it restates are already on screen — RoomStamp prints the same file and chat counts three lines to the right (196-200) and the timeline below prints the newest entry.
  _Do:_ Delete the useAdaptiveText call and homeDekFacts (FrontPage.tsx:424-442) and render the static subtitle unconditionally at 449-456. The adaptiveText module keeps its other consumers; Home stops paying a model call to restate its own stamp, and the privacy line stops being conditional on a race. See the added item below: this same deletion also closes an outbound-data hole.

- **[medium impact · small]** "Show deleted" is offered on every recording, including the ones with nothing deleted
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:1484`
  _Today:_ `const hasWords = segments.some((s) => s.words.length > 0);` (RecordingView.tsx:1484) is true of every word-timed transcript, edited or not. It gates the tab-bar checkbox `Show deleted` (2091-2099), so a freshly transcribed recording permanently carries a checkbox whose only effect is nothing. The correct expression is already written 1,150 lines later as the disabled test for "Export edited copy": `!cuts.length && !segments.some((s) => s.words.some((w) => w.del))` (2643).
  _Do:_ Add `const hasDeleted = segments.some((s) => s.words.some((w) => w.del));` beside `hasWords`, gate the checkbox (2091) on it, and reuse it in the export button's `disabled` (2643) so the two can never disagree. `hasWords` then has one consumer left (the export button's presence, 2640) and can be inlined. Move the predicate to recReview.ts as `hasDeletedWords(segments)` with a test.

- **[medium impact · small]** Two controls in one toolbar open the same journal
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:915`
  _Today:_ Eight controls share `.browser-chrome`, whose stylesheet comment says so and makes it wrap to a second row rather than overflow (browser.css:134-153). The shield's accessible name already leads with the action — "Show the activity journal. {claim.detail}" (:720).
  _Do:_ Delete the "Journal" button (BrowserView.tsx:915-923). The shield keeps the toggle — pressing a privacy claim to see the evidence behind it is a coherent model — and `.browser-shield[aria-pressed="true"]::after` (browser.css:482) already marks the open state. Keep `aria-pressed` as it is rather than swapping it for `aria-expanded`, or that selector stops matching; if `aria-expanded` is wanted, add it alongside and update the selector in the same edit. If the owner would rather keep a named control, do the inverse: strip the shield's button role so a privacy claim is a status rather than a control, and keep "Journal". Either way the strip loses one control.

"Download video" (:705-710) is rendered unconditionally at :965-971 and only makes sense on that same YouTube branch.

Route "Save page" through `needsFreshFetch` → `importLink`, which is exactly what `saveLink` (:696-704) already does, then delete the "Save link" button (:958-964) and the `saveLink` binding. Render "Download video" only when `needsFreshFetch(info.url)` is true, so it appears where it is the right answer and nowhere else. browserTrust.test.mjs:252-265 asserts four gated rows in that strip — update the expected count in the same change, and it will keep pinning that whatever survives is gated on `can.save`.

- **[medium impact · small]** Live tool-step chips are unbounded, duplicate the graph, and vanish when the turn ends

Keep `.lane-chip` in both cases. On the single-agent path, cap the visible chips at the last 6 with a `+N earlier` count so a long tool loop cannot own the bubble.

- **[medium impact · small]** Two pill idioms coexist — 47 sites, 11 different hand-tuned values
  `src/styles/paper.css:1012`
  _Today:_ The app draws a chip two incompatible ways. Wobbly ellipse pill (`999px 999px 999px 999px / Npx Mpx…`): 35 rules over 10 distinct vertical-radius pairs — 15× '14px 12px 14px 12px' (.nb-chip paper.css:1012 and 14 others), 6× '12px 10px 12px 10px' (.count sidebar.css:284 among them), 4× '8px 6px 8px 6px' (including aiPane.css:448's progress track), then 18/15, 16/14, 13/11 twice each and 22/20, 15/13, 7/5, 6/5 once. True pill (`border-radius: 999px`): 12 rules — shell.css:1406 (.scope-tag), 1531 (.library-chip), 2090 (.activity-progress), 2643 (.nav-chip), create.css:1020 and 1112, workspace.css:242 and 356, settings.css:1763, settings-annotations.css:161, viewer.css:285, onboarding.css:295. The two idioms share the sidebar: .nav-chip and .library-chip are true pills a few pixels from .count, which wobbles.
  _Do:_ Add two tokens beside --radius-lg in tokens.css: `--radius-pill: 999px` and `--radius-pill-hand: 999px 999px 999px 999px / 14px 12px 14px 12px`. Then one per role, deleting the other nine values: chips, badges and tags take --radius-pill-hand (already the majority at 15 sites); switch tracks and progress tracks take --radius-pill, because a track is a mechanism and a mechanism does not wobble. Four one-off tracks stop inventing their own — .token-bar-track (tokenBudget.css:74), .pw-meter-track (gate.css:662), .pull-bar (settings.css:1432), .pdf-scan-bar (pdf.css:266). Note aiPane.css:448's 8/6 wobble on .activity-progress is a track and would flip to --radius-pill under this rule; that is a visible change to the AI pane's progress bar, so make it deliberately or exempt it in writing.

- **[medium impact · small]** Settings' model delete re-implements DeleteControl and strands focus
  `src/settings/ModelSection.tsx:119`
  _Today:_ ModelSection.tsx:119-147 hand-rolls the arm/confirm pattern: when `confirmModel === m` the trash button is replaced by `<span className="model-confirm">` with a 'Delete?' hint and confirm-yes / confirm-no buttons (121-137). There is no ref and no focus effect, no `role="alert"`, and no `data-agent-blocked`. The shared `DeleteControl` (src/workspace/DeleteControl.tsx:30-90) does all four: it focuses the ✓ on arm (44-46), returns focus to the trigger when disarming left focus on the body (47-53), wraps the prompt in `role="alert" data-agent-blocked` (61), and sets `aria-label={title}` on the trigger (86).
  _Do:_ Replace ModelSection.tsx:119-147 with `<DeleteControl k={`model:${m}`} trigger={<TrashIcon size={14} />} title={`Delete ${m} from disk`} question="Delete?" onConfirm={() => confirmRemoveModel(m)} confirmDelete={...} askConfirm={askRemoveModel} cancelConfirm={cancelRemoveModel} />`, keeping the `disabled={m === model}` case as a plain titled button. That deletes about twenty-five lines, inherits the focus handling, the alert role and the agent block, and supplies the missing accessible name that the last checked item is about.

- **[medium impact · small]** SIMPLIFICATION: two wdio configs each carry their own copy of the qa.html vehicle
  `e2e/wdio.qa.conf.mjs:1`
  _Today:_ e2e/wdio.qa.conf.mjs (114 lines) and e2e/wdio.capture.conf.mjs (107 lines) are the same vehicle twice: build → `qa/make-qa.mjs` → `vite preview --host 127.0.0.1 --strictPort` → headless Chrome against dist/qa.html. Diffing them, the only genuine differences are the port (4173/4174), the specs glob, `mochaOpts.timeout` (60s vs 45min), two Chrome flags (`--hide-scrollbars`, `--force-device-scale-factor=1`) and one error-message label. Roughly 85-90 lines per file — `onPrepare`, `onComplete`, `run()`, `waitFor()`, the capabilities block — are duplicated, including the `--host 127.0.0.1` fix and the kill-the-preview-on-failure branch whose reasons survive only as a comment in the qa copy.
  _Do:_ Extract `e2e/qaVehicle.mjs` exporting `startVehicle(port)` / `stopVehicle()` and `chromeCaps(extraArgs)`, holding the build → make-qa → preview → waitFor sequence and its two comments once. Both configs import it and keep only what genuinely differs: port, specs glob, `mochaOpts.timeout`, and capture's two determinism flags. Net: ~85 duplicated lines per file collapse to one ~60-line module and two ~25-line configs.

Fold auditWave15.test.mjs's assertions into the subject files they belong to. Rule going forward: the filename names the capability, the comment names the packet.

  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:926`
  _Today:_ Seven regions can appear in normal flow between the chrome and the stage: the Save strip (BrowserView.tsx:926-976), the notice banner (:977-984), the results row (:993-1002), the takeover banner (:1004-1009), the protection alert (:1016-1034), the stalled banner (:1039-1043) and the error banner (:1044-1073). Each one changes the stage's height, which the ResizeObserver (created at :264, calling `pushBounds` at :232-251) pushes to Rust as a new native-view rect. `runSave` sets `notice` or `error` and never both (:670-682), but `openResultInNewTab`'s 4-second notice (:618-623) can still be up when a later failure sets `error`, so two Dismiss buttons for two banners about the same action is reachable.
  _Do:_ Two removals and one merge. Delete the takeover banner (:1004-1009): the button beside it already reads "Hand back to the agent" and `.browser-takeover.on::after` (browser.css:534-541) already rings it. Merge `notice` and `error` into a single `report: {tone, text, action?}` state with one banner and one Dismiss, so the "search for this instead" recovery (:1050-1062) becomes that banner's `action`. Keep the protection alert and the stalled banner separate — they are claims about the page rather than about an action, and they are the two this app must never bury.

- **[medium impact · medium]** The Create page and the Creations sidebar are the same list, twice
  `src/workspace/create/CreatePage.tsx:505`
  _Today:_ `Canvas` renders three populations: failed jobs with their reason (CreatePage.tsx:505-522, capped at three by `.slice(0, 3)` at line 143), active jobs with a hand-rolled meter (525-548), and files where `f.source === "generated"` as a thumbnail grid (568-591). `CreationsNav` (Sidebar.tsx:1454+) renders "Making now", "Didn't finish" (uncapped) and "Made in this room" for the same destination, the latter filtered by `isCreationFile` — `f.originDestination === "create"` (types.ts:165). Both are visible together whenever the contextual sidebar is open in Create, which is its default.
  _Do:_ Split them by what each can actually do. The sidebar keeps the running / failed / queued rows and gains the Stop controls (see the stop-control item), since it is uncapped and already scrolls. `Canvas` drops the failed and active cards entirely (CreatePage.tsx:505-548) and keeps only what a narrow rail cannot show — the thumbnail grid at a size you can judge a picture by — with its `cr-sec-head` count (569-574) as the only chrome.

- **[medium impact · medium]** StudioModal and AiActionModal are one dialog written twice
  `src/workspace/StudioModal.tsx:1`
  _Today:_ StudioModal.tsx (158 lines) and AiActionModal.tsx (239 lines) share every class name, the same `@`-mention popover over `s.studioAc` / `a.studioAcItems()` / `a.acceptMention`, the same `s.studioPromptRef` textarea with a byte-identical ArrowUp/ArrowDown/Enter/Tab/Escape/⌘Enter `onKeyDown` (StudioModal 101-140, AiActionModal 158-197), and the same capture-phase Escape effect — StudioModal.tsx:18-20 says so in a comment: "same bug as AiActionModal — one shared shape, one shared fix". They differ in the title string, an optional question/language field (AiActionModal 67-119), and whether a run keeps the dialog open.
  _Do:_ Extract one `PromptRunModal({ title, hint, extraField, running, onStop, onRun, value, onChange, acceptTarget })` holding the backdrop, the Escape effect, the mention popover, the textarea with its keydown, and the action row. StudioModal becomes a call with `running={false}` and no `extraField`; AiActionModal becomes a call passing its language/question input as `extraField` and `s.aiBusy` as `running`. Both files' bodies are then deleted, which is the point.

- **[medium impact · medium]** Three copies of the same trust label are on screen at once
  `src/workspace/TopBar.tsx:303`
  _Today:_ `trustState(cloud, s.privacyOn)` (markup.ts:57) renders as `.privacy-badge` in the top bar (TopBar.tsx:303-320, unconditional), as `.status-trust` in the status bar (src/shell/StatusBar.tsx:84-89, unconditional), and as `.local-mini` in the chat context strip (AiPane.tsx:237-244).
  _Do:_ Merge the top-bar badge into the model pill: `.model-pill-name` gains a second span carrying `trust.label`, and the pill takes `trust.tone` as a class — one control naming the engine and its consequence, in the place you go to change it. Delete the `.privacy-badge` IIFE at TopBar.tsx:303-321. The status bar keeps the room-wide readout; the chat strip keeps the per-turn one.

- **[medium impact · medium]** Workflows and Scripts are the last two pages that are secretly overlays
  `src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowsPage.tsx:25`
  _Today:_ Skills, Connectors, Memory and Recordings render off `area` (ViewerPane.tsx:1244-1260). Workflows, Scripts and Room Map render off boolean flags checked earlier in the same chain (:1228-1235), so they need: a Close button for two of the three (WorkflowsPage.tsx:25, ScriptsPage.tsx:22 — Map has none), an Escape branch each (effects.ts:860-875), a special case in `showArea` (Workspace.tsx:236-250), a derived `area` (Workspace.tsx:205-211), and flag-clearing calls scattered through Overlays.tsx:237-238, FrontPage.tsx:410-411, miscActions.ts:340/382 and ViewerPane.tsx:228-229. Pressing Close only sets the flag false: `area` falls back to `s.area` (which `openWorkflows` left as it was), the rail selection moves, the second column changes title and the pane shows whatever Home was showing.
  _Do:_ Delete both Close buttons and the two Escape branches at effects.ts:866-875, and make the pages ordinary `area` destinations like Skills: `case "workflows"` / `case "scripts"` in the ViewerPane chain, `s.setArea(...)` inside `openWorkflows`/`openScripts`, and drop `showWorkflows`/`showScripts` and their refs from state.ts:486-499 plus the six clearing sites. Do Map in the same pass or the branch survives for one page. (If the previous item lands, this is one destination fewer again.)

- **[medium impact · medium]** Nine menus have no keyboard grammar; the good one is a private hook
  `src/workspace/Overlays.tsx:117`
  _Today:_ `useMenuKeys` (Overlays.tsx:116-165) implements the full menu contract — focus the first item on open, wrapping ArrowDown/ArrowUp, Home/End, Escape, and a `revision` parameter that re-focuses when an armed confirm swaps the item list. It is not exported and has two callers, both in the same file (512, 520). QuickActions.tsx:74-95 is a second, inline copy of the same logic. Nine other `role="menu"` popovers have nothing: LayoutMenu.tsx:66, TopBar.tsx:393, Sidebar.tsx:370, ViewerPane.tsx:218 and 874, SketchView.tsx:1327, 1397, 1554, and src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:311.
  _Do:_ Export `useMenuKeys` into a shared module (it already handles the awkward case — an item list that changes while open) and adopt it at all nine sites: `ref` + `onKeyDown` on the `.pop-menu` div, and delete QuickActions.tsx:74-95's duplicate in the same pass. Drop each local `menu-backdrop` in favour of the hook's Escape plus the existing single-slot `openMenu` dismissal (TopBar.tsx:93-108), which is the item above's fix for ViewerPane. That removes nine backdrop divs and one duplicated hook, and gives every menu in the app one grammar.

- **[medium impact · large]** Merge Notes, Highlights and Chapters into one Findings tab
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:121`
  _Today:_ Four tabs (RecordingView.tsx:121-126), three of which render the same `ReadPanel` component with different `empty`/`blank` strings (2275-2319). All three read one `meta`, are filled by one job, and are refreshed by one event (`onRecReadDone`, 720-727). Because it is one component instantiated three times, the reader meets three copies of the same furniture: the read button (356-366), the two empty states (368-392) and the stale banner "The transcript changed since this was read." (456-460) appear once per tab. The split is invisible from the transcript, which is why both selection actions end with a toast naming the destination: "Marked. It's in Highlights." (1141) and "Saved. It's in Notes." (1157).
  _Do:_ Two tabs: `Transcript` and `Findings`. `ReadPanel` keeps its `row()` renderer and gains ruled section heads — Decisions / To do / Open / Points (it already has `NOTE_ORDER`, 251), then Marked moments, then Chapters — using the `SectionHead` pattern from RecordingsPage.tsx:479-486. One read button, one stale banner, one empty state ("Not read yet"); "Add chapter here" (330-341) moves to the Chapters section head. `tabCount` (1213-1218) returns notes+highlights+chapters for the merged tab. Then the two toasts at 1141 and 1157 can drop their location clause. While in here, delete the stale TABS doc comment at 110-120, which still describes an `empty` flag the array no longer has.

- **[medium impact · large]** Scripts is a Workflows page with one node, kept as a separate destination
  `src/workspace/scripts/ScriptsPage.tsx:11`
  _Today:_ A script becomes a workflow the first time it is run or scheduled: `ensure_script_workflow` (src-tauri/src/commands/scripts.rs:272-286) mints a one-node `script_run` workflow, `created_by='script'`, flipped straight to active, and it is called from the run (:689) and schedule (:713) paths; `list_scripts` then attaches whichever one exists (:364). `visibleWorkflows` hides those rows from the library and its count (workflows/selectors.ts:11-12), and `ScriptRow` imports `SchedulePopover` and `RunHistory` straight out of `../workflows` (ScriptRow.tsx:6-7). A workflow can also run a script as a `script_run` node (NodeParamSheet.tsx:418-469). The split costs a `WorkArea` key, a rail row (shell/navPrefs.tsx:49), a sidebar lens (Sidebar.tsx:1020-1064), a sidebar title (destinations.ts:36), a `showScripts` flag with its own open/close actions, and a second page-header idiom.
  _Do:_ Fold Scripts into the Workflows destination as a second section of `WorkflowLibrary`: keep the compose bar and "Your workflows" grid, and add a "Scripts in this room" section below rendering the existing `ScriptRow` list unchanged (it already carries its own trust copy, Run, Schedule and Runs). Delete `scripts` from `WORK_AREAS` (types.ts:85), `NAV_AREAS` (shell/navPrefs.tsx:49), `SIDEBAR_TITLES` (destinations.ts:36), `ScriptsNav` (Sidebar.tsx:1020-1064) and its dispatch (:325), `ScriptsPage.tsx`, and `openScripts`/`closeScripts` (scriptActions.ts:18-28); rename the destination "Workflows & scripts". Keep `s.scripts` and every script command exactly as they are.

- **[low impact · small]** SIMPLIFY: retire the ⌘3 alias and the keydown effect that exists only for it
  `src/shell/useLayout.ts:37`
  _Today:_ `PANE_KEYS` is a one-entry map holding ⌘3 as an alias for ⌘2 (useLayout.ts:37-39), kept explicitly "for at least one release so nobody's hands have to relearn anything" (useLayout.ts:32-36). Two releases have shipped since. It costs a capture-phase `window` keydown effect (useLayout.ts:636-660, which also carries the focus-Escape), a shortcuts row that apologises for itself — "Show or hide the Assistant (the older shortcut, still works)" (Overlays.tsx:334) — and a test asserting the map's contents (navRedesign.test.mjs:179-193). Being a React listener, it is also one of the keys the private browser's native page swallows, so it is an alias that works in ten destinations of eleven.
  _Do:_ Delete `PANE_KEYS` (useLayout.ts:22-39) and the first branch of the keydown effect (useLayout.ts:638-648), keeping the Escape branch and its capture registration. Delete the ⌘3 row from `SHORTCUTS` (Overlays.tsx:330-334, comment included). Rewrite navRedesign.test.mjs:179-193 as "useLayout claims no ⌘-key at all" — a stronger invariant than the one it asserts today, and one that stays true.

- **[low impact · small]** The tab model still carries a kind nothing can create
  `src/workspace/tabs.ts:17`
  _Today:_ `TabKind = "file" | "area"` (tabs.ts:17), with `area` documented as legacy-only: "Nothing mints one" (tabs.ts:14-16). It survives in `isDurable` (tabs.ts:39), in the restore's selection logic (tabs.ts:85-89, 130), in the persistence filter (tabs.ts:295), in a one-time prune effect (Workspace.tsx:623-628) and in `tabTitleFacts`' `tab.kind !== "file"` guard (Workspace.tsx:722). Related and wrong in the same file: `activateIndex`'s doc says "⌘1–⌘8 pick by position; ⌘9 is last" (tabs.ts:63), but ⌘1 and ⌘2 belong to the native View menu (menu.rs:205-213) and the real binding is ⌥⌘1–⌥⌘9 (Workspace.tsx:655-663).
  _Do:_ Keep the prune (Workspace.tsx:623-628) for one more release since it is what cleans old rooms' settings, but narrow `TabKind` to `"file"` (tabs.ts:17) and have the prune drop anything whose stored kind is not "file" at parse time (tabs.ts:295) instead of modelling it. Then `isDurable` (tabs.ts:39) is `tab.kind === "file"`, the restore branches at tabs.ts:85-89 and 130 simplify, and `tabTitleFacts`' guard (Workspace.tsx:722) goes. Fix the comment at tabs.ts:63 to name ⌥⌘1–⌥⌘9.

- **[low impact · small]** Delete the permanent 'start here' marginalia
  `src/workspace/FrontPage.tsx:512`
  _Today:_ An aside with the handwritten note 'start here' and a curved arrow (FrontPage.tsx:512-515) is rendered unconditionally inside the 'Work in this room' section, aria-hidden, pointing at the four area cards (518-548: Recordings, Workflows, Studio, Browser). It never turns off — a room used daily for six months still says 'start here' — and the page's own H1 two sections above reads 'Continue where you left off' (448).
  _Do:_ Delete the aside (512-515) and its .rh-margin / .rh-margin-note / .rh-margin-arrow rules. If a pointer is wanted, it belongs on the empty state gated on the room actually being new — which the item below folds into Home properly.

- **[low impact · small]** `hostOf` is implemented twice, and the two disagree about what an unparseable URL is
  `src/workspace/BrowserSearch.tsx:755`
  _Today:_ BrowserSearch defines its own `hostOf` (BrowserSearch.tsx:755-761), which on a parse failure returns `url.replace(/^https?:\/\//, "").split("/")[0]`. browserAnnounce.ts:26-33 exports a `hostOf` that returns `null` in the same case, and BrowserReader imports that one (BrowserReader.tsx:9, used at :165). The BrowserSearch copy feeds both the crumb (`<b>{host}</b>`, :637) and the monogram tile's letter (:622-623).
  _Do:_ Delete BrowserSearch.tsx:755-761 and import `hostOf` from `./browserAnnounce`, with `?? hit.url` at the two call sites (:581 feeding :622-623 and :637) so an unparseable address shows the address rather than a fragment of it. browserannounce.test.mjs already covers the surviving implementation; nothing new to write.

- **[low impact · small]** `StorySeam` is exported and rendered nowhere
  `src/workspace/create/StoryTab.tsx:1596`
  _Today:_ `export function StorySeam()` (StoryTab.tsx:1596-1607) renders a `cr-seam` block stating what leaves the Mac when a picture or clip is made. A grep of `src/` and `e2e/` for the identifier returns only its own definition — no import and no call site anywhere in the tree.
  _Do:_ Delete StoryTab.tsx:1595-1607. Whether the Story tab should carry a standing seam line is a separate decision, and the current text would be the weaker place to say it — it would repeat FilmReview's sentence at a moment when nothing is being sent.

- **[low impact · small]** The token meter draws one ratio three ways
  `src/workspace/TokenBudgetBar.tsx:92`
  _Today:_ Inside one button the same `fillPct` is drawn as a conic `.nb-dial` (line 92), as a segmented `.token-bar-fill` (line 99), and written out as `12,043 / 16,000` (line 114). The dial is annotated in-code as "Decorative in the strict sense — the exact figures are written out beside it — hence aria-hidden."
  _Do:_ Delete the `.nb-dial.token-bar-dial` span and its `--nb-val` inline style (TokenBudgetBar.tsx:91-95) plus the `.token-bar-dial` rules at tokenBudget.css:63 and 342. Leave `.nb-dial` in paper.css:1268 alone — it has other consumers. The bar keeps the segments, the figures and the `Near limit` / `At limit` tape.

- **[low impact · small]** Two top-bar racks for one idea: "run this thing in one click"
  `src/workspace/TopBar.tsx:207`
  _Today:_ The bar renders a "Workflows" pill for pinned, active, general-scope workflows (:110-119, :207-217) and, right beside it, a "Scripts" pill for `room-shortcut: global` scripts (:120-128, :219-235) — two `QuickActionsMenu` instances, two `openMenu` keys, two footers. The file's own header comment says this bar can carry eleven controls at once (:36-41). The two lists are also filled by different means: a workflow is pinned by a button in its detail header (WorkflowDetail.tsx:380-388); a script only by typing `# room-shortcut: global` into the file, which ScriptRow reports (:169-174) but cannot change.
  _Do:_ One rack: a single `QuickActionsMenu` over `[...pinnedActions, ...globalScriptActions]`, `buttonLabel="Run"`, one `openMenu` key, footer `{ label: "Workflows & scripts…", onClick: a.openWorkflows }`. The per-action icons already tell the two apart (WorkflowGlyph vs PlayIcon). Do NOT add a Pin toggle that writes the `room-shortcut` line into the script: approval is bound to the file's exact contents, so editing it would revoke approval and the row would read "Edited — needs approval again" (Sidebar.tsx:1044-1050) — a pin that un-arms the thing it pins.

- **[low impact · small]** Dead code: the guided connector form nothing renders
  `src/settings/useMcpConfig.ts:147`
  _Today:_ `useMcpConfig` exposes `connName`, `connCmd`, `connArgs`, their setters and `addConnector` (:12-14, :147-174, :181-188). Its only consumer is ConnectorsView.tsx:102-118, which destructures fourteen values and none of these; nothing else in the tree imports the hook. The guided form they belonged to no longer exists — the marketplace drawer and the Advanced textarea are the two ways in.
  _Do:_ Delete `connName`/`connCmd`/`connArgs`, their setters, `addConnector`, and the five corresponding keys in the returned object. `installServer` (:59-81) already does the same merge for the path that is actually used.

- **[low impact · small]** The Scripts empty state offers the same New script button twice
  `src/workspace/scripts/ScriptsPage.tsx:52`
  _Today:_ The page header renders a `New script` button on every render (:19-21). The empty state renders a second one — `className="primary"` with an inline `style={{ margin: "0.4rem 0" }}` — in the middle of the manifest explanation (:52-54). Both call `a.createNewScript()`.
  _Do:_ Delete the empty-state button and its inline style (:52-54); the header button is already present and already primary. If the empty state needs something in that spot, it is the trust paragraph the page currently hides from it (the `script-caution-page` gate at :37).

- **[low impact · small]** Drop the two counter buttons from the status bar — the Assistant pill already carries both numbers
  `src/shell/StatusBar.tsx:117`
  _Today:_ The same two counts render in three places from the same two functions. TopBar.tsx:348-374 puts them on the Assistant pill: a hand-circled number when approvals are waiting, a live dot when work is running, and both counts spelled into the aria-label ("Show the assistant (⌘2) — 3 things need your approval"). StatusBar.tsx:117-138 and 139-150 render them again as two buttons that both call `onShowActivity`. AiPane's Activity list renders them a third time as the actual cards.
  _Do:_ Delete StatusBar.tsx:117-150 and the `pendingApprovals`, `runningJobs` and `onShowActivity` props with them (StatusBar.tsx:48-50, 63-65, and the three arguments at Workspace.tsx:880-882). The Assistant pill keeps the badge and its aria-label, which is a better accessible name than a bare status cell. Combined with the layout-cell deletion above, the strip drops from six cells to three. Keep item 2's fix to `runningJobCount` anyway — the pill's `running > 0` test reads the same function.

- **[low impact · small]** --fs-lead and --fs-card are one rung wearing two names
  `src/styles/tokens.css:268`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:268-269 defines --fs-lead: 16px and --fs-card: 16px; tokens.css:457-458 defines both as 15px under compact. They are identical in both densities. The comment above them (tokens.css:262-265) describes 'Four tiers with real distance between them', which is five names for four tiers plus a duplicate. --fs-lead has 29 consumers, --fs-card has 42.
  _Do:_ Keep --fs-lead — its comment carries the load-bearing rule, 'never set instructions small' — and retire --fs-card to an alias: `--fs-card: var(--fs-lead);` moved into the legacy alias block at tokens.css:324+, with the note that new CSS uses --fs-lead. No consumer changes, no visual change, and the scale is four honest tiers again.

- **[low impact · small]** Eight legacy aliases are kept warm for rules that no longer exist
  `src/styles/tokens.css:324`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:324+ defines a legacy alias block whose stated purpose is 'old rules keep working, on the new palette', with the instruction 'New CSS should use canonical names only'. Counting consumers across every .css in src: --surface-1 0, --surface-4 0, --card-bg 0, --chip-bg 0, --border-subtle 0, --text-faint 0, --slate 0 — and --surface-2 has 3, --surface-3 2, --input-bg 1. Only four aliases carry real weight: --text-dim (47), --danger (21), --border (16), --warn (7) / --ok (6).
  _Do:_ Delete the seven zero-consumer aliases (--surface-1, --surface-4, --card-bg, --chip-bg, --border-subtle, --text-faint, --slate). Convert the six remaining uses of --surface-2, --surface-3 and --input-bg to --panel, --panel-2 and --panel-2 in place, then delete those three too. Leave --text-dim, --danger, --border, --warn and --ok, and put a number in the comment — 'about 100 rules still read these; the block goes when that reaches zero' — so the block has a finish line instead of being permanent.

- **[low impact · small]** SIMPLIFICATION: one drift checker, three entry points, one of them dead
  `package.json:18`
  _Today:_ `qa/check-mock-coverage.mjs` is invoked three ways: `npm run test:mock` (package.json:18), a direct step inside preflight's `--checks` branch (preflight.sh:99, with the comment at :94-97 explaining why it is repeated there), and e2e/page-script/mock-coverage.test.mjs, which spawns it as a node:test case. Only the last two have a stated reason. `test:mock` is referenced by nothing else in the tree.
  _Do:_ Delete the `test:mock` line from package.json. e2e/README.md:81 already names `node qa/check-mock-coverage.mjs --list`; move that line up into the "What to run before calling a change done" block at :16-21 so the full gap list is where people look for it, and let the two remaining entry points keep their stated reasons.


---

## By lens


### Information architecture, navigation and overall surface area (17)


- **[high·consistency]** "Sidebar" is the name of two different columns — twice in the same menu
  `src-tauri/src/menu.rs:205`
  _Today:_ View → "Sidebar ⌘1" (menu.rs:205-209) toggles the SECOND column and is retitled per destination by `menu_sync` (menu.rs:509-513, via `sidebarMenuLabel`, destinations.ts:67). Four rows below, View → "Show Sidebar Labels" (menu.rs:253-256) toggles the labels on the FIRST column, the rail. In-app the same word covers both: the rail's doc comment opens "The sidebar: the app's ONE primary navigation" (ActivityRail.tsx:15), its expander says "Collapse the sidebar to icons" (ActivityRail.tsx:113), its footer row says "Customize the sidebar" (ActivityRail.tsx:190,195), and Settings → Interface heads the same destination editor "Sidebar" (InterfaceSection.tsx:47) — while `useLayout.paneName` calls the second column "Sidebar" (useLayout.ts:673-674), the divider is labelled "Resize the sidebar" (Workspace.tsx:830) and the ⌘K row says "Show or hide the sidebar — The left pane (⌘1)" (Overlays.tsx:274). The rail's own `aria-label` is already "Destinations" (ActivityRail.tsx:99), and the Customize sheet's closing note (CustomizeSidebar.tsx:52-56) is the single surface that spells the difference out.
  _Problem:_ One word names two columns, including twice in the same View menu, so a reader pressing one control to affect the other gets a change somewhere they were not looking.
  _Do:_ Give each column one word, using the name the rail's own accessible label already uses. Rail → "destinations": menu.rs:255 "Show Sidebar Labels" → "Show Destination Names"; ActivityRail.tsx:113-114 → "Collapse destinations to icons" / "Expand destinations to show names"; ActivityRail.tsx:190,195 and CustomizeSidebar.tsx:160,163 → "Customize destinations"; InterfaceSection.tsx:47 "Sidebar" → "Destinations". Second column keeps "Sidebar", which is where ⌘1, `sidebarMenuLabel`, `paneName`, the splitter label and the ⌘K row already point it. Leave CustomizeSidebar.tsx:52-56 alone — it is already correct and is the sentence the rename makes redundant.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Home has two names, and the rail loses your place because of it
  `src/workspace/state.ts:465`
  _Today:_ The room's area state starts at `useState<WorkArea>("files")` (state.ts:465). The rail is passed the derived area (Workspace.tsx:205-211: workflows → scripts → map flags, else `s.area`) and marks a row current only when `area === areaKey` (ActivityRail.tsx:235); the catalog's key for Home is `"home"` (navPrefs.tsx:42). So on first launch — and after every `showFileHere` bounce, which sets state to `"files"` (Workspace.tsx:590) while persisting the string `"home"` on the very next line (591) — no rail row is highlighted at all. That is the failure ActivityRail.tsx:82-92 says must never happen. The two names are otherwise identical: same title "Library" (destinations.ts:29-30), same `fileArea` (Sidebar.tsx:102), same `showsDocumentTabs` (destinations.ts:105), same centre pane (ViewerPane.tsx:1261 `frontPageView`). `AREA_CRUMBS.files = "Files"` (ViewerPane.tsx:489) is unreachable: the override at 517-519 always wins for `files` (when derived `area` is "files", all three flags are false by construction) and the only other reader is gated on `FILE_BEARING_AREAS = ["recordings","scripts"]` (types.ts:174, used at ViewerPane.tsx:591).
  _Problem:_ On launch, and after every cross-destination file open, the app's primary navigation column highlights nothing — it stops answering the one question it exists to answer. The cause is that `home` and `files` are two names for one destination and only one of them is a rail key.
  _Do:_ Delete `"files"` from `WorkArea` and `WORK_AREAS` (types.ts:63-97) and make `"home"` the initial state (state.ts:465). Fallout, all mechanical: `NavArea` drops its `Exclude<WorkArea,"files">` (navPrefs.tsx:19) and `isArea`'s `v !== "files"` (navPrefs.tsx:91); `isWorkArea` drops `value !== "files"` (types.ts:113); `fileArea` becomes `area === "home"` (Sidebar.tsx:102); `areaHoldsFile` loses a case (types.ts:143); the three `s.setArea("files")` in `showArea` become `"home"` (Workspace.tsx:240,245,252) as does `showFileHere` (Workspace.tsx:590, whose persisted value already says "home"); `SIDEBAR_TITLES.files`, `newItemOf`'s `case "files"`, `showsDocumentTabs`'s `area === "files" ||` and `sidebarRegionLabel`'s `area === "files" ||` all go (destinations.ts:29,88,105,56); `AREA_CRUMBS.files` and the `areaCrumb` override (ViewerPane.tsx:489,517-519) go with them. Net: one fewer member every `Record<WorkArea, …>` has to cover, and the rail highlights Home on launch.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Six navigation entry points bypass the function that owns navigation
  `src/workspace/Overlays.tsx:262`
  _Today:_ `openArea` (Workspace.tsx:446-477) is the stated contract for going somewhere: `guardLeave` (the unsaved-Monaco dialog, Workspace.tsx:264-274), a write of `AREA_SETTING` so the room reopens where you left it, and a restore of that destination's last file from `lastFileRef`. Everything else navigates around it, in two shapes. Via `setArea`: the ⌘K rows `go-home`/`go-recordings`/`go-sketch`/`go-create`/`go-skills`/`go-connectors` (Overlays.tsx:262,263,265,266,270,271, each `leaveAreas(); s.setArea(...)` where `leaveAreas` is `setOpenFile(null)` plus three flags, Overlays.tsx:235-240), Home's `goArea` (FrontPage.tsx:408-414), `revealMemory` (miscActions.ts:338-349), `revealBrowser` (miscActions.ts:380-386), `openSkill` (skillActions.ts:17-21). Via the flags: `openWorkflows` and `openWorkflowDetail` (workflowActions.ts:22-35), `openScripts` (scriptActions.ts:18-24), and the two direct `s.setShowMap(true)` calls (Overlays.tsx:267, FrontPage.tsx:572-575). All sixteen call `setOpenFile(null)`, which unmounts the editor — precisely the exit `guardLeave` exists to intercept — and none writes AREA_SETTING.
  _Problem:_ Navigating by ⌘K, by a Home card, by a skill open or by any Workflows/Scripts/Map entry point silently discards unsaved editor text with no dialog, and does not record where you went, so the room reopens somewhere else next launch. Only the rail takes the safe path.
  _Do:_ Lift `openArea` out of Workspace.tsx into `WSActions` (it needs `s`, `a`, `tabs` and `guardLeave` only) and expose it as `a.goToArea(next)`. Rewrite the sixteen sites to call it: the six palette rows become `run: () => a.goToArea("sketch")` etc.; `goArea` (FrontPage.tsx:408) becomes a one-line delegate; `revealMemory`/`revealBrowser`/`openSkill` keep only their extra work (the memory-intro setting, the selected skill id) around a `goToArea` call; `openWorkflows`/`openScripts`/`openWorkflowDetail` wrap their refresh in one too, since `showArea` already routes those three through their real actions (Workspace.tsx:236-248). Then rename `s.setArea` to `s.setAreaRaw` in state.ts:465-466 so any future direct call reads as the exception it is.

- **[high·ux-defect]** In the private browser, nine of the app's thirteen shortcuts are dead
  `src/workspace/effects.ts:886`
  _Today:_ ⌘N, ⌘L, ⌘F/⌘K, ⌘,, ⌘J, ⌘/ and Escape live on a bubble-phase `window` keydown listener (effects.ts:886-916). ⌘3 and the focus-Escape live on a capture-phase one (useLayout.ts:636-660). ⌥⌘1–9, ⇧⌘]/⇧⌘[ and ⇧⌘T live on a third (Workspace.tsx:650-700). The codebase states twice, in writing, that the browser's page is a native child webview whose key focus this webview never hears from (menu.rs:139-148, useNativeMenu.ts:130-143) — which is exactly why ⌘T, ⌘W, ⌘1 and ⌘2 were moved to native menu rows (menu.rs:158-172, 205-213). The other keys were not moved, so in the Browser destination the shortcuts sheet (Overlays.tsx:303-352) advertises four groups of keys of which only ⌘1, ⌘2, ⌘W and ⌘T actually reach anything.
  _Problem:_ While a private page has focus, the app's single search-and-command entry (⌘K) and eight other advertised shortcuts do nothing, and the sheet that teaches them says nothing about it. A shortcut list that is wrong in one destination out of eleven is the app claiming an ability it does not have there.
  _Do:_ Move the accelerators the browser eats onto native rows in `APP` (menu.rs:156-172): File → "Search this room…" (`CmdOrCtrl+K`), File → "New Chat" (`CmdOrCtrl+N`), a Room section with "Lock Room" (`CmdOrCtrl+L`) and "Settings…" (`CmdOrCtrl+,`), Help → "Keyboard Shortcuts" (`CmdOrCtrl+/`); extend `RoomMenuActions` and `ROOM_ACTIONS` (useNativeMenu.ts:145-158) with the five handlers, add their section ids to the `gated_rows()` filter (menu.rs:294-300), and delete those five branches from effects.ts:887-916 so no key has two owners. In the sheet, move the Tabs group under a "Tabs (Home only)" heading — ⌥⌘1–9 / ⇧⌘[ ] / ⇧⌘T only apply where the strip is drawn (destinations.ts:105) — and add the missing ⌘T row (see my added item on it). If the menu work is too large for this pass, the honest minimum is one line in the sheet naming which keys the browser's page swallows.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Home lists the destinations a third time, and the list is wrong
  `src/workspace/FrontPage.tsx:517`
  _Today:_ Three rosters name the destinations. `NAV_AREAS` has 11 (navPrefs.tsx:41-52). The ⌘K palette has all 11 (Overlays.tsx:262-272), enforced by a test (e2e/page-script/navRedesign.test.mjs:277-292). Home's "Work in this room / Go to an area" (FrontPage.tsx:502-604) has 8 destinations: four ActionCards (Recordings, Workflows, Studio, Browser — FrontPage.tsx:517-548) and five AreaChips (Scripts, Room Map, Memory, Skills, Connectors — FrontPage.tsx:555-603). Sketch and Create are both missing, and Sketch is one of the four default pins (navPrefs.tsx:77). The "Studio" card is not a destination at all: it runs `s.setAiTab("studio"); layout.showPane("ai")` (FrontPage.tsx:531-537). The section's own comment (FrontPage.tsx:505-506) records that it was corrected once for exactly this — "it said 'All capabilities' while omitting three of them" — and it is two short again. Room Map is gated on two different thresholds: `files.length === 0` here (FrontPage.tsx:566-570) versus `files.length < 2` in the palette (Overlays.tsx:267).
  _Problem:_ Someone learning the app from Home concludes it cannot draw and cannot generate pictures, and is offered a ninth card that opens a pane tab rather than a place. A hand-written third roster will keep falling behind the catalog, as it already has twice.
  _Do:_ Render the chips below the dashed rule from the catalog instead of by hand: `NAV_AREAS.filter(x => x.key !== "home" && !FEATURED.has(x.key)).map(x => <AreaChip key={x.key} label={x.label} hint={x.blurb} icon={x.icon(14)} onClick={() => a.goToArea(x.key)} />)`, keeping the four hand-written `ActionCard`s above it as the featured set. Move the Studio card (FrontPage.tsx:530-538) out of "Go to an area" into the assistant-facing part of the page, since it opens a pane tab. Make the map's gate `s.files.length < 2` at FrontPage.tsx:566-570 to match Overlays.tsx:267. (Depends on `a.goToArea` from the openArea item; until that lands, `goArea` at FrontPage.tsx:408 takes the same key set.)

- **[medium·ux-defect]** File → New stays enabled in the six destinations where it does nothing
  `src-tauri/src/menu.rs:158`
  _Today:_ `newItemOf` gives ⌘T a verb only in browser, sketch, create and home (destinations.ts:79-93), and `newItemHere` returns silently everywhere else — and also in the browser when the room is offline (Workspace.tsx:370-377). But the native row is a `Row::Command` with the fixed label "New" (menu.rs:158-163), and `menu_sync` sets `set_enabled(view.enabled)` on every Command row it walks (menu.rs:518-521; `gated_rows()` includes the File section, menu.rs:294-300), where `enabled` only means a room is mounted (useNativeMenu.ts:82-95). So in Workflows, Scripts, Skills, Memory, Connectors and Room Map the File menu shows an enabled "New ⌘T" that produces nothing. `newItemLabel(area)` (destinations.ts:110-123) already computes both the right words and the null, and its only consumer is the sidebar header button (Sidebar.tsx:105,176-187) — nothing feeds it to the menu.
  _Problem:_ A live menu row that makes no change is the app claiming an ability it does not have, in six destinations out of eleven — and the row's fixed label also never tells you what ⌘T would make where it does work.
  _Do:_ Add `new_item: String` to `ViewMenuState` (menu.rs:415-435; empty = none), and extend the `menu_sync` loop's `MenuItemKind::MenuItem` arm (menu.rs:518-521) with the same shape the `view.library` retitle already uses (menu.rs:509-513): for `file.new-item`, `set_text(label)` and `set_enabled(!label.is_empty() && view.enabled)`. Send `newItemLabel(area) ?? ""` from `useNativeMenu` (useNativeMenu.ts:82-95, which already threads `sidebarTitle` this way; add it to the dependency array at 96-104 and to the unmount payload at 110-124). Send `""` for the browser while `!s.webOn`, so the offline case greys out too. The File menu then reads "New sketch ⌘T" in Sketch, "New page ⌘T" in the browser, and greys out in Memory.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Double-clicking a 5px divider resets the entire window layout
  `src/shell/Splitter.tsx:34`
  _Today:_ `onDoubleClick={() => layout.resetLayout()}` (Splitter.tsx:34), `Enter` does the same (Splitter.tsx:40-42), and the divider's `title` advertises it (Splitter.tsx:31). `resetLayout` is not "reset this divider": it restores all three ratios, un-hides both side panes, leaves focus mode, forces `railExpanded` back to true and re-arms `aiChoiceRef`/`focusedRef` so the automatic AI-column step-aside fires again (useLayout.ts:515-529). The same whole-window reset is already offered by name in four other places: LayoutMenu.tsx:125-136, the native View → Layout → Reset Layout (menu.rs:243-247 → useNativeMenu.ts:167), ⌘K's "Reset the layout" (Overlays.tsx:280) and the ⋯ room menu's "Reset the three-pane layout" (TopBar.tsx:412-420).
  _Problem:_ One mis-timed second click while dragging a pane divider throws out every layout choice in the room — both pane widths, both collapse states, focus mode and the rail's label preference — with no undo, from a control that looks local.
  _Do:_ Make the divider's gesture local. Add `evenOut(side: "a" | "b")` to useLayout's returned API (useLayout.ts:687-719), implemented as `applyResize(side, side === "a" ? DEFAULT_RATIOS.library : DEFAULT_RATIOS.ai)` — both are already in scope there and neither is exported today. Point Splitter.tsx:34 and :40-42 at `layout.evenOut(side)` and retitle Splitter.tsx:31 to "Drag to resize. Double-click to even out these two panes." Leave the whole-window reset to the menus that name it (and see my added item about there being four of those).

- **[medium·consistency]** Two file counts on screen at once, a few hundred pixels apart, disagreeing
  `src/Workspace.tsx:870`
  _Today:_ The status bar is handed `fileCount={s.files.length}` (Workspace.tsx:870) and prints "N files" under the title "Files stored in this room" (src/shell/StatusBar.tsx:90-92). The sidebar's header badge for the same destination is `libraryFiles(s.files).length` (Sidebar.tsx:127-128, drawn at 155-157) — the subset Home lists, excluding every `sectionOnly` object (fileVisibility.ts:19-33). Both numbers are true of different populations; neither says which.
  _Problem:_ In any room holding a section-only sketch, creation or recording, two readouts a few hundred pixels apart both present themselves as this room's file count and disagree.
  _Do:_ Delete the status bar's file count: remove the `<span className="status-item" title="Files stored in this room">` block (StatusBar.tsx:90-92), the `fileCount` prop (StatusBar.tsx:43,55) and the argument at Workspace.tsx:870. It is the quietest surface in the app, the sidebar badge already sits directly above the list it counts, and the room total is not a fact anyone acts on. If it is kept instead, add `title={`${headerCount} files listed in the Library`}` to the badge at Sidebar.tsx:155-157 so each readout names its own population.

- **[medium·copy]** "New page" is two different things on the same shortcut
  `src/workspace/destinations.ts:118`
  _Today:_ `newItemLabel` returns "New page" for `browser`, whose ⌘T calls `pages.open()` (Workspace.tsx:346-349), and "New page" for `home`, whose ⌘T calls `a.createNewNote()` (Workspace.tsx:364) — destinations.ts:112-119. Both are bound to ⌘T; the label is what the sidebar header button and its tooltip show (Sidebar.tsx:105,176-187). The rest of the app repeats the split: ⌘K says "New page — A blank Markdown note, ready to edit" (Overlays.tsx:244), the Add menu says the same (Sidebar.tsx:386-401), and the browser's empty state says "New page — Search the web, or open an address" (Sidebar.tsx:1138-1146).
  _Problem:_ ⌘T in Home makes a blank Markdown note; ⌘T in the browser opens a web page. Both are called "New page", so a shortcut learned in one destination produces a different kind of object in another.
  _Do:_ Keep "New page" for the browser — its destination is already titled "Private pages" (destinations.ts:42) — and rename Home's to "New note": destinations.ts:118-119 (`case "note": return "New note"`), the palette row's label at Overlays.tsx:244 (keep its hint), and the Add menu item at Sidebar.tsx:397. Those three plus Workspace.tsx:364 are every `createNewNote` site, so nothing else needs to move.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** ⌘K is the complete route to destinations only — not to what lives inside them
  `src/workspace/Overlays.tsx:241`
  _Today:_ `buildPaletteActions` covers every destination and a test enforces it (navRedesign.test.mjs:277-292), on the stated grounds that the palette is "the ONLY route the embodiment loop can take" for anything behind the More tools disclosure (Overlays.tsx:249-261). Three surfaces that can only be reached inside a collapsible pane have no row: Trash, the third tab of Home's library pane (Sidebar.tsx:216-231); the Studio tab (AiPane.tsx:130-139); and the Activity tab (AiPane.tsx:140-149), whose only other routes are the two status-bar counters (`showActivity`, Workspace.tsx:736-739, passed at 882) and Home's timeline. New folder (Sidebar.tsx:402-412) has no row either, though its object is a Home concept.
  _Problem:_ When the library or assistant pane is collapsed there is no keyboard route to Trash, Studio or Activity at all, and none the embodiment loop can take — which is the exact cost the palette was built to pay for everywhere else.
  _Do:_ Add three rows to `buildPaletteActions` (Overlays.tsx, beside the make-something group at 243-248): `open-trash` → `a.goToArea("home"); s.setLibraryTab("trash"); layout.showPane("library")`; `open-studio` and `open-activity` → `s.setAiTab("studio"|"activity"); layout.showPane("ai")`, the pattern `focusComposer` already uses (miscActions.ts:359-361) and `showActivity` repeats (Workspace.tsx:736-739). Both belong to the LAYOUT_ROWS drop-list check at Overlays.tsx:288-290 since they touch `layout`. Skip New sketch and New folder: Sketch's ⌘T, header button and landing button already cover the first (destinations.ts:83, Sidebar.tsx:176-187, ViewerPane.tsx:279), and a folder only exists in the list that already offers it. Extend navRedesign.test.mjs:277 to assert the palette covers a named list of pane-locked surfaces, not only `NAV_AREAS`.

- **[medium·test-gap]** The roster test guards one of the three destination lists
  `e2e/page-script/navRedesign.test.mjs:277`
  _Today:_ The one completeness test greps the palette source for `id: "go-<area>"` once per `NAV_AREAS` key (navRedesign.test.mjs:277-292). There is no equivalent for Home's "Go to an area" cards and chips (FrontPage.tsx:517-603), which are missing Sketch and Create and carry a non-destination card. And there is no test asserting that navigation goes through `openArea`, which is how sixteen sites came to call `s.setArea` or raise a view flag directly and skip both the unsaved-edits guard and the remembered-place write.
  _Problem:_ The only guard on navigation completeness watches the surface that is already correct, while the two that have actually drifted — Home's roster and the guard-and-remember contract — are unwatched.
  _Do:_ Add two source-level tests in the same grep style. (a) "Home offers every destination": slice FrontPage.tsx between `rh-cards` and the end of `rh-more` and assert every `NAV_AREAS` key except `home` appears by label. (b) "only the shell navigates": read Overlays.tsx, FrontPage.tsx, miscActions.ts, skillActions.ts, workflowActions.ts and scriptActions.ts and assert none contains `s.setArea(`, `setShowMap(true)`, `setShowWorkflows(true)` or `setShowScripts(true)` — after the `goToArea` fix the only remaining writers are `showArea` in Workspace.tsx and state.ts itself.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** ⌘T is the destination's primary verb and appears in no shortcut list
  `src/workspace/Overlays.tsx:303`
  _Today:_ `SHORTCUTS` (Overlays.tsx:303-352) has four groups and no ⌘T row: "Around the room" lists ⌘K/⌘F, ⌘N, ⌘,, ⌘J, ⌘/, ⌘L, Esc; "Tabs" lists ⌘W, ⇧⌘T, ⌘⇧]/⌘⇧[, ⌥⌘1–⌥⌘9. The key itself is declared in the native File menu (menu.rs:158-163) and is the per-destination new-item verb (destinations.ts:79-93). The only place it is printed in-app is the sidebar header button's tooltip, which is rendered only when `newItem && newItem !== "note"` (Sidebar.tsx:176-187) — so in Home, where ⌘T makes a note, no surface in the app mentions ⌘T at all. The palette advertises this sheet as "Every shortcut in one sheet (⌘/)" (Overlays.tsx:285).
  _Problem:_ The one shortcut whose meaning changes per destination is missing from the sheet that claims to be every shortcut, and is unprintable in the destination people start in.
  _Do:_ Add a row to the "Around the room" group in `SHORTCUTS` (Overlays.tsx:305-315): `["⌘T", "Make this destination's new item — note, page, sketch or creation"]`. Better still, render it from the source of truth: the sheet already receives no props, so pass `area` into `ShortcutsSheet` and print `newItemLabel(area) ?? "Nothing to make here"` (destinations.ts:110-123), which makes the row true per destination and gives `newItemLabel` a second consumer.

- **[medium·copy]** ⌘W is documented as "close the current tab" and can close the window
  `src/workspace/Overlays.tsx:319`
  _Today:_ The shortcuts sheet's Tabs group says `["⌘W", "Close the current tab"]` (Overlays.tsx:319). `closeItemHere` (Workspace.tsx:387-401) actually closes, in order: the active private-browser page; else the active tab; else the open file (through `guardLeave`); else `getCurrentWindow().close()`. The native row is labelled "Close" (menu.rs:164-168) and the reasoning — that closing the window is meant to be the last case, not the only one — is written at menu.rs:150-155.
  _Problem:_ In every destination with no tab and no open file — Sketch with nothing open, Memory, Skills, Connectors, a browser with no page — the key the sheet describes as closing a tab closes the app window instead. That is the app describing a destructive action as a harmless one.
  _Do:_ Change Overlays.tsx:319 to `["⌘W", "Close the page, document or tab you are in — the window when there is none"]`, matching the order in `closeItemHere` (Workspace.tsx:387-401) and the native row's own reasoning at menu.rs:150-155.

- **[medium·consistency]** A tab is labelled by a model, while every other surface shows the file's real name
  `src/shell/TabStrip.tsx:271`
  _Today:_ Each tab's visible label is `<TabTitleText>` (TabStrip.tsx:271-276), which asks a model for a "2 or 3 words" title from the file's name and kind (TabStrip.tsx:49-58, facts supplied by Workspace.tsx:720-728) and renders `generated ?? fallback` (TabStrip.tsx:62). The real filename survives only in the native `title=` tooltip (TabStrip.tsx:211) and in the close button's label (TabStrip.tsx:282-283). Meanwhile the sidebar row (Sidebar.tsx FileRow) and the breadcrumb (ViewerPane.tsx:593-595) show the saved name. The generated titles are cached in memory only and do not survive a restart (adaptiveText.ts:26-28), and the master flag is read once per session so toggling it in Settings → Behavior does not affect what is already mounted (adaptiveText.ts:29-36).
  _Problem:_ The same open document carries two different names on screen at once — a model's paraphrase in the strip, the user's own name in the sidebar and breadcrumb — and the strip's name can change between launches because its cache is per-session. Matching a tab to the row that opened it becomes a guess.
  _Do:_ Show the saved name in the strip: replace `<TabTitleText …/>` at TabStrip.tsx:271-276 with the same `fileLabel(...)` the breadcrumb uses (ViewerPane.tsx:594), and delete `TabTitleText` (TabStrip.tsx:29-63), the `titleFacts` prop (TabStrip.tsx:96-98) and `tabTitleFacts` (Workspace.tsx:720-728). That removes a model call per tab, a cache, and the only place in the app where a document is shown under a name the user did not write. If the generated phrasing is wanted, keep it in the `title=` tooltip at TabStrip.tsx:211, where the filename is today — never as the primary label.

- **[low·consistency]** Two groups of destinations, four names for them
  `src/shell/ActivityRail.tsx:129`
  _Today:_ The rail heads its two tiers "Core" (ActivityRail.tsx:129-131) and "On demand" (ActivityRail.tsx:143-147), both `aria-hidden` decorative divs; the disclosure button between them says "More tools" (ActivityRail.tsx:162, aria-label "Show N more tools" at :152-156); `CustomizeSidebarBody` calls the same two groups "Pinned" (CustomizeSidebar.tsx:38) and "Under More tools" (CustomizeSidebar.tsx:45); the store underneath calls them `pinned` and `more` (navPrefs.tsx:199-206).
  _Problem:_ Four labels describe two groups, and the surface that edits the split uses different words from the surface it edits, so the reader has to infer the mapping.
  _Do:_ Two words, one pair. Change ActivityRail.tsx:130 "Core" → "Pinned", matching CustomizeSidebar.tsx:38. Delete the "On demand" heading and its divider entirely (ActivityRail.tsx:143-147) — it is `aria-hidden` decoration and the disclosure button immediately below already carries the group's name in words. Change CustomizeSidebar.tsx:45 "Under More tools" → "More tools".

- **[low·ux-defect]** One filter box is shared by three destinations and never cleared when you leave
  `src/workspace/state.ts:357`
  _Today:_ `fileFilter` is a single room-level string (state.ts:357). The one search input writes it (Sidebar.tsx:265-273) and is rendered for Home's library, Recordings and Sketches (Sidebar.tsx:249-252), each with a different placeholder — "Filter files and pages" / "Filter recordings" / "Filter sketches". Three different lists read it: Home's `shownFiles` (Sidebar.tsx:85,95), `RecordingsNav` (Sidebar.tsx:914-917) and the sketches list (Sidebar.tsx:1289-1301). Nothing clears it on a destination change — `setFileFilter("")` exists only on the input's own clear button (Sidebar.tsx:279). `fileSort` (Sidebar.tsx:95) is shared the same way.
  _Problem:_ Filtering Recordings for "standup" and then clicking Home leaves the library filtered to "standup" — the empty state says "No files match "standup"" (Sidebar.tsx:720) about a list the user never filtered. The query is at least visible in the box, but it silently changes what a different destination shows.
  _Do:_ Clear the query when the destination changes: in the Sidebar effect block near Sidebar.tsx:107-118, add `useEffect(() => { s.setFileFilter(""); }, [area])`. One line, no new state, and it makes each destination's filter mean only that destination. (If per-destination persistence is ever wanted, that is a `Partial<Record<WorkArea,string>>` in state.ts — but the empty default is the honest behaviour and the smaller change.)

- **[low·ux-improvement]** Two destinations pin the rail's disclosure open by proxy, and one row can be current twice
  `src/shell/ActivityRail.tsx:93`
  _Today:_ When the current destination is unpinned and the disclosure is closed, the rail renders exactly that one row under the collapsed "More tools" button: `const current = nav.more.includes(area as NavArea) ? (area as NavArea) : null; const moreRows = layout.moreToolsOpen ? nav.more : current ? [current] : []` (ActivityRail.tsx:93-94), with the reasoning at ActivityRail.tsx:82-92. When the disclosure is then OPENED, that same row is drawn again as part of `nav.more` — so it is never duplicated, but the button's aria-label still says "Show N more tools" / "Hide the other N tools" (ActivityRail.tsx:152-156) while N-1 of them are hidden and one is already on screen. In the closed state the group heading "On demand" (ActivityRail.tsx:143-147) also sits above a single row that is not on demand — it is where you are.
