  _Problem:_ The count in the disclosure's accessible name is wrong in exactly the state the disclosure was designed for: with an unpinned destination current and the drawer closed, it offers to show N tools when one of them is already showing.
  _Do:_ Make the label count what is actually hidden: `const hiddenCount = layout.moreToolsOpen ? nav.more.length : nav.more.length - moreRows.length;` and use `hiddenCount` in the aria-label at ActivityRail.tsx:152-156. Combined with deleting the "On demand" heading (see the two-groups-four-names item), the closed state then reads as one current row above a button that offers the rest, with a truthful number.


### First run, unlock, recovery, front page and the empty room (22)


- **[high·ux-defect]** A failed auto-lock is completely silent
  `src/workspace/effects.ts:836`
  _Today:_ The idle timer calls onLock() bare (effects.ts:836), with no catch. handleLock (App.tsx:499) re-throws when api.closeRoom() fails (App.tsx:524-529) after already calling setLocking(false), so the seal overlay is torn down, the workspace stays on screen, and the rejection lands as an unhandled promise with nothing on screen. The manual lock path does it correctly: chatActions.ts:763-767 wraps await onLock() and pushes 'Couldn't lock the room — it's still open. Try again.'
  _Problem:_ The idle auto-lock can fail and leave the room open with no message anywhere. The user walked away believing it sealed itself; it did not, and the app said nothing. This is the one failure a privacy app must never swallow.
  _Do:_ Wrap the auto-lock call the way the manual one already is: at effects.ts:836 replace onLock() with void Promise.resolve(onLock()).catch(() => s.pushToast('error', 'Auto-lock failed — this room is still open.')). Nothing else changes; s.pushToast is already used a dozen times in this file and the copy exists verbatim one file over.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The printed recovery sheet does not say which room it opens
  `src/screens/RecoveryModal.tsx:58`
  _Today:_ The printed page is the .recovery-sheet subtree (print rules at src/styles/misc-moonshot-cards.css:526-587 hide the whole body and re-reveal only that, and drop .recovery-sheet-actions). Its content is the title 'Your recovery code' (59-63), the code (64) and the note (65-69). Nothing names the room, the file name or the path. The Settings sheet (RecoverySection.tsx:36-42) is identical.
  _Problem:_ Someone with two rooms ends up with two visually identical printed sheets and no way to tell which code opens which. The sheet is designed to be filed away for years, which is precisely when the association is lost.
  _Do:_ Pass the room path into RecoveryModal (App.tsx:631-638 already renders it beside pendingInfo, which holds the RoomInfo) and render one line under the title: fileNameOf(path) in the mono .gate-file face, exactly as UnlockScreen.tsx:57 does. Add the same line to RecoverySection's sheet (RecoverySection.tsx:37). No new data and no new component.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Settings retires a working recovery code without saying it did
  `src/settings/RecoverySection.tsx:70`
  _Today:_ RecoverySection renders 'Create a recovery key' (button at 70-76, in the else branch at 68-78) whenever local state holds no code — it never asks whether the room already has one, and every room made through the create flow does (App.tsx:412-425). useRecovery.ts:12-24 calls writeRecoveryKey() unconditionally, and db::write_recovery (versions.rs:277) renames a fresh sidecar over the old one (versions.rs:302), so pressing it silently invalidates the code the user printed at create time. The section's hint (26-30) still reads 'it's the only way back in', with no mention that pressing the button ends the old one.
  _Problem:_ A user who wants to check the feature, or who forgot they already have a key, destroys their printed copy with one click and is told nothing. The paper in the drawer is now worthless and looks identical to a working one.
  _Do:_ Call hasRecoveryKey(roomPath) in useRecovery (usePrivacy.ts:79 already makes exactly that call) and thread the boolean into RecoverySection. When true: label the button 'Replace the recovery key', and above it render the same element PrivacySection.tsx:155-161 uses — <p className="set-note set-note--flag nb-sem-pending"> — carrying the sentence PrivacySection.tsx:177-181 already writes after a password change: 'This room already has a recovery key. Making a new one stops the old code working.'

- **[high·ux-defect]** A recovery unlock blames the code for every failure, including a damaged room or a missing drive
  `src/App.tsx:472`
  _Today:_ unlockMessage's own doc comment (App.tsx:41-43) states 'Every unlock path funnels through here — typed password, Touch ID, and the recovery code — because they used to disagree.' It is called at App.tsx:454 (typed password) and App.tsx:493 (Touch ID) and nowhere else. handleRecoveryUnlock catches with a bare `catch {` and a fixed string: setError("That recovery code didn't work. Check it and try again.") (472-473). open_room_with_recovery (rooms.rs:229-238) recovers the password and then runs the FULL open_room, so a read-only disk, a damaged file, a room already open in another copy, a missing sidecar or a disconnected drive all arrive in that same catch — and the catch does not even console.error, unlike the other two paths.
  _Problem:_ The one user who has already lost their password is told their last remaining credential is wrong, when the real cause may be an unplugged drive or a damaged file — the two things unlockMessage exists to name. They will retype a correct code until they give up, and there is not even a console record of what actually failed.
  _Do:_ Make handleRecoveryUnlock use the funnel its doc claims it uses: change the catch at 472-473 to `catch (e) { const msg = String(e); console.error('recovery unlock failed:', msg); setError(/RECOVERY|code/i.test(msg) ? "That recovery code didn't work. Check it and try again." : unlockMessage(msg)); }` — the wrong-code sentence survives for a genuine code failure, and every host sentence ('File not found.', the read-only and damaged-file cases) reaches the screen as it already does on the other two paths.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The recovery sheet says 'Copied' whether or not the clipboard write succeeded — and that unverified claim disarms the skip guard
  `src/screens/RecoveryModal.tsx:74`
  _Today:_ The Copy button (RecoveryModal.tsx:74-82) calls setRecoveryCopied(true) FIRST, then `void navigator.clipboard.writeText(recoveryCode)` inside a try/catch. The catch only sees a synchronous throw; a rejected promise from writeText (denied permission, no clipboard in the webview) is swallowed by `void` and never observed. The label then reads 'Copied' (84) and, because recoveryCopied is true, skip() (24-28) returns immediately with no confirm — the same flag the guard is built on. RecoverySection.tsx:46-53 and PrivacySection.tsx:185-190 have the identical shape.
  _Problem:_ The app states a fact it did not verify, about the one string that is shown once and can never be recovered, and the false claim then switches off the warning that exists to catch exactly this case. The user leaves believing the code is on their clipboard; it may not be, and no dialog will ever ask again.
  _Do:_ Await the write before claiming it: replace the body at 74-82 with `navigator.clipboard.writeText(recoveryCode).then(() => setRecoveryCopied(true)).catch(() => setCopyFailed(true))`, and render the failure in place of the 'Copied' label as 'Couldn't copy — write the code down from the screen.' Apply the same shape to RecoverySection.tsx:46-53. setRecoveryCopied must never be set on a path that has not confirmed the copy, because it is also the guard's input.

- **[high·ux-defect]** When the recovery code cannot be written, the room opens anyway and nobody is told it has no recovery key
  `src/App.tsx:421`
  _Today:_ handleCreate wraps writeRecoveryKey() in try/catch (App.tsx:415-425). On failure it does console.error('Could not create a recovery code', e) and calls enterRoom(info) (421-424) — the room opens normally, the recovery sheet never appears, and nothing on screen or in the room ever mentions it. One screen earlier the create form has just promised, unconditionally: 'There is no password reset — if you forget this password, only the one-time recovery code on the next screen can reopen the room' (CreateScreen.tsx:180-185). The unlock screen for that room will then render no 'Forgot password?' affordance at all (UnlockScreen.tsx:119-128, gated on has_recovery_key), which is indistinguishable from a room that has one.
  _Problem:_ The app makes a promise on the create screen, fails to keep it, and enters the room as though it had. The user believes a printed code exists somewhere; there is none, and the only signal is a console line they will never see. This is the highest-stakes silent failure in the product.
  _Do:_ In the catch at 421-424, before entering, set the gate's own error line — setError('The room was created, but its recovery code could not be written. Make one now in Settings → Privacy & recovery, or this room can only ever be opened with its password.') — and keep the user on the create screen with a single 'Continue' that calls enterRoom(info), reusing the existing `error` rendering (CreateScreen renders it already). No new component: it is the same gate-error element the password validation at App.tsx:344-345 uses.

- **[high·privacy]** Home's generated subtitle sends a room item's title to whatever engine the room is set to — including a cloud one — to rewrite the line promising nothing leaves this Mac
  `src/workspace/FrontPage.tsx:430`
  _Today:_ homeDekFacts includes `mostRecent: { title: recent[0].title, kind: recent[0].kind }` (FrontPage.tsx:430) — the newest timeline entry's real title, i.e. a file or chat name — and the whole facts object is JSON.stringify'd into the prompt (437) and sent through generateUiText → generate_ui_text (ai_actions.rs:441-469). That command resolves the model with resolve_structured_model (moonshot.rs:28-43), which returns the room's configured engine and explicitly returns it when is_external_engine is true — claude-cli, codex-cli or openrouter (external.rs:49-52). The sentence it is being asked to replace is 'Recent work, current background activity, and everything this room can do — nothing here leaves this Mac on its own.' (FrontPage.tsx:452-453).
  _Problem:_ On a room set to a cloud engine, simply landing on Home ships a document title off the machine so that a model can paraphrase two numbers already printed beside it — and the string it overwrites is the page's own claim that nothing leaves on its own. The feature is on by default (adaptiveText.ts:91 masterFlagOn = true) and needs no user action to fire.
  _Do:_ Same edit as the 'delete the AI-written subtitle' item — remove homeDekFacts and the useAdaptiveText call (FrontPage.tsx:424-442) and render the static subtitle unconditionally — but this is the reason to do it rather than defer it. If the dek is kept for any reason, it must at minimum be gated on !isCloudRoute(s.model, s.ai) (the predicate FrontPage.tsx:65 already uses) and must drop `title` from the facts.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The recovery key is a second file on disk, and the app never says so
  `src/screens/RecoveryModal.tsx:65`
  _Today:_ write_recovery_key writes a <path>.recovery sidecar beside the room file — its doc comment at src-tauri/src/commands/rooms.rs:211-215 says so, and db/versions.rs:302 does it. has_recovery (versions.rs:338) is just a path-exists check. The gate promises 'sealed inside one encrypted file that never leaves this computer' (StartScreen.tsx:54-57) and lists 'One file, fully encrypted' (StartScreen.tsx:72). The modal note (65-69) says only 'it never leaves this Mac'. The unlock screen's 'Forgot password? Use a recovery code' renders only when has_recovery_key is true (App.tsx:243, UnlockScreen.tsx:119-128) and renders nothing at all when false.
  _Problem:_ Copy, move or back up the .arcelle alone and the printed code becomes dead — the 'Forgot password?' link silently disappears with no explanation, discovered on the one day it matters. The 'one file' claim is also literally false the moment a recovery key exists, which is every room the create flow makes.
  _Do:_ Two changes. (1) In the RecoveryModal note (65-69) name the file: 'This code unlocks the room through a small companion file, <name>.arcelle.recovery, saved next to it. Keep them together — copying the room on its own leaves the code with nothing to open.' (2) In UnlockScreen, when hasRecovery is false, render one .gate-hint line under the password field: 'No recovery file was found next to this room.' Today the absence of the affordance is indistinguishable from a room that never had one.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The first screen after unlock invites a question the room cannot answer yet
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:1263`
  _Today:_ A brand-new room fails the frontPageView test (ViewerPane.tsx:484-487) so the centre shows the 'Your room is sealed' sheet (1263-1305) with three buttons: Add a file (1275-1277), Summarize room (1278-1290), Ask the room (1291-1296). None of them reads the engine state. Meanwhile the AI pane on the right — visible by default (useLayout.ts:251-267) — shows either 'This room's AI runs on Ollama, a free app' with a Get Ollama button (ChatPane.tsx:376-390) or the 'Pick a model to download' grid (ChatPane.tsx:429-457).
  _Problem:_ The window tells the new user two different stories about what to do first. The centre, the larger and more prominent surface, points at 'Ask the room' — an action that cannot work until a multi-gigabyte download completes, and it does not say so. The one thing genuinely blocking the room is announced only in the narrow right column.
  _Do:_ Make the sealed sheet defer to the engine state it can already read: import isModelReady from workspace/localModel, and when !s.ai?.running || !isModelReady(s.ai, s.model), replace the three-button row (1274-1297) with one line and one button — 'This room's AI isn't set up yet' + 'Set up the AI' calling a.focusComposer(layout) to bring the pane forward — keeping 'Add a file' as the secondary. Once the model is ready the current three return unchanged. Note this overlaps the 'two front pages' item below; if that one lands, apply this gate inside FrontPage's Continue empty state instead.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Printing the recovery code does not count as saving it, contrary to the file's own comment
  `src/screens/RecoveryModal.tsx:86`
  _Today:_ The comment at RecoveryModal.tsx:18-23 states 'Copying (or printing) counts as saving it, so the guard only stands in the way of the user who genuinely has nothing written down.' The Print button (86-88) is onClick={() => window.print()} and never touches setRecoveryCopied. Only Copy (74-82) sets it.
  _Problem:_ A user who prints the sheet — the safest thing they can do with it — is then treated as having nothing written down and made to confirm a warning about losing the room forever. The intended behaviour is written down and not implemented.
  _Do:_ Pick one and make the code and the comment agree. Preferred, given RecoverySection.tsx:32-35 already records that the Tauri webview's window.print() can be a no-op: leave setRecoveryCopied alone, relabel the button 'Print / Save as PDF…', and delete '(or printing)' from the comment at line 21. A print that may never have happened must not silently disarm the guard.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** 'Try a demo room' is the full five-step room-creation ceremony
  `src/App.tsx:308`
  _Today:_ chooseDemo (App.tsx:308-312) navigates to the create screen with templateKey 'demo' and the name pre-filled. The user then chooses a password of at least 8 characters (MIN_PASSWORD, rooms/constants.ts:19), repeats it, reads 'There is no password reset — …only the one-time recovery code on the next screen can reopen the room' (CreateScreen.tsx:180-185), presses Create & Enter, picks a save location in a native panel (App.tsx:359-363), and is handed a one-time recovery code (App.tsx:412-425).
  _Problem:_ 'Try a demo' promises a look around and delivers the heaviest commitment path in the product. It also trains the user to click through the recovery-code sheet — the most consequential dialog in the app — on a throwaway room, so the ceremony is already discounted by the time it matters.
  _Do:_ Rename the StartScreen button (StartScreen.tsx:90-92) from 'Try a demo room' to 'Create a demo room', because that is what it does. Do NOT skip the writeRecoveryKey block for demo rooms: the demo room is password-sealed like any other, so skipping it would leave a room with no way back in and would make the unconditional promise at CreateScreen.tsx:181-184 false. If the ceremony is to be lightened, the change belongs in the create flow for every room, not in a special case that quietly removes the only recovery path.

- **[medium·accessibility]** 'Summarize room' is disabled with its reason sealed inside a tooltip that cannot open
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:1278`
  _Today:_ The Summarize room button (ViewerPane.tsx:1278-1290) uses the real disabled attribute with three OR'd conditions and no title, no aria-describedby, no visible note. On the first-run screen it is always greyed. FrontPage.tsx:340-344 documents the opposite rule for this exact situation, in writing: 'aria-disabled, never the disabled attribute. A disabled button does not fire pointer events, so its title cannot appear.'
  _Problem:_ The very first screen of the app shows a dead control with no explanation, on a screen whose whole job is teaching. The house pattern that fixes it was written for this case and is not applied here.
  _Do:_ Convert it to the AreaChip pattern (FrontPage.tsx:345-389): aria-disabled instead of disabled, onClick omitted while unavailable, and the reason carried as visible text — 'Add a file first — there's nothing to summarize yet' when s.files.length === 0, 'A job is already running' when the jobs condition binds. FrontPage.tsx:563-576 is the working precedent (Room Map on an empty room). Rendering the reason also removes the guesswork about which of the three conditions is in force.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The Touch ID tip is shown to Macs that have no Touch ID
  `src/screens/UnlockScreen.tsx:104`
  _Today:_ UnlockScreen renders 'Tip: enable fingerprint unlock in Settings → Privacy.' whenever !canTouchId (104-108). canTouchId comes from api.touchIdHas (App.tsx:204-219), i.e. biometrics::has (src-tauri/src/biometrics.rs:64) — a Keychain attribute query for an existing entry. It never asks whether the machine has a biometric sensor. The tip also names 'Settings → Privacy' while the settings nav group reads 'Privacy & recovery' (Settings.tsx:65-69).
  _Problem:_ On a Mac with no Touch ID sensor — a Mini, an iMac, any external keyboard without one — this advice appears on every single unlock, forever, and cannot be followed. It is the last thing the user reads before typing their password.
  _Do:_ Delete the tip (UnlockScreen.tsx:104-108). It is unconditional advice on a screen that asks exactly one question, and the Touch ID button already appears by itself the moment an entry exists. If it must survive, it has to be gated on a new biometrics::available() (LAContext canEvaluatePolicy) exposed beside touchid_has rather than on absence-of-entry — and its pointer corrected to 'Settings → Privacy & recovery'.

- **[medium·consistency]** Escape does nothing in the feedback modal
  `src/workspace/FeedbackModal.tsx:129`
  _Today:_ FeedbackModal declares role='dialog' aria-modal='true' (129-132) but installs no key handler and does not use useFocusTrap, so Tab walks out into the workspace behind it and Escape falls through to the app-level listener (effects.ts:846-885, bubble-phase at :918). Because the textarea autofocuses (149) that listener's typing guard (877-879) fires, so Escape does nothing; once the user has tabbed out to a button, the same key closes the file behind the modal instead. Every sibling sheet does the opposite with a capture-phase window listener that stops propagation: CompareModal.tsx:33-45, StudioModal.tsx:14-25, AiActionModal.tsx:15-.
  _Problem:_ The one modal a user opens when something is already going wrong is the one that ignores Escape and leaks focus, and once focus has left the fields the same key closes something behind it. aria-modal claims a containment the component does not implement.
  _Do:_ Do both halves, because neither alone is enough. (1) Add the capture-phase listener the siblings use — copy CompareModal.tsx:33-45 verbatim, calling close() (which already refuses while drafting, FeedbackModal.tsx:62-64) — so Escape closes this dialog and nothing underneath it. (2) Adopt useFocusTrap for Tab containment (modalRef + onModalKeyDown on the panel div at 120-134); note its own Escape path is bubble-phase and does not stopPropagation (useFocusTrap.ts:42-47), which is why (1) is still required.

- **[medium·copy]** The gate promises 'Offline by default' while the app contacts GitHub on every launch
  `src/screens/StartScreen.tsx:64`
  _Today:_ The start screen leads with 'sealed inside one encrypted file that never leaves this computer' (54-57) and lists three assurances, the first being 'Offline by default' (64). checkForUpdatesQuietly (updater.ts:79-93) runs on every launch unless prUpdateCheck is '0' — its own comment (updater.ts:11-13) calls it 'one of the app's two unprompted outbound requests' and notes it fires before any room is unlocked, i.e. while this exact screen is on display.
  _Problem:_ The one screen where the app makes its promise in the user's own words is the screen where the promise is quietly not kept. This is the class of claim the product's honesty stance exists to police, and the codebase already knows it — the comment naming the outbound request is in the updater file.
  _Do:_ Keep the bullet and make it true: change line 64 to 'Offline except a version check' and add the detail as a fourth short <li>: 'Checks GitHub for updates on launch — switchable off'. Do not delete the bullet; 'Offline by default' is the reason people install this app, it just needs to be true as written.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Starting a launch-time update download cannot be stopped
  `src/updater.ts:110`
  _Today:_ Answering 'Install & relaunch' at launch (confirm at updater.ts:109-116) begins downloadAndInstall (134-144), whose only argument is a progress callback — no abort handle. The banner it draws (showDownloadProgress, 165-202) contains a text span and a progress bar and nothing else: no cancel, no close. The model pull's Stop button (ChatPane.tsx:424-426) works only because Rust owns a cancellable job for it (comment at 421-423).
  _Problem:_ A user who says yes at the gate on a tethered connection has no way out but quitting the app, and the banner is drawn outside React (161-164) so it survives navigation.
  _Do:_ Say so in the question, since the answer cannot be taken back: change the confirm body at updater.ts:110 to `Version ${update.version} is available.\n\nInstall it now and relaunch Arcelle? The download can't be stopped once it starts.` Do not add a Stop button to showDownloadProgress unless the plugin call is first replaced by a genuinely abortable one — a button that hides the banner while bytes keep arriving is worse than none.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** A recent room whose file is gone still sends you to a password form for a file that is not there
  `src/screens/StartScreen.tsx:105`
  _Today:_ A missing room's row is marked (StartScreen.tsx:103, 131-135: 'File not found — moved, deleted, or on a drive that isn't connected') but the row is still a live button (105-117) whose onOpenRecent goes straight to the unlock gate (App.tsx:573: goTo({ kind: 'unlock', path })). The user types a password, presses Unlock, and only then does api.openRoom fail with 'File not found.', which unlockMessage passes through (App.tsx:67-70). The row's own comment (108-111) claims 'the open path is what knows how to ask you where it moved to' — but nothing on this route opens a picker; that is chooseOpen (App.tsx:296-303), reachable only by pressing Back and then 'Open Room…'.
  _Problem:_ The screen knows the file is missing and still asks for its password, so the user's first act at the gate is to authenticate against nothing. The remedy the comment describes exists but is two navigations away and unnamed.
  _Do:_ In App.tsx's onOpenRecent handler (573), look the path up in the `recent` state already held there and, when that record's `missing` is true, call chooseOpen() instead of goTo({ kind: 'unlock', path }) — the native panel is already titled 'Open an Arcelle Room' and filtered (296-303). No new screen, no new component, and the row's existing title/marker copy stops being advice with no action behind it.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** No 'Check for Updates…' in the app menu — the only switch for the app's only unprompted outbound call is behind a password
  `src-tauri/src/menu.rs:123`
  _Today:_ The APP menu section (menu.rs:123-138) holds only the predefined About/Services/Hide/HideOthers/ShowAll/Quit rows — no Check for Updates item, contrary to the macOS convention. The launch check fires from main.tsx:35 before any room is unlocked (updater.ts:79-93), and the switch that disables it lives in Settings → Updates & version (AboutSection.tsx:150-167), reachable only from inside an unlocked room. Row (menu.rs:95-108) has no checkable variant.
  _Problem:_ A user standing at the gate who does not want the app contacting GitHub has to first unlock a room to say so. The preference is device-wide (updater.ts:22) and is read before any room exists, so its only control being room-scoped is backwards.
  _Do:_ Add Row::Command { id: "app.check-updates", label: "Check for Updates…", accel: None } after About in menu.rs:123-138, and wire the frontend menu handler to a new exported checkForUpdatesNow() in updater.ts that reuses the existing confirm → download → relaunch body (updater.ts:109-156) while ignoring the auto-check preference. Do NOT promise a checkbox in that flow: the Row enum has no checkable variant and no such dialog exists. Reaching the preference itself from the gate is a separate change and should be scoped as one.

- **[low·copy]** The recovery-unlock screen states one origin for a code that has three
  `src/screens/UnlockScreen.tsx:189`
  _Today:_ Recovery mode closes with 'The recovery code was shown once, when this room was created.' (189-191). Codes are also minted from Settings → Recovery key (RecoverySection.tsx:70-76 via useRecovery.ts:12-24) and re-issued automatically by a password change (PrivacySection.tsx:168-181). Each write renames a fresh sidecar over the old one (db/versions.rs:302), so only the newest code can work.
  _Problem:_ A user who minted a code in Settings last month, or whose code was re-issued when they changed their password, is told at the door that their code came from a moment it did not — which reads as 'this is the wrong code' at exactly the point where they most need to trust it.
  _Do:_ Change the note at 189-191 to: 'The recovery code was shown once, when it was created — at setup, in Settings, or when the password was last changed. Only the newest one works.' The last clause is load-bearing (db/versions.rs:302) and is stated nowhere on this screen today.

- **[low·ux-improvement]** Nothing follows a recovery unlock, though the user by definition has no password
  `src/screens/UnlockScreen.tsx:189`
  _Today:_ handleRecoveryUnlock (App.tsx:462-477) calls openRoomWithRecovery and then enterRoom(info) — the same path as an ordinary unlock, with no acknowledgement that the user arrived by the emergency route and still does not know the password. Settings → Privacy's change-password form (PrivacySection.tsx:134-166) requires the CURRENT password, so it cannot help this user, and the recovery code is not consumed by use (rooms.rs:229-238), so it keeps working.
  _Problem:_ The user is left without being told what state they are in: the password is unchanged, and whether the code they just spent still works is unstated at the one moment they are thinking about it.
  _Do:_ Say it where they are already reading, in the recovery form's note (UnlockScreen.tsx:189-191, merged with the origin fix above): '…Only the newest one works. Unlocking with a code does not change the password — the same code will keep working until you make a new one.' Do not route them to Settings → Privacy: change-password needs the current password (PrivacySection.tsx:134-150), which is precisely what this user does not have. A real 'set a new password with a recovery code' path is a backend change and should be scoped as one.

- **[low·accessibility]** Icon-only quick actions take a sentence-long hint as their accessible name
  `src/workspace/QuickActions.tsx:121`
  _Today:_ Inline pills render aria-label={a.hint ?? a.label} (QuickActions.tsx:121) with the same string also on title (118). The comment above it (119-120) says 'An icon-only button's name came from title alone, which is a hint and not a label. Same words, said properly.' — but the fix chose the hint over the label, so where a hint exists it becomes the name.
  _Problem:_ A screen reader announces the full explanatory sentence as the control's name where a short label was available, which is slower to scan and inconsistent with the overflow items below (166-181) that render a.label as their text.
  _Do:_ Change line 121 to aria-label={a.label} and leave title={a.hint ?? a.label} at 118. If the hint should also be announced, attach it via aria-describedby the way AreaChip does at FrontPage.tsx:361-388 — that pattern is already in the codebase and solves exactly this split.

- **[low·accessibility]** Quick-action pills are disabled with the real attribute, so the sentence explaining them can never be shown
  `src/workspace/QuickActions.tsx:122`
  _Today:_ Inline quick-action pills are icon-only and set disabled={a.disabled} (QuickActions.tsx:122) while carrying their explanation only as title/aria-label (118, 121). A disabled button fires no pointer events, so the tooltip cannot open — the exact failure the codebase documents at FrontPage.tsx:340-344 ('aria-disabled, never the disabled attribute… the Room Map chip on an empty room was greyed out with the explanation for WHY sealed inside a tooltip that could not open') and solves with AreaChip (345-389).
  _Problem:_ An icon-only control that is greyed out with no reachable explanation gives the reader nothing at all — not even the guess a labelled button would allow. The house pattern for this exact case exists two files away and is not applied.
  _Do:_ In QuickActions, mirror AreaChip: replace disabled={a.disabled} (122) with aria-disabled={a.disabled || undefined} and onClick={a.disabled ? undefined : a.onRun}, so the title and description stay reachable on hover and focus. The overflow menu items (172) may keep the real attribute — they carry visible text.


### Recording capture UX and the transcript review experience (24)


- **[high·ux-defect]** The podcast panel's privacy sentence is false the moment you press Preview
  `src/workspace/PodcastPanel.tsx:212`
  _Today:_ The unfoldable boundary note reads "…every line of this script is sent to Microsoft's Edge TTS service to be spoken. Nothing else goes with it, and nothing is sent until you press Record." (PodcastPanel.tsx:212-217). The per-host play button directly above it (247-258) calls `preview()` (128-156), which takes the host's first matching script line — or invents `Hello, I'm ${host.name}.` when no turn matches — caps it at 300 chars and sends it via `api.previewPodcastVoice` → `preview_podcast_voice` (podcast_audio.rs:350) → `speak_one` (speech_cmds.rs:98-123) → the sidecar's `/tts`, which is `edge_tts.Communicate` (sidecar/arcelle_sidecar/tts.py:132-136). The button is labelled (`title` at 249, `aria-label` at 250) but neither string mentions the network. And because `speak_one` runs `speakable_text` → `redact_for_speech` (speech_cmds.rs:35-41, 45-55), the privacy-door paragraph (220-227) is wrong in the same direction: it warns only that "the recording will say Person A", when the preview substitutes too.
  _Problem:_ The panel states a data boundary and then crosses it with the control sitting directly above the sentence — and its privacy-door warning is scoped to Record when the door also applies to Preview.
  _Do:_ Three string edits, no new UI. (1) PodcastPanel.tsx:216-217 → "…and nothing is sent until you press Preview or Record." (2) The preview button's title (249) → `` title={`Send one of ${host.name || "this host"}'s lines to the voice service and hear it back`} ``. (3) The privacy-door paragraph (222-226) → "…names in the script are replaced before they are spoken — a preview and the finished recording will both say "Person A" where the script says a real name." Same doctrine the panel already applies to the door trap.

- **[high·ux-defect]** "Recording saved — transcript included" is asserted, not checked
  `src/workspace/recordingActions.ts:462`
  _Today:_ `stopLiveRecording` awaits `api.recStop()` and throws the return value away, then fires one fixed toast — "Recording saved — transcript included." (recordingActions.ts:459-466) — on any resolution. The discarded value is the final `RecMeta` (api.ts:1263; `rec_stop` returns `Result<RecMeta, String>`, src-tauri/src/commands/recording_cmds.rs:504-540). A session with the `Live transcription` checkbox off (RecordingView.tsx:1869-1881), a silent room, or a meeting where only the failed Mac-audio lane was on all end with `segments == []`, and the user is still told the transcript is included. The engine already branches on exactly this fact: it only starts the reading pass `if !meta.segments.is_empty()`, and `start_rec_read` refuses outright with "That recording has no transcript to read yet." (src-tauri/src/commands/jobs/rec_read.rs:534-536).
  _Problem:_ The most load-bearing receipt in the flow states a fact the code never checked, on exactly the recordings where it is false.
  _Do:_ Take the meta — `const meta = await api.recStop();` — and branch the receipt on `meta.segments.length`: keep "Recording saved — transcript included." when there are segments, and say "Recording saved. Nothing was transcribed — open it and press Re-transcribe to write it up." when there are none (keeping the existing `{ label: "Open", run: … }` action in both cases). Put the choice in recReview.ts as `stopReceipt(meta)` so it is testable like every other rule that module holds, and add a case to e2e/page-script/recReview.test.mjs.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Nothing says which model reads the recording — and it reads it automatically on Stop
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:364`
  _Today:_ The `Read this recording` / `Read again` button carries `title="The room reads the transcript and writes what happened in it"` (RecordingView.tsx:356-366), and the three tabs' blank copy describes findings without saying where they come from (2287, 2302, 2317). The pass runs on whatever engine the room resolves — `let (chat_model, lane) = resolve_pass_engine(state).await; let steps = build_read_steps(windows.len(), lane);` (src-tauri/src/commands/jobs/rec_read.rs:541-542, 338-342) — so in a cloud room the whole meeting transcript goes to the provider. And not only when pressed: `rec_stop` starts the read itself whenever `!meta.segments.is_empty()` (src-tauri/src/commands/recording_cmds.rs, end of `rec_stop`), and the background sweep picks up unread recordings one per tick (src-tauri/src/commands/jobs/auto_index.rs:149-168). The control immediately above it was deliberately fixed to say the opposite kind of thing: "…on the room's AI model … In a cloud room that means each sentence is sent to the provider as it lands" (1591-1601).
  _Problem:_ A whole meeting transcript can leave the machine without anyone pressing anything, and the recording screen never says so — while the control beside it does.
  _Do:_ Mirror the live-translate wording on the read button (362): `title="The room reads the transcript and writes what happened in it — on the room's AI model. In a cloud room the transcript is sent to the provider."` And extend the empty-state paragraph that already handles this correctly for AI actions (2141-2146) with one clause naming the reading pass as one of them and saying it starts by itself when a recording is stopped.

- **[high·ux-improvement]** Renaming a speaker is invisible unless you hover the chip
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:2265`
  _Today:_ `SpeakerChip` is a button whose entire affordance is a `title` — "Name this speaker — renames every line they said" (RecordingView.tsx:221-227) — with `cursor: text` and an underline that appears only on hover/focus (recording.css:911-924). The one on-screen sentence about naming is itself hover-only: the capture-choices row shows "Speakers detected automatically — name them later" with "Afterwards, click a speaker's name to say who they were" hidden in its `title` (1582-1588), and that row renders only when `showsChoicesInline(stage)` and `status === "idle"` (1866-1868) — i.e. never on a recording anyone is reviewing. The footer hint under the transcript names the other two edits and not this one: "Select words above to correct them, or to delete them from the recording." (2265-2270).
  _Problem:_ The most valuable correction in a meeting transcript — putting real names on the voices — is discoverable only by hovering something that looks like a label.
  _Do:_ Extend the one sentence at 2266-2269, which is already positioned, styled and conditioned on `!isLive && segments.length > 0`: "Click a speaker's name to say who they were. Select words to correct them, or to delete them from the recording." Nothing is added to the screen. Do NOT give the chip a dotted under-edge: `underline dotted` already means "click this word to play from here" (recording.css:1092-1093) and reusing it would make one mark mean two things.

- **[high·ux-defect]** "Read this recording" is offered on recordings the engine will refuse to read
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:386`
  _Today:_ `ReadPanel`'s empty state renders `readButton("Read this recording", true)` unconditionally (RecordingView.tsx:378-392) — the comment above it calls the empty state "a BUTTON, not an apology" (263-265). But the Notes / Highlights / Chapters tabs are reachable on any recording, including one with no transcript, and `start_rec_read` refuses that case outright: `if turns.is_empty() { return Err("That recording has no transcript to read yet.".into()); }` (src-tauri/src/commands/jobs/rec_read.rs:534-536). `startReading` (1223-1233) optimistically sets `reading`, catches, resets, and dumps the backend sentence into an error toast. So on exactly the file that most needs help — audio, no words — the prominent button on three tabs is a dead end that answers with an engine error string.
  _Problem:_ The one primary button on three empty tabs fails by design on the recordings that reach those tabs empty, and explains itself with a backend error message.
  _Do:_ Pass `hasTranscript = segments.length > 0` into `ReadPanel` and branch the empty state on it: with no transcript, replace the read button with the sentence "Nothing has been transcribed yet — there is nothing here to read." and the same hoisted `Re-transcribe` control the transcript empty state should get (RecordingView.tsx:2578-2615). With a transcript, keep today's button. This is the same predicate as `emptyTranscriptState(stage)`, so put it in recReview.ts once.

- **[high·ux-defect]** "Reading this recording…" never clears if the read job fails
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:592`
  _Today:_ `reading` is set optimistically in `startReading` (1223-1233) and cleared in exactly one place: the `onRecReadDone` handler (720-727). The engine emits that event only on success — `if matches!(outcome, RunOutcome::Done) { window.emit("rec-read-done", …) }` (src-tauri/src/commands/jobs/rec_read.rs:719-722). A read that fails mid-way, is cancelled, or dies with the job runner emits nothing the view listens for, so `reading` stays true for the life of the mount: all three tabs show the busy panel "Reading this recording…" with "this can take a minute" (368-376) and a disabled read button (359), for work that has already stopped. It also affects the automatic reads, since `rec_stop` and the background sweep both start this job.
  _Problem:_ The screen claims a job is running long after it has failed, and disables the only control that could retry it — the exact failure the app's honesty rule exists to prevent.
  _Do:_ Emit the terminal event for every outcome, not just success: change rec_read.rs:719-722 to always emit `rec-read-done` with an `ok: matches!(outcome, RunOutcome::Done)` field. RecordingView's handler (720-727) keeps `setReading(false)` unconditionally, reloads the meta when `ok`, and pushes an error toast otherwise. No new UI; one field on an event that already exists.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The Recordings shelf sends the newest recording's name to the room's model to write a subtitle
  `src/workspace/RecordingsPage.tsx:69`
  _Today:_ On every render with at least one recording, the page builds `recDekFacts` including `newestName: displayName(newest.name)` (RecordingsPage.tsx:69-76) and hands it to `useAdaptiveText` inside a prompt that embeds `JSON.stringify(recDekFacts)` (77-86), purely to replace the static subtitle "Captured here, transcribed here" (100-102). That call goes to `api.generateUiText` → `generate_ui_text`, whose model comes from `resolve_structured_model` (src-tauri/src/commands/moonshot.rs:28-42) — which returns the room's explicitly set model as-is when `is_external_engine(m)`. So in a cloud room, opening the Recordings shelf sends a recording's filename to the provider, without a press and without a word on screen. The trust chip in the status bar says what kind of room this is; nothing on this page says that this decorative sentence is a model call at all.
  _Problem:_ A file name — often the most sensitive thing about a meeting — leaves the machine to generate a subtitle nobody asked for, on a page whose whole subject is private capture.
  _Do:_ Prefer removal: delete `recDekFacts`, the `useAdaptiveText` call (69-86) and the `recDek ??` at line 101, leaving the static "Captured here, transcribed here". That deletes a network call, an import and 18 lines, and costs a sentence nobody navigates for. If the living dek is kept, at minimum drop `newestName` from `recDekFacts` — counts alone identify nobody — and gate `enabled` on the room's engine being local.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The transcript never follows playback
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:967`
  _Today:_ `onTime` (RecordingView.tsx:967-985) sets `activeSeg` from `segmentAt`, and CSS paints that phrase with `.rec-line.is-active` (recording.css:786) plus `.rec-seg.active` (recording.css:811). Nothing scrolls it into view. The file has exactly two scroll calls: the live-capture tail-follow, `if (recordingNow) listEndRef.current?.scrollIntoView(...)` (770-772), and the one-shot "Show in transcript" effect (957-965). The scroll container is `.rec-panel` (recording.css:719-724, `overflow-y: auto`); `listRef` is on `.rec-transcript` inside it (2119). So pressing Play on a long meeting lights a phrase that walks off the bottom within seconds and never comes back.
  _Problem:_ During review the highlighted phrase scrolls out of view and never returns — the one thing playback/transcript sync exists for.
  _Do:_ Add a follow effect beside the existing `findSeg` one (957-965), copying its pattern exactly: on `playing && activeSeg` changing, `listRef.current?.querySelector(`[data-seg="${CSS.escape(activeSeg)}"]`)?.scrollIntoView({ block: "center", behavior: prefersReducedMotion() ? "auto" : "smooth" })`. Give `.rec-panel` its own ref (it has none today) and hang a `scroll` listener on it that clears a `followRef` when the user scrolls during playback; `seek()` (987-992), `scrubTo()` (997-1005) and `showInTranscript()` (1010-1014) set it back to true. No new visible control.

- **[high·ux-defect]** A recording with audio and no transcript dead-ends: the page tells you to record MORE
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:2124`
  _Today:_ When `segments.length === 0` and there are no partials, the transcript panel prints the product pitch — "This file records and understands speech — live. Press *Continue recording*: your words … appear here as text" (RecordingView.tsx:2124-2151). The branch is on `status === "idle"`; `everRecorded` is consulted only to swap the button's NAME at 2136. So a file holding an hour of audio and no words (live transcription off, the speech model missing at the time, an old recording) is told the answer is to record more into it. The control that fixes it sits inside a collapsed `<details>` titled "Export & rebuild" with the hint "Transcript, subtitles, edited copy, translation, re-transcribe" (2538-2544); the button and its confirm are at 2578-2615. RecordingsPage sends people straight here: `ATTENTION_COPY["not-yet"] = "Nothing has read it yet. Open it to write it up."` (RecordingsPage.tsx:341).
  _Problem:_ The one screen that can rescue an untranscribed recording advertises the feature that already ran, and hides the rescue behind a closed drawer named after exports.
  _Do:_ Split the `rec-empty` branch on `stage` (recReview.ts:47-50) rather than on `status`. For `stage === "finished"`: "This recording has audio but no transcript — nothing has written it up yet." plus the `canRetranscribe` block hoisted out of the drawer (reuse 2578-2615 verbatim, confirm included), labelled "Write it up" here. Keep the pitch copy for `stage === "fresh"` only. The drawer keeps its own copy for the re-run case. Put the choice in recReview.ts as `emptyTranscriptState(stage)`.

- **[high·consistency]** Imported audio gets a strictly poorer review screen than a room recording — and the shelf invites the import
  `src/viewers/registry.tsx:333`
  _Today:_ `isRecordingFile` counts every `audio/*` and `video/*` file as a recording (api.ts:1571-1577), the Recordings shelf tallies them, and its empty state invites them: "drag in audio and video files, which write themselves up in the background" (RecordingsPage.tsx:120-123). But the viewer registry routes kind `audio`/`video` to `AudioView` (registry.tsx:351-385) and only kind `recording` to `RecordingView` (334-350). AudioView reconstructs speakers by regexing `"Name: text"` out of the extracted text (AudioView.tsx:56-64) and offers no renaming, no correction, no delete-from-recording, no marks/notes/chapters, no search, no exports and no read pass. Its only remedy for a wrong transcript is a `Transcribe` / `Re-transcribe` button with no confirmation (AudioView.tsx:468-482), where the same act inside RecordingView demands one and warns that speaker names are re-numbered (RecordingView.tsx:2578-2603). AudioView is not simply the lesser screen, though: it owns video playback, the container facts strip (435-444), trim and Save frame (488-530), which RecordingView has no equivalent for.
  _Problem:_ Two review screens for the same job, with the shelf, the counts and the copy insisting they are the same kind of thing — so which features a meeting has depends on how it got into the room.
  _Do:_ Do the cheap half first, this release: give AudioView's re-transcribe the same confirm and the same warning copy as RecordingView.tsx:2580-2603 (it destroys an existing transcript with one click today), and stop the label lying about which act it is. Then, for AUDIO only, route an `audio` file that has a transcript to `RecordingView` by building a `RecMeta` from its timed rows on first open (segments from `parseRows`, speakers from the label column, `words: []` so word-level delete correctly stays unavailable) — RecordingView already handles a wordless transcript (`visible === null` falls back to `seg.text`, 819-831) and `canRetranscribe` already covers a meta with no duration (1495). Leave `video` on AudioView until trim, Save frame and the facts strip have a home; a merge that loses them is not a merge.

- **[high·view-quality]** Split RecordingView: capture and review are two screens sharing one file
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:562`
  _Today:_ 2,668 lines; 44 `useState`/`useRef` declarations inside the component (48 in the file, four of them in `SpeakerChip` and `ReadPanel`); 30 lines where `isLive`/`recordingNow` decides what renders. One component owns: ten event subscriptions plus two mount fetches (651-767), the `<audio>` element and its cut-skip timer (900-1035), the document `selectionchange` listener and the four edit verbs (1045-1233), speaker naming (1235-1290), translation/re-transcription/three exports (1292-1462), and roughly 1,030 lines of JSX (1620-2653) covering a capture console, a player, four tabs, a clips rail, a selection bar and a drawer. Nothing structural separates the two screens even though they are mutually exclusive: `showsChoicesInline` / `needsPreflight` already encode that the capture choices belong to one and not the other (recReview.ts:45-61).
  _Problem:_ Every change to the capture console risks the review screen and vice-versa, and the rules that decide what renders are buried in JSX — which is exactly where the last three P1s in this view were found.
  _Do:_ Keep `RecordingView` as a ~120-line shell owning `meta` and the element, and split by JOB, not by JSX block: (1) `useRecordingMeta(fileId, pushToast)` — the ten `api.onRec*` subscriptions plus the reload effects, returning `{meta, setMeta, partials, levels, durationCs, sysNote, micNote, liveTranslations, translating, retrans}`; (2) `useRecPlayback(mediaRef, cuts, segments)` — src, play/pause, volume/rate mirrors, `armCutSkip`, `scrubTo`, `activeSeg`; (3) `RecCapture.tsx` — the transport's live half, meters, mute, live-STT, preflight, the two source banners, the ghosts; (4) `RecReview.tsx` — clock, seek+marks, volume/speed, waveform, facts; (5) `RecTranscript.tsx` — turns, ghosts, selection capture, with a `follow` prop that is "end" while capturing and "playhead" while reviewing (see the follow-playback item); (6) `RecSelectionBar.tsx`; (7) `RecToolsDrawer.tsx`; and move `SpeakerChip` and `ReadPanel` into their own files. The `<audio>` element and `mediaRef` stay in the shell so "one player" survives the split.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Timestamp buttons promise "Play from here" while recording, and do nothing
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:2192`
  _Today:_ The `<audio>` element only renders when `canPlay` (`!!src && durationCs > 0`, RecordingView.tsx:1496; `src` is null whenever `isLive`, 901; element at 2002-2033), so during capture `mediaRef.current` is null and `seek` returns silently at `if (!el) return` (987-992). But every phrase in a live transcript still renders `<button className="rec-stamp" title="Play from here" aria-label={`Play from ${…}`} onClick={() => seek(seg.t0)}>` (2192-2199); every word does the same (2212-2215); and every findings row draws a time button titled "Play from this moment" with `aria-label={`Play from ${…}`}` (411-421), reachable mid-meeting because "Mark this moment" writes highlights live (1169-1178). The clips list already handles this correctly with `disabled={!canPlay}` (2347).
  _Problem:_ During a live meeting the transcript is full of controls that claim to play audio and silently do nothing — including to a screen reader, which announces each one as "Play from 2:14".
  _Do:_ Thread `canPlay` into the transcript rows and `ReadPanel`. When false, render the stamp as a plain `<span className="rec-stamp nb-num">` (no button, no promise), drop the word `onClick`, and make the findings row's time button `disabled` with `title="Playback starts when the recording is stopped"`. Put the predicate in recReview.ts as `canSeekNow(status, durationCs, hasSrc)` so it is tested once rather than re-derived at four call sites.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Naming a voice teaches the whole room, and only the receipt says so
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:221`
  _Today:_ The chip's tooltip scopes the act to this file: "Rename — the engine calls this voice Speaker 2" / "Name this speaker — renames every line they said" (RecordingView.tsx:221-227), and the edit input's accessible name is the bare `Name for ${label}` (190). The larger consequence is disclosed only afterwards, in a transient toast: "Every line Speaker 2 said is now "Dana". New recordings will recognise this voice." (1273-1286). That store is real and is read back — `speakerGuessed` checks `meta.recognized` (1245-1248), and `renameSpeaker` has a dedicated confirm-the-guess branch (1252-1257) — so a typo, or a name typed onto the wrong voice, propagates into recordings that have not been made yet, warned about only by a toast the user may already have dismissed.
  _Problem:_ An edit with room-wide, forward-in-time consequences is described as a local rename until after it has happened.
  _Do:_ Say it before the act, in the two strings already there: the unnamed tooltip (226) becomes "Name this speaker — renames every line they said here, and this room will recognise the voice in later recordings", and the edit input's `aria-label` (190) becomes `Name for ${label} — also taught to this room`. No new UI; the toast then only has to confirm.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Clicking a word starts playback
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:2212`
  _Today:_ Every rendered word is clickable and plays: `onClick={() => { if (window.getSelection()?.isCollapsed) seek(w.t0); }}` (RecordingView.tsx:2212-2215), and `seek` always calls `el.play()` (987-992). The same surface is the editing surface — words are selected here to delete or correct them (`captureSelection`, 1045-1088, wired at 2122) — so one area answers a click with audio and a drag with an edit bar. A real play affordance already exists on every line: the stamp button, titled "Play from here" (2192-2199).
  _Problem:_ Reading a transcript with the mouse makes the room start talking; the click meant to place attention starts audio instead.
  _Do:_ Change the word handler (2214) to `scrubTo(w.t0)` (997-1005), which moves the playhead and updates the clock and the active phrase without starting the element — and which, when audio is already playing, simply continues from the new point. The stamp button keeps `seek` and stays the one control that starts playback, exactly as its title says.

- **[medium·copy]** Three words for one thing: transcribe, write up, read
  `src/workspace/RecordingsPage.tsx:336`
  _Today:_ On the shelf, "write up" and "read" both mean transcribe: `"not-yet": "Nothing has read it yet. Open it to write it up."` and `"model-missing": "Install a speech model in Settings and this will transcribe itself."` (RecordingsPage.tsx:336-342), with the chip "Writing up" for a job in flight but "Transcribed" once done (433-439), and the figure label "written up of 12" (468-473). One click away, inside the recording, "read" means something else entirely — the AI findings pass: "Read this recording", "Reading this recording…", "the room read it and found none" (RecordingView.tsx:356-392) — while the act the shelf calls "writing up" is called "Transcribing" and "Re-transcribe" there (2608-2614), and "Transcribing on this Mac…" in AudioView (459).
  _Problem:_ The word that means "turn speech into text" on one screen means "have the AI analyse the text" on the next, and the shelf cannot even keep one word for it across two lines of the same function.
  _Do:_ One meaning per word. Speech→text is "transcribe" everywhere: `ATTENTION_COPY["not-yet"]` → "Nothing has transcribed it yet. Open it and press Re-transcribe.", the in-flight chip (434) and NowPanel heading (544) → "Transcribing", and `transcribedPhrase`'s four strings (468-473) → "transcribed", "transcribed — all of them", "transcribed — none of N yet", "transcribed of N". Leave "read" to the findings pass, where the vocabulary is already consistent.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** Playback speed and volume reset to 1× on every recording you open
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:625`
  _Today:_ `volume` and `rate` are component state initialised to 1 (RecordingView.tsx:625-626), and the viewer host remounts per file — `<div key={`${openFile.id}-${c.kind}-${rev}`}>` (ViewerRouter.tsx:257). So someone reviewing five meetings at 1.5× sets the speed five times. The app already has the pattern: mic mute and live-STT are held in module state in liveRec.ts (52-61, 101-107) explicitly "because the view unmounts while the recording keeps running" (47-51), and RecordingView reads them back on mount (614-615). The effect at 948-952 already pushes both values onto a freshly mounted element.
  _Problem:_ A preference re-stated on every file, in a screen whose whole purpose is working through a stack of recordings.
  _Do:_ Move the two mirrors to module state in recReview.ts (`lastRate()` / `noteRate()`, same for volume), initialise `useState(lastRate())` / `useState(lastVolume())` at 625-626, and write through in the element's own `onVolumeChange` / `onRateChange` handlers (2022-2026), which are already the single write path. Nothing persists to disk; it lasts the session, like the mute.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Subtitles are exported timed to a file that may not exist
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:1346`
  _Today:_ With any cut present, `exportTranscript("srt")` re-times every cue onto the SHORTENED timeline (`keptPhrases(true)` → `cutShiftBefore`, RecordingView.tsx:1314-1331, 1351) and says so honestly: "Saved "x.srt" into this room — timed for the edited copy, not the original." (1376-1381). But the edited copy is a separate button the user may never have pressed (`exportClean`, 1389-1405, drawn at 2640-2649). The room is then holding a subtitle file that lines up with no audio in it, and the receipt does not offer the missing half — even though `pushToast` takes an action (`{ label, run }`, 85-89) that Toasts.tsx:54-66 renders as a real button.
  _Problem:_ An export that is correct in isolation and useless in practice, with the file it matches one un-pressed button away.
  _Do:_ When `cuts.length > 0`, attach the action to that toast (1376-1381): `pushToast("success", `Saved "${f.name}" into this room — timed for the edited copy, not the original.`, { label: "Export the edited copy", run: () => void exportClean() })`. If the edited copy already exists the button is a harmless re-export; if it does not, one click completes the pair.

- **[medium·consistency]** The save readout is written twice, in different words, from the same drain
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:1663`
  _Today:_ RecordingView derives the saving sentence inline (1663-1677): "Audio saved — writing the recording into the room…" / "Audio saved — finishing the transcript (3 to go)" / "Audio saved — finishing the transcript…" / "Saving…". RecordingsPage exports `saveDetail(recSave)` for the same `SaveDrain`, and its wording differs on three of four branches (RecordingsPage.tsx:267-273): "Audio saved — writing into the room…" / "Audio saved — transcribing (3 to go)" / "Audio saved — finishing the transcript…". Its doc comment says these are "the same three sentences the Activity pane uses, and for the same reason" — so there are three consumers of one rule and one of them re-derives it. TopBar's chip is a fourth, showing a bare "Saving…" (TopBar.tsx:200-202).
  _Problem:_ One save, described three ways across three screens the user moves between while waiting to find out whether their recording is safe.
  _Do:_ Import `saveDetail` into RecordingView and replace the inline chain at 1663-1677 with `{saveProgress ? saveDetail(saveProgress) : "Saving…"}`, keeping the `.rec-save-note` line beneath it. Move `saveDetail` out of RecordingsPage.tsx into recReview.ts (or workspace/recSession.ts, which already exists for exactly this kind of shared rule) so the shelf, the viewer and the top-bar chip all read one function.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The transcript's accessible name promises edits that a live recording refuses
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:2121`
  _Today:_ `.rec-transcript` carries a fixed `aria-label="Transcript — select words here to correct them, or to delete them from the recording"` (RecordingView.tsx:2117-2122), and it is focusable (`tabIndex={0}`). But editing is gated on `canEdit = !isLive` (1483), and the selection bar with Mark / Note here / Fix the words / Delete from recording only renders when `selection && canEdit` (2403). The comment at 1480-1482 explains why: the backend refuses `rec_delete_range` while the file has a live session. The visible footer hint that says the same thing is correctly conditioned on `!isLive` (2265-2270); the accessible name is not, so a screen-reader user mid-meeting is told to select words for an action that will silently produce no bar.
  _Problem:_ A screen reader is told, during capture, to do the one thing the capture makes impossible — and the sighted equivalent of that sentence is already correctly hidden.
  _Do:_ Make the label follow the same condition as the footer hint: `aria-label={isLive ? "Transcript — appearing as people speak" : "Transcript — select words here to correct them, or to delete them from the recording"}` at 2121.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "Listening… speak, or bring the meeting on" is shown while paused and while saving
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:2149`
  _Today:_ The empty transcript branches on `status === "idle"` only (RecordingView.tsx:2126): everything else falls to `<p className="rec-empty-lead">Listening… speak, or bring the meeting on.</p>` (2148-2150). `status` also takes the values `paused` and `saving` (647), where the microphone is closed — the transport itself says so a few hundred lines up ("Paused at 4:12", 1653-1657; "Audio saved — …", 1658-1677) and the state tape reads "Paused" / "Saving" (1509-1512). So a paused meeting with nothing transcribed yet tells the user to keep talking, and so does one being written out.
  _Problem:_ The page invites speech into a closed microphone, contradicting the transport chip and the state tape on the same screen.
  _Do:_ Branch on `stage` (recReview.ts:47-50) instead of `status`: `recording` keeps "Listening… speak, or bring the meeting on.", `paused` gets "Paused — nothing is being recorded. Resume above to keep going.", `saving` gets "Saving — the audio is already safe; the transcript is finishing.", and `fresh`/`finished` get the two states described in the dead-end item above. One `switch` in `emptyTranscriptState(stage)`, tested in recReview.test.mjs.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** The always-visible recording chip says less than the app knows
  `src/workspace/TopBar.tsx:190`
  _Today:_ The chip is the only capture surface once you navigate away — "Recording" / "Recording paused" / "Saving…" with a pulsing dot and a fixed `title="A live recording is running — click to open it"` (TopBar.tsx:189-203). It carries no elapsed time, no stop, and no health. Elapsed time exists (`rec-level` carries `durationCs`) but only RecordingView subscribes to it (RecordingView.tsx:728-732); lane failures reach the user as a single toast when the view is closed (effects.ts:678-687, which already tracks a `flaggedSources` set keyed `${fileId}:${source}`) and then vanish, after which the chip still reads "Recording" with a live dot though the mic lane is dead. The `saving` case is worse: the drain's counts are in workspace state (`s.recSave`) and the chip shows a bare "Saving…", while RecordingsPage spells out "Audio saved — transcribing (3 to go)" from the same value (`saveDetail`, RecordingsPage.tsx:267-273).
  _Problem:_ The one thing on screen while you are in the meeting either under-reports a save that is already safe or over-reports a capture that has half-failed.
  _Do:_ Two cheap truths, no new panel. (1) In the saving state, APPEND `saveDetail(s.recSave)` (already exported, RecordingsPage.tsx:267) to the chip's existing title rather than replacing it, so hovering answers "is my recording safe" without losing "click to open it". (2) Promote the lane health `effects.ts` already computes: have the `onRecSource` listener (678-687) write a workspace `recSources` map alongside `flaggedSources`, and when a lane is in error render the chip as "Recording · mic only" / "Recording · no mic" with the dot in the pending colour. Motion stays exactly where it is — the pulsing dot only ever means a healthy open microphone.

- **[medium·test-gap]** The rules that decide what the review screen may claim are still in JSX
  `src/viewers/recReview.ts:1`
  _Today:_ recReview.ts exists precisely because "the three P1s this module was written for were all cases of one of those rules being buried in JSX, where it could not be checked and quietly went wrong" (recReview.ts:1-13), and it is covered by e2e/page-script/recReview.test.mjs. The decisions this review turned up are all still inline and therefore untestable: whether a timestamp may claim "Play from here" (`canPlay` is defined once at RecordingView.tsx:1496 and consulted at exactly one place, 2347 — the three sites that make the claim, 2192, 2212 and 411, never ask it); what the stop receipt is allowed to say (recordingActions.ts:462); whether "Show deleted" should be offered (`hasWords`, RecordingView.tsx:1484); and which empty state an untranscribed-but-recorded file gets (RecordingView.tsx:2126).
  _Problem:_ Every claim this review found to be false lives in exactly the place the codebase already decided such claims must not live.
  _Do:_ Move all four into recReview.ts as pure functions — `canSeekNow(status, durationCs, hasSrc)`, `stopReceipt(meta)`, `hasDeletedWords(segments)`, `emptyTranscriptState(stage)` — and add cases to e2e/page-script/recReview.test.mjs for each: a live session yields no seek, a zero-segment meta yields the "nothing was transcribed" receipt, an unedited word-timed transcript yields no Show-deleted, and `stage === "finished"` yields the transcribe prompt rather than the record pitch.

- **[low·ux-defect]** The translate drawer shows "Translating 0/1…" before it knows how many parts there are
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:1295`
  _Today:_ `runTranslate` seeds progress optimistically with a made-up denominator: `setTranslating({ done: 0, total: 1 })` (RecordingView.tsx:1292-1296). That value is rendered in two places straight away — the drawer's always-visible run tape, `Translating ${translating.done}/${translating.total}…` (2546-2552), and the button label (2574) — so a 40-minute transcript announces "Translating 0/1…" until the first real `rec-translate-progress` event replaces it (754-757). The 1 is not a small number, it is a number nobody computed. The neighbouring re-transcribe seed is honest by contrast: `{ doneCs: 0, totalCs: Math.max(1, durationCs) }` uses the real duration (1432).
  _Problem:_ A count the app invented is shown as a fact, in a product whose stated rule is never to show a fabricated progress figure.
  _Do:_ Model the pre-first-event state as unknown rather than as 0/1: make `translating` `{ done: number; total: number } | "starting" | null`, seed it with `"starting"` at 1295, and render "Translating…" (no figures) in both the tape (2547-2549) and the button (2574) until a real event arrives. Same treatment as the export button, whose comment at 600-606 already states this rule: "There is no honest percentage to show — nothing reports one — so the button says what it is doing and no more."

- **[low·code-quality]** The tabs' doc comment describes a design that was removed
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:110`
  _Today:_ The comment above `TABS` (RecordingView.tsx:110-120) states: "Transcript is the only one this app has data for today; the other three say so in their own words", explains an `empty` flag that "keeps that from being a lie told by the tab ROW", and instructs "Delete the flag, not the tab, on the day the data exists." That day has come: the `TABS` array (121-126) carries no flag, the tab bar renders counts instead and says so — "The count IS the state now. These tabs used to carry an `empty` flag … the room fills them in today" (2056-2059) — and `ReadPanel` fills all three from `meta`. In a codebase where the comments are the design record (recReview.ts:1-13 is a load-bearing example), a header block asserting the opposite of the code below it is the most expensive kind of stale.
  _Problem:_ The file's own explanation of its tab row contradicts the tab row, in a component whose comments are how its rules are transmitted.
  _Do:_ Replace 110-120 with what is true: the four tabs read one `RecMeta`, three of them render `ReadPanel` and are filled by the `rec_read` job, and each tab states its own count so "is there anything in Notes" is answered before a click. If the merge-into-Findings item is taken, this comment is rewritten there instead — but it must not survive either way.


### Private browser UX: chrome, search, reading view, privacy signalling (23)


- **[high·ux-defect]** "Nothing is written to disk" is contradicted by the room's own web cache
  `src/workspace/browserPrivacy.ts:169`
  _Today:_ The verified claim reads "Nothing is written to disk, and the tracker block list is loaded." (browserPrivacy.ts:169); the degraded claim repeats it (:161); `protectionAlert`'s unavailable branch repeats it a third time (:105). EPHEMERAL_VS_ROOM draws the durable side as only "The assistant's actions and the files you choose to save" (:65-68). Meanwhile `browser_clear_journal` calls `db::clear_web_cache` alongside `clear_browse_journal` (browse.rs:1084-1090), and `clearWarning` counts what that erases: "…and 42 cached items (searches, page text and previews)?" (browserJournal.ts:155-165).
  _Problem:_ The shield's tooltip promises nothing is written to disk while the same feature's Clear button offers to erase dozens of cached searches, page texts and preview images from the room's DB. Two surfaces of one feature state opposite facts, and the one making the promise is the security string.
  _Do:_ Three string edits, no new UI. Change "Nothing is written to disk" to "The web session writes nothing to disk" at browserPrivacy.ts:161, :169 and :105. Extend EPHEMERAL_VS_ROOM (:65-68) to name the third thing: "The assistant's actions, the searches and page text the room cached, and the files you choose to save stay in this encrypted room — the Journal's Clear erases the cache." browserTrust.test.mjs:141-146 already asserts the copy names both sides of the line; extend it to require the cache be named, and add a `!/^Nothing is written to disk/` guard so the unqualified promise cannot come back.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The results page says only the query left the Mac while it fetches eight result origins
  `src/workspace/BrowserSearch.tsx:420`
  _Today:_ `SearchHeader` prints a fixed "only your query left this Mac" (BrowserSearch.tsx:420) and three spans later a conditional "previews fetched privately" (:441-451). With previews on — the default (`result_previews_enabled`, search.rs:54-56) — the enrich pass requests the top eight result pages through the Rust guard (:99-116). On a cache hit the same header prints "no network touched" (:415) *and* "only your query left this Mac" (:420) in one sentence. The skeleton states it a third time before anything is known (:371).
  _Problem:_ With previews on the app has contacted eight strangers' servers from the user's IP while the line at the top of the paragraph says only the query left the machine; on a cache hit the same line is wrong in the other direction. This is the one screen where the privacy sentence is the product, and it is the only privacy string in the browser with no test behind it.
  _Do:_ Derive the single privacy clause from what actually happened, in a new exported pure function beside `privacyClaim` in browserPrivacy.ts — `searchPrivacyLine({cached, previewsEnabled, previewCount})`: cached → "nothing left this Mac"; previews on → "your query, and a request to the top 8 result pages, left this Mac"; previews off → "only your query left this Mac". Delete the separate "previews fetched privately" span (:441-451) and fold its content (no cookies, no scripts, no fingerprint, switchable in Settings → Online features — a section that does exist, OnlineSection.tsx:45) into the new clause's `title`. Use the same function in `BrowserSearchSkeleton` (:371), where only the previews-unknown case applies. One truthful sentence replaces three that argue with each other, and it becomes testable at the same time.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Back, Forward and Reload drive the invisible page behind the results list
  `src/workspace/browserChrome.ts:92`
  _Today:_ `chromeAbilities` gates `save` and `read` on `usable` (open && not parked) but `navigate` on `open` alone (browserChrome.ts:88-96), so the three nav buttons (BrowserView.tsx:789-814) stay live while `searchOpen` parks the page at 1×1. `nav()` (:635-642) calls `browser_go`, which evals `history.back()` straight into the hidden webview and emits no event (browse.rs:1033-1052); nothing closes the results list, and only the 1.2s poll notices anything moved.
  _Problem:_ Back is the key most people press to leave a results page. Here it silently moves the invisible page behind the list, leaves the list up, and gives no feedback at all — the user is looking at results over a page that has gone somewhere else.
  _Do:_ In BrowserView, intercept `nav("back")` while `searchOpen`: close the results and show the page again, which is what Back means from a results page in every other browser. Gate `navigate` in `chromeAbilities` on `!view.searchOpen` so Forward and Reload go disabled rather than acting on a hidden document (the existing test at browserTrust.test.mjs:210-219 already builds that exact view and can assert it in one added line). Leave `navigate` true for `blank` and `readerHasTheFloor` — both are coherent, since the reader re-reads on `info.url` change (BrowserReader.tsx:152-154).

- **[high·ux-defect]** The chrome shows no loading state, and Stop is unreachable for the commonest navigation
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:806`
  _Today:_ The third nav button swaps between Reload and Stop purely on `busy` (BrowserView.tsx:806-813), and `busy` is only ever set by a typed address (:537), an opened result (:589) or a new tab (:616). Clicking a link inside the page sets nothing, so the chrome shows no loading state and Stop is unreachable for the commonest navigation there is. The app already knows better: `info.ready` is polled every 1.2s and drives the screen-reader line "Loading {where}" (browserAnnounce.ts:106-108).
  _Problem:_ Click a link inside a page and there is no sign the browser is doing anything and no way to stop a heavy load — the button still reads "Reload the page". Assistive tech is told "Loading example.com"; the eye is told nothing. The two channels disagree about the same fact, from the same poll.
  _Do:_ Derive `loading = info.open === true && !blank && !info.error && info.ready !== "complete"` in BrowserView (the `!info.error` term matters — a stalled page reports no `ready`, and without it the button would sit on Stop forever beside the stalled banner at :1039-1043). Use `busy || loading` for the Stop/Reload swap and its aria-label. Mark the address bar with a static `data-loading` attribute the stylesheet renders as an edge tick — a state the user needs, with nothing in motion. Also fix apiTypes.ts:1753: `ready?: string | boolean | null`, because `reportable_page_state` returns a JSON `false` mid-navigation (browse.rs:1014-1022) and the current type says that cannot happen.

- **[high·ux-defect]** "Ask the assistant about this" can land in a pane nobody can see, and promises reading it does not do
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:1243`
  _Today:_ `onAsk={(query) => s.setQuestion(query)}` (ViewerPane.tsx:1243) only writes the composer; it never reveals the Assistant, which is hidden in two layout presets. The file viewer's "Quote in chat" does the same and then calls `layout.showPane("ai")`, with the reason in a comment: "a quote landing in a box nobody can see is the same as no quote at all" (ViewerPane.tsx:553-563). The button's copy is "it can read these pages and work from them; the results are already cached, so its search is free" (BrowserSearch.tsx:305-311) — true of the assistant's tools, but nothing is attached by the press, the scope in force is `room`, and with `web_agent_browse` off (commands.rs:1066-1079) the assistant cannot open those pages at all.
  _Problem:_ In two layout presets the results page's largest call to action produces no visible change whatsoever. And its copy describes what the assistant could do rather than what the button does, so a user who presses it has no way to tell whether anything happened.
  _Do:_ Change the prop to `onAsk={(query) => { s.setQuestion(query); layout.showPane("ai"); }}`, matching the quote path exactly — one line, no new surface. Rewrite the second clause to what the press does: "puts the question in the chat — these results are already cached, so the assistant's own search costs nothing." (BrowserSearch.tsx:305-311). If the stronger promise is wanted instead, the button must attach the top three results through the existing `add` path (:128-145) so the claim is made good rather than asserted.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The search skeleton is drawn underneath the still-live page, so a search from an open page shows nothing
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:1102`
  _Today:_ `{searching && !searchOpen && <BrowserSearchSkeleton query={pending} />}` (BrowserView.tsx:1102) renders while a search is in flight, and `.browser-body:has(> .bsearch) .browser-stage { flex: 0 0 0 }` (browser.css:682-686) collapses the stage placeholder to zero width because the skeleton also carries the `bsearch` class. But `searching` is not a parking cause: `chromeView` is built from `parked`, `blank`, `searchOpen` and `readerHasTheFloor` only (BrowserView.tsx:213-224), so `pushBounds` takes the non-parked branch, measures the now-zero-width stage, and bails at `if (r.width < 2 || r.height < 2) return;` (:246) without sending anything. Rust keeps the last rect it was given — the full pane — so the native page still covers the whole area.
  _Problem:_ Type words into the address bar of a page that is already open and press Enter, and the skeleton with its echoed query and its elapsed-seconds counter is painted underneath a native webview that is still showing the old page at full size. Nothing at all changes on screen until the results land. That is precisely the "it's stuck on Searching…" report the skeleton was written to answer (the owner report is quoted at BrowserSearch.tsx:344-350), and the fix is inert for the commonest way to start a search. The DOM half is already right; only the bounds push disagrees.
  _Do:_ In the `chromeView` memo (BrowserView.tsx:213-224) pass `searchOpen: searchOpen || searching`, which is the state the stylesheet at browser.css:682-686 already assumes, and add `searching` to the dependency array. The page then parks for the length of the search and the skeleton is visible. Pin it: `pageIsParked` is pure and already has a test block (browserTrust.test.mjs:221-233) — assert that the view BrowserView builds while `searching` parks the page, so the CSS rule and the bounds pusher cannot drift apart again.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The agent's Save has no parked-page guard, so it can write a one-pixel fragment into the room and call it the page
  `src-tauri/src/commands/browse/saved.rs:41`
