  _Today:_ `capture_and_save` calls `browser::call(app, "capture", …)` directly (saved.rs:37-45). Every other read of the live page goes through `ready_to_be_read` first, which spins until the webview's bounds clear `MIN_READABLE_PX` or refuses with PARKED_REFUSAL (reader.rs:61-85, called at :98 and :139). `capture` builds its text with `readMarkdown("main")` (page.js:807, 602-625), which drops any node failing `isVisible` — a `getBoundingClientRect` test (page.js:134-137). That is the exact layout dependence `too_small_to_read` exists for. The agent's `browse_save` reaches this with no guard at all (browse.rs:719-725).
  _Problem:_ The user has the results list or the reading view up — the page is parked at 1×1 — and the agent calls `browse_save`. The capture runs against a one-pixel layout viewport, `readMarkdown` drops nearly every node, and a fragment is written into the room under the real page's title and reported as "Saved …". The room's own doctrine is that a page reporting on itself from a 1×1 viewport answers ok:true with a fragment (reader.rs:68-71); the save path is the one path that never asks. It is also the reason item 6's Save-from-the-reader fix cannot simply take a borrow.
  _Do:_ Add `ready_to_be_read(app).await?` as the first line of `capture_and_save` (saved.rs:37-41) — the helper is already `pub`-visible within the module tree and already carries the settle loop and the user-facing refusal string. A save from a parked page then waits for real bounds or refuses in words, instead of succeeding with less than the page. reader.rs already has unit tests for `too_small_to_read` (:209, :223, :291); add one asserting the save command is on the guarded path.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Once you search there is no control that takes you back to the page you were on
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:993`
  _Today:_ The only banner offering to cross between the results and the page is the one shown when the results are CLOSED: `search && !searchOpen && !readerOpen` renders "◂ Results" (BrowserView.tsx:993-1002). Nothing renders the inverse. `setSearchOpen(false)` is reachable only from the agent's navigation event (:474), a typed address (:543), opening the reader (:562), opening a result (:588) or opening one in a new tab (:621) — every one of which either leaves the page or navigates it. `BrowserSearch`'s own `onKeyDown` (:183-206) handles arrows, Enter, p and a, and does not handle Escape.
  _Problem:_ Search from a page you were reading, decide none of the eight results is worth opening, and there is no way back: the list stays over your page, Escape does nothing, and Back drives the hidden page instead (item 4). The only exits are navigating away or leaving the destination entirely, both of which lose the page the search was meant to help with.
  _Do:_ Take item 4's fix as the primary one — intercept `nav("back")` while `searchOpen` and flip the results closed — and add the visible half by rendering the existing results row in reverse: when `searchOpen && info.open && !blank`, the same banner (BrowserView.tsx:993-1002) shows a "Page ▸" button calling `setSearchOpen(false)` with the address restored from `info.url`. That is one row that already exists, rendered in the state it is currently missing from, not a new surface. Add Escape to `BrowserSearch`'s `onKeyDown` (:183-206) calling a new `onClose` prop, matching the reader's Escape (BrowserReader.tsx:173-177) and the Save strip's (BrowserView.tsx:934-938).

- **[high·ux-defect]** Save goes dead exactly while you are reading the page you want to save
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:725`
  _Today:_ `can.save` is false whenever the page is parked, and the reading view parks it (browserChrome.ts:61-63, 88-96; the ability list is read at BrowserView.tsx:725). The reader's own toolbar (BrowserReader.tsx:235-277) offers mode, Re-read, Compare and Close — no Save. Unlike the read path, the save path has no bounds guard at all: `capture_and_save` (saved.rs:37-45) calls the page script's `capture` op directly, while `browser_page_text` and `browser_page_selection` both go through `ready_to_be_read` and its settle loop (reader.rs:73-85, 98, 139).
  _Problem:_ You read an article in the reading view, decide it is worth keeping, and Save is greyed out. Disabled buttons show no tooltip on macOS and nothing anywhere says "close the reading view first". The surface where the user has just decided a page is worth keeping is the one surface with no way to keep it.
  _Do:_ Two changes, in this order. First close the hole: add `ready_to_be_read(app).await?` to the top of `capture_and_save` (saved.rs:37-41), so a capture from a parked page waits for real bounds or refuses with PARKED_REFUSAL, exactly as every other read of the page already does. Only then wrap `runSave` (BrowserView.tsx:670-682) in `borrowStage(true)`/`borrowStage(false)` and let `can.save` stay true while `readerOpen` — with the settle loop in place the borrow is safe rather than a race. Then add one "Save into the room" button to `browser-reader-tools` (BrowserReader.tsx:235-277) calling the same `savePage`.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Asking about the page is refused exactly while you are reading it
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:434`
  _Today:_ `publishBrowserPage` sets `readable: !pageIsParked(chromeView)` (BrowserView.tsx:434-447). `readerHasTheFloor` is one of the four parking causes (browserChrome.ts:61-63), so with the reading view up `readablePage` returns null (browserScope.ts:53-54), `offeredScopes` collapses to `["room"]` (browserScope.ts:117-121), and the strip reads "Answering from the whole room" (AiPane.tsx:192 + browserScope.ts:151). The send path never even tries: `scope.sendsPageText` is false, so `scopedQuestion`'s `api.browserPageText` call (chatActions.ts:379) is skipped.
  _Problem:_ Open the reading view, read the article, ask "what does this say about X" — the turn is answered from the room index, not from the article on screen. The one moment the user is most obviously reading a page is the moment page scope is withdrawn, with no explanation beyond a silently changed strip.
  _Do:_ The reader already holds the text (`Loaded.text`, BrowserReader.tsx:44, filled at 91-98). Add `text: string | null` to `BrowserPageSignal` (browserSignal.ts:28-39) and to `same()` (45-53); have BrowserReader call a new `onText` prop from `load`'s success branch; have BrowserView publish `readable: !pageIsParked(chromeView) || readerText !== null` with that text; have `scopedQuestion` (chatActions.ts:365-390) use the carried text instead of calling `api.browserPageText` when it is present. More honest than a viewport borrow: the characters sent are the characters on screen, with no second extraction that could disagree. Note the carried text is the chunk the reader is showing, so `withPageContext`'s omitted-count must be computed from `Loaded.total`, not from the string length, or the turn will under-report what it left out.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Save selection is offered when nothing is selected, though the app already knows
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:951`
  _Today:_ "Save selection" is at BrowserView.tsx:951-957, gated on `saving || !can.save` like its three neighbours. `info.hasSelection` is already polled every beat, published to the rest of the app (:441-445) and is what makes the chat's "the selected passage" scope appear (browserScope.ts:120-121, :137) — the Save strip is the one consumer that ignores it. Pressing it with nothing highlighted fails down in the page script with "Nothing is selected on the page." (page.js:789).
  _Problem:_ The app knows there is no selection and offers the action anyway, so the failure is one it could have prevented. The enabled state is also the only place the browser chrome could show that it noticed a selection at all, and it is spent.
  _Do:_ Gate it: `disabled={saving || !can.save || info.hasSelection !== true}` at BrowserView.tsx:952. Its enabled state then IS the honest signal that a selection has been noticed, at zero new surface. browserTrust.test.mjs:252-265 currently asserts all four rows carry the same gate; extend it to assert the selection row asks one question more than its neighbours.

- **[medium·copy]** The claim this browser most needs to make disappears the moment you start browsing
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:1183`
  _Today:_ `NOT_ANONYMOUS` (browserPrivacy.ts:70-77) rides in every `claim.detail` branch, but `claim.detail` reaches the screen only as the shield's `title`/`aria-label` (BrowserView.tsx:720, 862-872). It is also in `startScreenCopy` (browserPrivacy.ts:176-190), which is rendered only while no page is loaded (BrowserView.tsx:1104-1115). Once a page loads, the entire visible privacy story is a chip reading "Private", and the journal panel — the surface the chip opens — prints `EPHEMERAL_VS_ROOM` alone (BrowserView.tsx:1183).
  _Problem:_ The sentence the module itself calls the one this browser owes the user harder than Safari or Firefox do has no persistent readable home. It exists on a screen you have left and in a tooltip you have to hover to find.
  _Do:_ Render the whole claim where a user goes to check it: change BrowserView.tsx:1183 to `{`${EPHEMERAL_VS_ROOM} ${NOT_ANONYMOUS}`}` (NOT_ANONYMOUS is already imported? no — add it to the existing import at :23-28). No new panel, no new control, no new string: the sentence gets a persistent home instead of living only in a hover.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "◂ Results" brings the list back but leaves the address bar naming the hidden page
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:995`
  _Today:_ `runSearch` sets the address to the query (BrowserView.tsx:512-513); `openResult` replaces it with the settled URL (:593); the "◂ Results" button (:995) only flips `setSearchOpen(true)`. The scheme badge is suppressed whenever `searchOpen` (:744), so the address box shows the hidden page's URL behind a magnifier with nothing to flag the mismatch.
  _Problem:_ After opening a result and coming back, the screen shows results for one query while the address box names the page hidden behind them. Pressing Enter navigates to that hidden page instead of re-running the search on screen.
  _Do:_ In the "◂ Results" handler (BrowserView.tsx:995), add `setAddress(search.query)` and `setEditing(false)` beside `setSearchOpen(true)` — the same two lines `runSearch` uses at :512-513. Closing the results needs nothing: the poll restores `info.url` while `!editing` (:400).

- **[medium·accessibility]** Four toggles change their label and set aria-pressed, which tells a screen reader the opposite state
  `src/workspace/BrowserReader.tsx:241`
  _Today:_ The reader's mode button is `aria-pressed={mode === "full"}` and reads "Main content only" in exactly that state (BrowserReader.tsx:241-251), so full mode is announced as "Main content only, pressed". "Compare with page"/"Hide the live page" has the same shape (:262-269), as do the skip link (BrowserView.tsx:776-784) and Take over/Hand back (:874-882). The correct pattern is already in the same file: "Read as text" keeps one fixed label and lets `aria-pressed` carry the state (:904-913).
  _Problem:_ A screen reader is told the reverse of what is true on four controls in this area — the label describes the action the press will take while `aria-pressed` describes the state it is in, and the two read as one sentence.
  _Do:_ Make all four match "Read as text": one stable label naming what the toggle controls — "Navigation, headers and footers", "Live page beside the text", "Page as text", "Take over" — with `aria-pressed` alone reporting on/off. Where a visible label swap is genuinely wanted (Take over/Hand back is the strongest case), drop `aria-pressed` on that one control instead. What must not survive is both at once. Note `.browser-takeover.on::after` (browser.css:534-541) keys off the class, not the attribute, so removing `aria-pressed` there costs no styling.

- **[medium·consistency]** The reading view calls every non-http address "Encrypted connection"
  `src/workspace/BrowserReader.tsx:166`
  _Today:_ The reader decides with `insecure = (page?.url ?? info.url ?? "").startsWith("http://")` (BrowserReader.tsx:166) and renders a green `nb-sem-done` "Encrypted connection" tape for everything else (:207-213) — including an empty URL, an unparseable one, and any non-http scheme. Three lines above, its own header is careful about the same fact. BrowserView parses with `schemeOf` and renders neither badge when the scheme is neither https nor http (BrowserView.tsx:87-94, 744-749).
  _Problem:_ A security claim is made by default rather than by check, in the one panel that renders a page's text as though it were a document.
  _Do:_ Move `schemeOf` out of BrowserView (:87-94) into browserAnnounce.ts beside `hostOf` (:26-33) — same module, same shape, already exported and already covered by browserannounce.test.mjs — and have both callers use it. The reader then shows the green tape only for `https:`, the red tape only for `http:`, and nothing otherwise. One rule, one test, one fewer private helper.

- **[medium·view-quality]** The journal and the reading view fight for the same pane
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:1133`
  _Today:_ The journal (`flex: 0 1 320px; min-width: 232px`, browser.css:816-823) and the reader (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0`, browser.css:1116-1123) are flex siblings in `.browser-body` (BrowserView.tsx:1133 and :1291; the container is `display:flex` at browser.css:665-670). Compare adds a third 320px item (browser.css:1141-1143). Nothing in either toggle prevents all three at once, and the one with the floor is the audit log.
  _Problem:_ Journal open + reader open + Compare on puts three columns in a centre pane already narrowed by the Assistant, and the document is the item that yields because it is the only one with `min-width: 0`. Nothing about the two panels' purposes wants them side by side.
  _Do:_ One body panel at a time: call `setJournalOpen(false)` inside `openReader` (BrowserView.tsx:561-573), and call `closeReader()` from the journal toggle (:868) — which already returns focus to the address bar. `.browser-body` then holds the stage plus at most one panel, and Compare's 320px sliver has room to be worth having.

- **[medium·test-gap]** No test pins navigation against parking, or the results page's privacy line against previews
  `e2e/page-script/browserTrust.test.mjs:203`
  _Today:_ The suite pins parking for save and read — "the results page parks the webview…" asserts only `can.read` and `can.save` (browserTrust.test.mjs:210-219) — and "a blank new tab can be navigated" positively asserts `navigate === true` (:203-208) without ever asking what navigate should do behind the results list. `privacyClaim`, `startScreenCopy`, `EPHEMERAL_VS_ROOM` and `NOT_ANONYMOUS` have twelve tests between them (:51-152). "only your query left this Mac" (BrowserSearch.tsx:420) has none — the string appears in no test file at all.
  _Problem:_ Two of the highest-impact defects here could be reintroduced and shipped past a fully green suite: the navigate-while-parked hole is untested, and the results page's privacy sentence — the only privacy claim in this feature with no coverage — is untestable in its current form because it is spelled inline in JSX.
  _Do:_ Add two cases. One asserting `chromeAbilities({open:true}, {...VIEW_OPEN, searchOpen:true}).navigate === false` once the parking fix lands — the view object is already built at :212-216 and can be reused. One pure-function test over the results header's privacy clause across cached / previews-on / previews-off, which requires extracting that clause out of the JSX into the exported `searchPrivacyLine` proposed above, beside `privacyClaim` where the rest of this app's privacy wording already lives and is already testable.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Re-running a search from the results page leaves the previous results on screen with no sign anything is happening
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:503`
  _Today:_ `runSearch` (BrowserView.tsx:503-519) sets `searching` and leaves `search` and `searchOpen` untouched until the new page arrives at :510-511. The skeleton is gated on `searching && !searchOpen` (:1102), which is false here. So a second search started from the results page — the natural thing to do, since the address bar holds the old query and the results page has the keyboard — shows the previous query's eight results, its previous timing and its previous engine dial, unchanged, until the new answer lands.
  _Problem:_ The results on screen are presented as the answer to a query that is no longer the one being asked, with no busy state anywhere and no way to tell how long it has been. The elapsed counter that exists precisely for this wait (BrowserSearch.tsx:351-356) never renders.
  _Do:_ Change the skeleton's guard at BrowserView.tsx:1102 from `searching && !searchOpen` to `searching`, and order the two branches so the skeleton renders instead of `<BrowserSearch>` while a search is in flight (:1092-1102). The stale list is replaced by the echoed new query and its elapsed count, which is what the skeleton was written to say. Combined with the parking fix above, both entry points into a search then show the same honest waiting state.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Clicking away from a half-typed address silently reverts it to the current page
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:850`
  _Today:_ The address input sets `editing` true on change (BrowserView.tsx:846-849) and false on blur (:850). The poll then overwrites the box: `if (!editing && next.url) setAddress(next.url)` (:400). Blur happens when the user clicks the native page, the Save button, the shield, or anything else in the chrome — the native webview is a sibling view, so focus really does leave the input.
  _Problem:_ Type half an address, glance at the page or press Save, and within 1.2 seconds what you typed is replaced by the current page's URL with no warning and no undo. `forgetThePage` goes to real trouble to protect a half-typed address from the close path (:335-344, and a test pins it at browserTrust.test.mjs:289-299) — the ordinary poll then throws it away through the other door.
  _Do:_ Change the blur handler at BrowserView.tsx:850 to `onBlur={() => setEditing(address !== (info.url ?? ""))}`: leaving the box with text that differs from the live page keeps `editing` true, so the poll leaves it alone, and leaving it unmodified releases it as before. Pressing Enter already clears `editing` via `go` (:541) and Escape-to-page is unaffected. Extend browserTrust.test.mjs:289-299, which already owns the "never take the address the user is typing" invariant, to cover the blur route.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "Ask the assistant about this" destroys whatever was already in the composer
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:1243`
  _Today:_ `onAsk={(query) => s.setQuestion(query)}` (ViewerPane.tsx:1243) overwrites the composer's contents with the search query. The comparable path in the same file appends: "Quote in chat" calls `s.setQuestion(withQuote(s.question, quote.text, openFile.content.name))` (ViewerPane.tsx:557), and `withQuote` explicitly preserves the draft — `return base ? `${base}\n\n${block}` : block` (quoteSelection.ts:175-179).
  _Problem:_ A half-written question in the chat box is deleted by pressing the results page's largest button, with no undo — the composer is a plain controlled input. The app already decided this is not acceptable for quoting a file; the browser's equivalent action never got the same treatment.
  _Do:_ Reuse the existing shape: `onAsk={(query) => { s.setQuestion(appendQuestion(s.question, query)); layout.showPane("ai"); }}`, with `appendQuestion` a two-line sibling of `withQuote` in quoteSelection.ts:175-179 (trim the draft, blank line, the query). Same file, same doctrine, and it folds into the one-line `showPane` fix item 7 already asks for.

- **[medium·accessibility]** The results page grabs the keyboard into a container with no accessible name
  `src/workspace/BrowserSearch.tsx:283`
  _Today:_ `<div className="bsearch" onKeyDown={onKeyDown} tabIndex={0} ref={listRef}>` (BrowserSearch.tsx:283) takes focus the moment results arrive (`listRef.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true })`, :213-215), and it carries no role and no accessible name. The results replace the native page, which emits nothing to assistive tech, and `announcement` (browserAnnounce.ts:87-116) only diffs `BrowserInfo` — a search produces no announcement at all. The empty-results branch is worse: it renders the same class with `tabIndex={-1}` and never attaches `listRef` (:252), so the focus move silently does nothing there. The reader shows the right pattern: a focusable `<h1 ref={headingRef} tabIndex={-1}>` that focus lands on when the view opens (BrowserReader.tsx:196, 161-163).
  _Problem:_ Press Enter on a query and, for a screen-reader user, focus jumps into an unnamed group with no announcement of where it went or that eight results exist — and the single-key shortcuts the page advertises are live on an element nothing has described. With no results the focus move does not happen at all, so the keyboard is left behind on the address bar while the visible page has changed completely.
  _Do:_ Give the header's existing `<h1 dir="auto">{result.query}</h1>` (BrowserSearch.tsx:394) an id and point the container at it: `role="group" aria-labelledby={headingId}` on :283, so the focus lands somewhere that names itself with the query already on screen — no new visible text. Attach `listRef` to the empty branch's container (:252) and give it `tabIndex={0}` so the focus effect works there too. Both are attributes on markup that already exists.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Adding the same result twice quietly makes a second copy in the room
  `src/workspace/BrowserSearch.tsx:86`
  _Today:_ The `adds` map that draws "In room · attached" (BrowserSearch.tsx:664-668) is cleared on every new results page (`setAdds({})`, :88). `import_search_result` (search.rs:300-312) → `import_web_source` → `save_web_markdown` steps past the existing name with `db::available_name` (files.rs:1204) and never looks for a file with the same source URL, even though `insert_file_from_url` stores that URL two lines later (files.rs:1206-1214).
  _Problem:_ A result saved from an earlier search shows a plain ＋ again; pressing it creates "Article.md" and "Article 2.md" with identical contents, and nothing on the card ever says the room already has it.
  _Do:_ Have `import_search_result` (search.rs:300-312) look up an existing `source_kind = "web"` file with the same source URL and return its `FileMeta` instead of inserting. Seed `adds` from that answer when the results page mounts (BrowserSearch.tsx:86-96) so the card reads "Already in the room" rather than offering to duplicate it. The same fix removes a class of near-duplicate room files that only search results produce.

- **[low·copy]** "Read as text" is described as an accessibility workaround, not as the reading mode it is
  `src/workspace/BrowserView.tsx:909`
  _Today:_ The button's title reads "The page is a separate native layer this app cannot put into the reading order. This shows its text here, where a screen reader and the keyboard can reach it." (BrowserView.tsx:909), while what it opens offers extracted main content, a main-only/full toggle, honest "first N of M characters" counts and links routed back through the private browser (BrowserReader.tsx:235-332).
  _Problem:_ The tooltip is an implementation confession, so a sighted mouse user has no reason to press it — a genuinely good article view is hidden behind an apology for the platform.
  _Do:_ Retitle BrowserView.tsx:909 to what it gives everyone: "The page as clean article text — selectable, searchable and reachable by keyboard, without the page's own layout." Keep the native-layer sentence where it belongs: on the skip link (:776-784), whose entire reason to exist is the reading order.


### Sketch, Create, Story and Studio: the making surfaces (23)


- **[high·ux-defect]** The Sketch landing's "New sketch" button cannot be clicked
  `src/viewers/sketch.css:511`
  _Today:_ `.sk-empty` is declared three times across two globally-imported stylesheets: skills.css:554 (grid, justify-items:start), sketch.css:332 (padding + dashed border + text-align:center + color) and sketch.css:511 (position:absolute; inset:0; display:grid; place-items:center; pointer-events:none). App.css imports skills.css at line 37 and sketch.css at line 41, so sketch.css wins every property it declares — and 332 and 511 do not conflict, they COMBINE. Three unrelated consumers wear the class: the sketch canvas overlay (SketchView.tsx:1726, which wants the absolute layer and re-enables the pointer only on `.sk-empty-card`, sketch.css:528), the Sketch destination's landing (ViewerPane.tsx:269), and the Skills list empty state (SkillsView.tsx:722). `.sk-empty p` (sketch.css:339) additionally recolours and re-fonts any paragraph inside all three.
  _Problem:_ Three separate consequences from one name collision. (1) The Sketch destination's landing — the first thing you see on choosing Sketch with nothing open — puts its `New sketch` primary button (ViewerPane.tsx:277-283) inside a `pointer-events:none` layer that is also absolutely positioned over the pane. The button is dead to the mouse; keyboard focus still activates it, which is how it survives a driver test. (2) The Skills empty state (SkillsView.tsx:722-730) is torn out of flow, centred, made pointer-transparent, and its `.sk-empty-copy` paragraph is restyled to the hand font by `.sk-empty p`. (3) The sketch canvas's own three-card offer inherits the dashed border and padding from 332 and draws a dashed rectangle around the whole drawing surface.
  _Do:_ Rename the canvas-overlay rules to a class only the canvas uses — `.sk-canvas-offer` / `-cards` / `-card` / `-title` / `-copy` — moving sketch.css:511-554 wholesale, and update SketchView.tsx:1726-1758 to match. Fold sketch.css:332-342 (`.sk-empty` + `.sk-empty p`) into that same rename, since ViewerPane's landing is the only other thing that wanted the dashed card and it can carry its own class. That leaves exactly one `.sk-empty` in the tree, skills.css:554, and gives SketchLanding a plain block whose button works with the mouse.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Nothing the room draws for you can be undone
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:345`
  _Today:_ `applyAgent` (SketchView.tsx:345-347) merges the agent's document into the user's and calls `setDoc(merged)` directly, bypassing `commit` (301-308) — the only place `pushHistory` runs on the ordinary edit path. The toolbar's Undo is `disabled={!history.past.length}` (1508) and `UNDO_DEPTH` is 80 (model.ts:968).
  _Problem:_ The headline gesture of this editor — open a blank sketch, ask the assistant to draw the room's files as a diagram — produces forty shapes with the toolbar's Undo greyed out, because the user's own history is still empty. The only way back is ⌘A then Delete, which also takes anything the user drew. In the mixed case it is worse: Undo is enabled but its top entry is a snapshot from before the agent arrived, so one press silently discards both the agent's drawing and the user's last stroke, and nothing on screen says that is what happened.
  _Do:_ In `applyAgent`, before `setDoc(merged)` at SketchView.tsx:347, add `setHistory((h) => pushHistory(h, docRef.current))` so the agent's arrival is exactly one undoable step. `historyHint` (model.ts:354) needs no change — it already reports depth honestly once there is a depth to report.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The Create page shows a progress bar pinned at 0% for the whole run
  `src/workspace/create/CreatePage.tsx:527`
  _Today:_ `Canvas` hand-rolls a meter instead of calling `jobMeter`: `const pct = live ? Math.min(100, Math.round(live.done)) : 0;` (CreatePage.tsx:527), rendered as a `role="progressbar"` with `aria-valuenow={pct}` and a `cr-job-fill` at `width: {pct}%` (536-544), plus a written `{pct}%` (545). The backend emits `done` only at variation boundaries — `done = (index * 100) / variations` (create.rs:654) — so for the ordinary one-picture or one-clip job `done` is 0 from the first event to the last. What actually moves is the label: create.rs:864-871 builds `"Filming… 43%"` from the provider's own published percentage and re-emits at the unchanged `base_done`.
  _Problem:_ A three-minute video generation shows a determinate bar at 0% and the written figure "0%" for its entire life, then disappears. A queued job that has not begun shows the same determinate 0% bar. `src/workspace/jobProgress.ts` was written to keep those three states apart and is documented at length at lines 1-16, but its only caller is `AiPane.tsx:945`; the one page where a paid, minutes-long job is actually watched computes its own number instead. Meanwhile the honest quantity — the provider's percentage — is already sitting in `live.label`, one line above, unused for the bar.
  _Do:_ Delete the `cr-job-bar` / `cr-job-fill` / `{pct}%` span (CreatePage.tsx:535-545) and the `pct` binding at 527. Leave the card as icon + `cr-job-title` + `cr-job-sub`, and make `cr-job-sub` read `live?.label ?? (j.status === "queued" ? "Queued" : "Working…")` — today it prints "Queued" for a running job that has not yet reported. The label already carries the provider's real percentage when there is one and states no number when there is not, which is exactly the rule jobProgress.ts writes down.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Your cast and shot lists disappear when a cloud catalogue fails
  `src/workspace/create/CreatePage.tsx:214`
  _Today:_ `CreatePage` gates its whole body on the model catalogue: `loading` replaces everything with "Reading what this room's models can do…" (199), `loadError` replaces it with an error note (201-205), and `models.length === 0` renders `EmptyShelf` INSTEAD of the tab strip (214-215). The Story tab — cast, portraits, shot lists, loglines, script splitter — is inside that gate at 253-260. `loading` is initialised `true` (50) and re-set true by the mount effect (75), so every arrival blanks the page while `list_create_models` is fetched.
  _Problem:_ Cast members, their portraits, shot lists and loglines are the user's own writing, held locally (`api.storyBoard` → `story_board`, api.ts:507). None of it needs a provider. But let a key expire or the network drop and the entire Story surface vanishes behind "No provider is connected" — the room refuses to show you your own notebook because somebody else's catalogue would not load. Every visit to Create also flashes blank first for the same reason.
  _Do:_ Move the gate inside. Render `cr-controls` and the three tabs (CreatePage.tsx:217-252) as soon as the fetch has been attempted, and let `surface === "story"` render `StoryTab` regardless of `models.length`. Show `EmptyShelf` and the `loadError` note only inside the `cr-worktable` branch (263-...), where a model actually matters. `StoryTab` already survives an empty catalogue: its selects fall back to `— pick —` (StoryTab.tsx:800, 838, 1504, 1532) and `ShotRow` disables Seconds on `lengths.length === 0` (1546).

- **[high·ux-defect]** The Create page's "made in this room" grid lists every sketch and the room summary
  `src/workspace/create/CreatePage.tsx:144`
  _Today:_ `CreatePage` builds its output grid from `const made = s.files.filter((f) => f.source === "generated");` (144), rendered under the heading "made in this room" with a file count (568-591), and used for the "Nothing made here yet" empty state (549-566). The contextual sidebar's equivalent list uses a different predicate entirely: `isCreationFile` = `f.originDestination === "create"` (types.ts:165, Sidebar.tsx:1423). On the Rust side `source = "generated"` is written by sketch creation (sketch.rs:259, 293, 472), the room deep-summary (summarize.rs:257), the organizer (organize.rs:481), podcast episodes (podcasts.rs) and video edits (video.rs:328, 382) — while `origin_destination` is set separately by `mark_section_only`, to "sketch" for drawings (sketch.rs:184, 478) and "create" only for generations (create.rs:716, 1060).
  _Problem:_ Every drawing in the room, the AI-written room summary, and anything the organizer produced appear in Create's own gallery under the heading "made in this room", beside a count that includes them — as though the generation bench had produced them and someone had paid a provider for them. The sidebar standing next to it, filtered correctly, shows a different and shorter list, so the two disagree about the room's contents with no way to tell which is right. The empty state "Nothing made here yet" also never appears in any room that has one sketch or one summary in it.
  _Do:_ Change CreatePage.tsx:144 to `const made = s.files.filter(isCreationFile);`, importing it from `./types` as Sidebar.tsx:30 already does. That is the predicate the destination contract defines and the one every other Create surface uses.

- **[high·ux-defect]** A finished picture opening itself throws the whole bench away
  `src/workspace/effects.ts:292`
  _Today:_ Every field of the generation bench lives in `CreatePage` local state — `prompt`, `frame`, `refs`, `seconds`, `resolution`, `aspectRatio`, `variations`, `pickedModel`, `handoff` (CreatePage.tsx:52-71). `CreatePage` is one branch of ViewerPane's area ternary (ViewerPane.tsx:1253-1254), and that whole ternary sits inside `{openFile ? (…) : (…)}` at line 637 — so opening any file unmounts it. On a finished job, `effects.ts:285-292` builds `open = () => void a.viewFile(p.fileId)` and calls `if (open && !midTurn) open();`, where `midTurn` is only `s.askingRef.current` — a chat turn in flight. Being on the Create page is not a reason to hold back.
  _Problem:_ Generate a picture, it lands, it opens itself, and the bench you typed it on is gone — prompt, starting frame, references, seconds, resolution and the 1/2/4 setting all reset to defaults. Closing the file returns you to an empty Create page. "Make a variant of that with the light warmer" means retyping everything. The code even argues the opposite case for chat: the comment at effects.ts:276-284 says an interruption delivered during primary work costs more than the same one at a boundary, then applies that reasoning only to `askingRef`. Composing a paid generation is primary work by the same standard.
  _Do:_ In effects.ts:286, widen the hold-back condition to `const midTurn = s.askingRef.current || s.areaRef?.current === "create";` (or whichever ref carries the current area) so a finished creation offers the toast's existing "Open" action instead of replacing the bench. The Create page's own grid (CreatePage.tsx:568-591) already shows the new file a moment later, so nothing is lost by not jumping.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The Creations sidebar prints "Filming… 43% 0/100"
  `src/workspace/Sidebar.tsx:1469`
  _Today:_ `CreationsNav`'s running rows render `{p ? `${p.label} ${p.done}/${p.total}` : j.status === "queued" ? "Queued" : "Working…"}` (Sidebar.tsx:1469). For every `create` job the backend sends `done` from `(index * 100) / variations` (create.rs:654) and re-emits video status at that same unchanged `base_done` (create.rs:871) — so for the ordinary one-variation job every event carries `done: 0, total: 100`, while the real percentage rides inside `label` as "Filming… 43%" (create.rs:864-870).
  _Problem:_ The row appends a fraction to a label that already contains the truth, and the two contradict each other in the same sentence: "Filming… 43% 0/100". The comment directly above it (1465-1468) states that this row never invents a number and exists to avoid the fake-progress problem — but a `0/100` glued to a live 43% is worse than an invented number, because it reads as the run having made no progress at all. This is the surface the Create page's own broken meter should be fixed towards, so it has to be right first.
  _Do:_ Drop the ` ${p.done}/${p.total}` suffix at Sidebar.tsx:1469, leaving `{p ? p.label : j.status === "queued" ? "Queued" : "Working…"}`. The label already carries the provider's percentage when it publishes one and states only a phase when it does not — which is what the comment above the line claims the row does.

- **[high·ux-defect]** A running or queued generation cannot be stopped from anywhere in Create
  `src/workspace/create/CreatePage.tsx:525`
  _Today:_ `Canvas`'s active-job cards (CreatePage.tsx:525-548) carry no control at all — only the failure card has a dismiss (513-521). `CreationsNav`'s "Making now" rows are static divs (Sidebar.tsx:1458-1474). Stop / Remove / Resume exist only on `JobRow` in the AI pane's Activity tab (AiPane.tsx:1050-1069), wired to `a.pauseJob` / `a.resumeJob` (studioActions.ts:82-88).
  _Problem:_ Pressing "Send all 21 — film them" in FilmReview (FilmReview.tsx:124-133) queues 21 separate paid `create` jobs (create.rs:1580-1597) that run one at a time. Realising at clip three that the cast has the wrong face, there is nothing on the page you are standing on that stops the other eighteen; you have to know the AI pane has an Activity tab and that background jobs live there. The most expensive action in the app is the one with no brake beside it.
  _Do:_ Give `CreationsNav`'s rows in Sidebar.tsx:1455-1474 the same two controls `JobRow` already renders: "Stop" (`a.pauseJob`) while `j.status === "running"`, "Remove" (also `a.pauseJob`) while `"queued"`, and a "Resume"/"Retry" (`a.resumeJob`) on the failed rows at 1477-1487. Both actions are already exported from `studioActions.ts` and already reach the sidebar through `a`; this renders them in the destination that started the work rather than adding anything new.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "Take it to Story" dead-ends when there is no shot list yet
  `src/workspace/create/StoryTab.tsx:1006`
  _Today:_ The bench's `WholeScriptNotice` offers "Take it to Story — {plan.parts} parts" (CreatePage.tsx:1038-1040), which sets `handoff` to the prompt and switches surface (301-302). `StoryTab` passes it to `ShotList` (StoryTab.tsx:172), but `ShotList` returns early on `board.lists.length === 0` with the "No shot list yet / Start one" empty state (1006-1026) — before `ScriptSplitter`, the only component that reads `handoff` (it seeds `open` and `script` from it at 644-645), is rendered at 1088-1097.
  _Problem:_ A first-time user is by definition in the no-list state. They paste a five-minute script, the bench correctly tells them it marks its own 21 chunks and offers to carry it over, they press the button — and land on a screen headed "No shot list yet" with no mention of their script. It is still in `CreatePage`'s `handoff` state and will reappear if they happen to press "Start one", but nothing on screen says so, and the reasonable reading is that the handoff dropped it.
  _Do:_ Thread `handoff` into the empty state at StoryTab.tsx:1006-1026 (it is already in scope — the prop is destructured at 929). When it is non-null the paragraph reads "Your script is waiting. A shot list is the order things happen in — one line per shot" and the button at 1019-1023 reads "Start a list from your script" instead of "Start one". No wiring changes: `onNewList` already reloads into a board where `ScriptSplitter` mounts open with the text.

- **[medium·consistency]** Studio rows never admit that a run is already going
  `src/workspace/StudioShelf.tsx:48`
  _Today:_ All three shelf rows render a static `<span className="studio-row-state">Create</span>` (StudioShelf.tsx:48, 61, 76) and are never disabled, while the AI-action chips directly below them are `disabled={s.aiBusy}` (90) and the "Summarize the room" row beneath renders `{working ? "Working…" : "Create"}` with `is-working nb-tape nb-sem-pending` and `disabled={s.files.length === 0 || working}` (AiPane.tsx:365, 378-380). Nothing on the studio path dedups: `openStudioPrompt` (studioActions.ts:102), `runStudio` (123) and `start_studio_job` (jobs.rs:1125) all start a fresh job unconditionally, unlike `startDeepSummary`, which explicitly refuses to duplicate (studioActions.ts:50-57). A studio run does surface elsewhere — `studio-running` at AiPane.tsx:344-357, driven by `s.studioStep.text`.
  _Problem:_ Press Flashcards, get a toast, and the row you pressed is unchanged. Press it again — nothing stops you — and a second identical background job is queued against the same material and billed for it. The state signal that does exist (`studio-running`) sits above the shelf and names a phase, not a row, so with two runs going it cannot say which row is busy. The one row on the shelf that reports its own state makes the other three read as broken by comparison.
  _Do:_ In StudioShelf, derive `const runningKinds = new Set(s.jobs.filter((j) => j.kind === "studio" && (j.status === "running" || j.status === "queued")).map((j) => (j.plan as { kind?: string } | null)?.kind))`. Each of the three rows then renders `runningKinds.has(kind) ? "Working…" : "Create"` in `studio-row-state`, carries `nb-tape nb-sem-pending` while working, and sets `disabled={runningKinds.has(kind)}` — the three signals AiPane.tsx:365-380 already uses. Nothing new appears on screen; the existing span changes word.

- **[medium·accessibility]** Three Create dialogs claim to be modal and behave like panels
  `src/workspace/create/PicturePicker.tsx:53`
  _Today:_ `PicturePicker` (53), `DocumentPicker` (54) and `FilmReview` (78) each declare `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"`. None registers any keydown listener; none of `cr-pick-scrim` / `cr-review-back` carries an `onClick`. The pickers' only dismissal is the ✕ (PicturePicker.tsx:65, DocumentPicker.tsx:66); FilmReview also has "Not yet" (121-123). StudioModal.tsx:14-28 + 38-41, CompareModal.tsx:33-45 + 56 + 62 and AiActionModal.tsx:15-33 + 41-46 + 53 all carry the same capture-phase Escape effect plus a closing backdrop. Focus management is absent from all six: the two prompt modals lean on `autoFocus` on a field, CompareModal focuses nothing, and none restores focus on close.
  _Problem:_ `aria-modal="true"` tells a screen reader that everything behind is inert, which is not true here — the page behind stays tabbable and reachable. Escape, the reflex on a Mac, does nothing in these three, and because the app-level Escape handler is still live underneath it can close the file behind the picker instead. FilmReview is the worst placement of the three: it is the sheet standing between the reader and twenty-one paid generations.
  _Do:_ Extract the effect the other three already carry into `useModalEscape(open, onClose)` in a new `src/workspace/modal.ts` and call it from all six, deleting three near-identical effect bodies. Add `onClick={onClose}` to `cr-pick-scrim` (PicturePicker.tsx:53, DocumentPicker.tsx:54) and `cr-review-back` (FilmReview.tsx:78) with `onClick={(e) => e.stopPropagation()}` on the inner `nb-panel`, matching `studio-prompt-backdrop`. Separately — and applying to all six, not just these — give each panel `tabIndex={-1}` and focus it on open.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** A pen stroke's label is accepted and never drawn; a line's lands in the corner
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1904`
  _Today:_ The footer's Label field appears for any single selection (SketchView.tsx:1856-1864) and `relabel` (1013-1019) writes `label` onto every non-text element. In `Drawn`, the shared `label` node is built at `x={0} y={0}` (1904-1909) and rendered only under `el.type === "line"` (1983). `rect`, `ellipse` and `arrow` each draw their own label at a sensible place (1921-1925, 1939-1943, 1966-1970). `pen` and `line` both fall to `default:` (1976-1977), which emits the stroke path and nothing else. `const box = bboxOf(el)` is already computed at 1979.
  _Problem:_ Two silent failures from one control. Name a pen stroke and the text appears in the object chip (`chipLabel`) and in the accessible description (`describeElement`, model.ts:331) but never on the drawing — the field takes your input and shows nothing. Name a line — which is what the agent draws most of when it lays out a diagram — and the label renders at canvas coordinates 0,0, the top-left of the page, potentially scrolled off screen away from the shape it belongs to.
  _Do:_ In `Drawn`, move `const box = bboxOf(el);` (currently 1979) above the `switch`, build the shared `label` node at `x={box.x + box.w / 2} y={box.y + box.h / 2 + 9}` instead of 0,0 (1904-1909), and change the render guard at 1983 from `el.type === "line" ? label : null` to `el.type === "line" || el.type === "pen" ? label : null`. Two edited lines close both holes.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Story list title and logline write on every keystroke and drop characters
  `src/workspace/create/StoryTab.tsx:1065`
  _Today:_ Both inputs are controlled by server state and call the mutation from `onChange`: `onUpdateList(current.id, e.target.value, current.logline)` (StoryTab.tsx:1065) and the logline equivalent (1072). `onUpdateList` routes through `run()` (80-92) → `setBusy(true)` → `await api.storyUpdateList` → `await load()`, which replaces `board` entirely, → `setBusy(false)`. Every button, select and checkbox in the panel takes `disabled={busy || …}` (1019, 1051, 1153, 1200, 1546). The shot row's own action field does the opposite and is right: local `useState` seeded from the shot, written on `onBlur` only when changed (1447-1455).
  _Problem:_ Typing a nine-character title fires nine database writes and nine full board reloads. `busy` flips on every one, so the panel's buttons and selects flicker disabled while you type. And because the input's `value` comes from `board`, a keystroke landing during a round trip is computed against the stale value and is lost — renaming a list is unreliable in exactly the way that is hardest to report as a bug.
  _Do:_ Give the title and logline local `useState` seeded from `current` and keyed on `current.id`, and call `onUpdateList` from `onBlur` only when the value actually changed — the same three lines `ShotRow` already uses at StoryTab.tsx:1447-1455. Nothing in the panel needs the board reloaded for a rename.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** The sketch empty state's third card promises a diagram and prints a sentence
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1751`
  _Today:_ The third empty-state card, titled "Turn room files into a diagram" with the copy "Ask the assistant, with this sketch open.", has `onClick={() => setNote("Ask the assistant to draw from your room's files.")}` (SketchView.tsx:1751-1758). `note` is rendered in the footer's save-status slot — `{note ?? saveWord}` at 1885, bottom-right, the same span that otherwise says "Saved" — and is cleared by the next `scheduleSave` (292). Its two siblings do real work: 1729-1738 arms the pen in sticky mode, 1740-1748 calls `startTemplate()`.
  _Problem:_ Three cards look identical; two act and one restates its own subtitle into the corner where the save indicator lives. A first-time user reads that as the button having failed, and if a save is scheduled a moment later the sentence disappears mid-read. It is also the only card of the three that leaves you no closer to the thing it names.
  _Do:_ Delete the card (SketchView.tsx:1750-1759) and its `setNote` call, and fold the fact into the footer hint at 1871-1873, which already reads "Pick a tool and draw — or ask the room's AI to draw it for you." Two working cards beat three where one is an announcement. Wiring it instead would mean widening `ViewerContext` (registry.tsx:78-104) with a composer handle for one card's sake, which is new shared surface for a hint.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** The tool that stays on has no way to be discovered
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1289`
  _Today:_ Sticky mode is armed only by `onDoubleClick` on a tool button (SketchView.tsx:1299-1303). The button's title is `` `${tl.hint}${tl.key === tool && sticky ? " · staying on" : ""}` `` (1289), so "staying on" is legible only once you have already discovered the gesture. The only visual is a 2px inset underline (`.sk-locked-tool`, sketch.css:377-379). Separately, the "Draw freely" empty-state card calls `setSticky(true)` (1733) with the copy "Use the pen and shapes to sketch ideas.", which does not mention it. Two exits already exist and are equally unmentioned: a single click on any tool (1294) and Escape (1108).
  _Problem:_ One shape then back to Select is the right default and the file argues for it well, but the escape hatch from it is a double-click nothing names. Meanwhile a first-timer who presses "Draw freely" is silently put INTO the hidden mode, watches the pen keep drawing, and has no word for what is happening or how to stop it — the inset underline is not something you notice unless you already know to look for it.
  _Do:_ Two copy changes, no new controls. Make the tool title unconditional: `` `${tl.hint} · double-click to keep it on` ``, becoming `` `${tl.hint} · staying on — click any tool or press Escape to stop` `` when `tl.key === tool && sticky` (SketchView.tsx:1289). And add a sticky branch to the footer hint (1869-1875): while `sticky` it reads "{Tool} stays on — Escape or another tool to stop", which is then also what "Draw freely" explains for free.

- **[medium·copy]** The Make button never says how many it is about to make
  `src/workspace/create/CreatePage.tsx:967`
  _Today:_ "How many" offers 1 / 2 / 4 (CreatePage.tsx:917-932) and `variations` is passed straight to `startCreateJob` (160). The button reads `{busy ? "Starting…" : sending > 0 ? "Send and make it" : "Make it"}` (967) — singular regardless of the setting. The seam paragraph above it (940-959) names the engine and counts attached pictures but never the multiplier. The number is first stated in the toast fired after the press: "Making 4 — they will open when they are ready." (174-175).
  _Problem:_ On a page whose every other spend control is careful about what a press costs — FilmReview counts the parts on the button itself (FilmReview.tsx:131-132), `cr-split-sum` spells out "21 pictures and 21 clips to pay for" (StoryTab.tsx:863-867) — the most-pressed button in Create understates itself by up to a factor of four. The multiplier is chosen in one control and disclosed only after the money is committed.
  _Do:_ Make the button state the quantity: `` `Make ${variations === 1 ? "it" : variations}` `` and `` `Send and make ${variations === 1 ? "it" : variations}` `` (CreatePage.tsx:967). Add the same count to the seam sentence at 946-950 when `variations > 1`: "…and this is {variations} separate calls."

- **[medium·accessibility]** The object strip puts one tab stop per shape between the canvas and the footer
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1806`
  _Today:_ The object strip is `role="listbox" aria-multiselectable="true"` (SketchView.tsx:1806-1811) containing one `<button role="option">` per element (1812-1850), each carrying `aria-selected`, `aria-posinset`, `aria-setsize` and a full `describeElement` accessible name. There is no `tabIndex` management and the row has no `onKeyDown` (the file's only element-level keydown is the canvas text input at 1776). Multi-select exists only through `ev.shiftKey` on a mouse click (1841-1849). `chipRefs` (208) is used for `scrollIntoView` (1167), never for focus.
  _Problem:_ The comment above the strip is right that this is "the one way to select a shape without being able to see where it is", which makes it precisely the surface a keyboard-only or screen-reader user depends on. On a forty-shape diagram, tabbing off the canvas means forty presses to reach the footer's Label field. And the listbox pattern a screen reader announces — arrow to move, space to toggle in a multi-selectable list — is not the pattern implemented, so the instructions it reads out are wrong.
  _Do:_ Give the row roving focus: on each chip, `tabIndex={selected.includes(e.id) || (selected.length === 0 && i === 0) ? 0 : -1}`; on `sk-objects-row`, an `onKeyDown` handling ArrowLeft/ArrowRight to move focus via the existing `chipRefs` map and set `selected` to that one id, and Space to toggle membership using the same shift-click branch already written at 1841-1849. One tab stop in, arrows within — which is what the role already promises.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Naming one shape fills the sketch's undo history with keystrokes
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1013`
  _Today:_ The footer's Label field writes on every keystroke: `onChange={(e) => relabel(chosen.id, e.target.value)}` (SketchView.tsx:1862), and `relabel` (1013-1019) calls `commit(...)` with no options, so `commit` takes the `opts.undoable !== false` branch and pushes a full document snapshot per character (304). `UNDO_DEPTH` is 80 and `pushHistory` slices to the last 80 (model.ts:968, 977-980); each entry is a whole `Sketch`. Text notes go through the same path.
  _Problem:_ Typing "Ingestion pipeline" into the Label field puts 18 whole-document snapshots on the undo stack and clears the redo stack 18 times. The toolbar's Undo — the one the footer hint at 1878-1881 carefully explains is the DRAWING's history, not the field's — now walks back through the label one character at a time, and on a busy page the structural edits the user actually wants to reach have been pushed past the 80-entry ceiling by a single rename. `ShotRow` in StoryTab.tsx:1447-1455 gets the same problem right with local state and `onBlur`.
  _Do:_ Keep the live redraw but make one naming session one undo step: call `relabel` with `commit(next, { undoable: false })` (the option already exists at SketchView.tsx:302-305), and add `onFocus={() => setHistory((h) => pushHistory(h, docRef.current))}` to the Label input at 1859-1863 so the pre-edit document is captured once when the field is entered.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The translate action focuses the prompt, not the language field it requires
  `src/workspace/AiActionModal.tsx:143`
  _Today:_ For a definition with `needsLanguage: true` and `needsQuestion: false` — which is exactly "translate" and only translate (apiTypes.ts:231-233) — AiActionModal renders the language `<input … autoFocus>` at 69-87 AND the prompt `<textarea … autoFocus={!def.needsQuestion}>` at 139-146, whose expression is `true` in that case. Two elements declare autoFocus in one mount; the textarea commits later and takes the focus. Meanwhile `questionMissing = (def.needsQuestion || def.needsLanguage) && !aiPrompt.question.trim()` (38-39) keeps the Run button disabled until that unfocused field is filled (229).
  _Problem:_ Open Translate and the caret lands in the prompt box. The Target-language field above it — the one control that must be filled before Run stops being greyed out — never receives focus, so the reader types into the prompt, reaches for Run, finds it disabled, and has nothing telling them which of the two fields is empty. `needsQuestion` (research) has the same shape and is handled correctly by the `!def.needsQuestion` guard; `needsLanguage` was simply left out of it.
  _Do:_ Change the textarea's guard at AiActionModal.tsx:143 to `autoFocus={!def.needsQuestion && !def.needsLanguage}`, so exactly one element declares autoFocus and it is the required field.

- **[medium·consistency]** The Summarize row says "Working…" about work it did not start
  `src/workspace/AiPane.tsx:311`
  _Today:_ `StudioView` computes `const jobRunning = s.jobs.some((j) => j.status === "running" || j.status === "queued");` (311-313) over EVERY job kind, then `const working = s.summaryStarting || jobRunning;` (314). That flag drives the Summarize row's `disabled={s.files.length === 0 || working}` (365), its `is-working nb-tape nb-sem-pending` marker (378) and its label `{working ? "Working…" : "Create"}` (380). The action behind it already refuses to duplicate on its own: `startDeepSummary` looks for an unfinished `j.kind === "deep_summary"` job and surfaces or resumes it instead of starting a second (studioActions.ts:50-57).
  _Problem:_ Queue a film run or a Flashcards deck and the "Summarize the room" row greys out and says "Working…" — about a job it did not start and cannot report on. It is the one row on the shelf whose state signal is load-bearing, so it is the one row where a false reading costs the most: the reader concludes a summary is in flight when none is, and waits. The dedup the row appears to be enforcing is already enforced correctly one layer down, keyed to the right job kind.
  _Do:_ Narrow the derivation at AiPane.tsx:311-313 to `s.jobs.some((j) => j.kind === "deep_summary" && (j.status === "running" || j.status === "queued"))`, matching the kind `startDeepSummary` itself checks (studioActions.ts:51) and the kind the Rust side creates (jobs.rs:863). `s.summaryStarting` stays as the optimistic pre-flight flag it was written to be.

- **[medium·accessibility]** Compare opens with the keyboard still behind the scrim
  `src/workspace/CompareModal.tsx:56`
  _Today:_ `CompareModal` renders `<div className="compare-backdrop" onClick={…}>` around `<div className="compare-modal" role="dialog" aria-modal="true" …>` (56-63), with a capture-phase Escape effect (33-45) and a closing backdrop. It contains no `autoFocus` and no focus call — the two `useEffect`s at 26-31 and 33-45 only reset view state and bind the key handler — and nothing restores focus when it closes at 104, 114 or via Escape. The dialog's own actions include an armed "Restore this version" (89-113), the destructive one.
  _Problem:_ Opening Compare from the History popover leaves the keyboard on the trigger button underneath the scrim. `aria-modal="true"` tells a screen reader that everything behind is inert while the focus is in fact still out there, so the first Tab walks the page under the overlay rather than reaching "Plain view", "Restore this version" or "Close". Closing it drops focus to the document body. This is the same gap as the three Create dialogs, in a modal the audit treated as the good example.
  _Do:_ Add `const panelRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null)` and `ref={panelRef}` plus `tabIndex={-1}` to `compare-modal` (CompareModal.tsx:57-63), then focus it from the existing open effect at 26-31 (`panelRef.current?.focus()`). Do the same in the shared `useModalEscape`/`PromptRunModal` extraction proposed for the other five modals so it is written once.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** Every cast portrait fetches the room's whole thumbnail list
  `src/workspace/create/StoryTab.tsx:524`
  _Today:_ `HeroFace` (StoryTab.tsx:524-539) runs `api.storyPictures()` in its own effect keyed on `[fileId]` and keeps one thumbnail from the result, so a cast of twelve issues twelve identical whole-room fetches on mount and again whenever a face changes. `Canvas` (CreatePage.tsx:487-501) and `FilmReview` (FilmReview.tsx:51-60) each independently build the exact `Record<fileId, thumbB64>` map `HeroFace` needs; `PicturePicker` fetches the list again on every open (PicturePicker.tsx:30-43). `HeroFace` returns `<span className="cr-hero-noface" aria-hidden />` (537) for both "not loaded yet" and "this hero genuinely has no picture".
  _Problem:_ On a room with a real cast the strip fills in raggedly, portrait by portrait, with the same base64 payload crossing the bridge a dozen times. Until each one lands the strip claims those people are faceless — which is the single fact the tab is built around, since a hero without a picture is the one that will not keep its face across shots. The reader cannot tell a slow load from a missing portrait.
  _Do:_ Fetch once in `StoryTab` alongside `load()`, hold `thumbs: Record<string, string> | null`, and pass it down to `CastStrip` → `HeroFace` (which becomes the pure `({ thumb }: { thumb?: string })`) and to `FilmReview` as a prop in place of its own effect (FilmReview.tsx:51-60). While the map is null, `HeroFace` renders a neutral placeholder that is not `cr-hero-noface`, so "loading" and "no picture" stop sharing one appearance.

- **[low·test-gap]** No test holds the Create page to the app's own progress-honesty rule
  `e2e/page-script/createPage.test.mjs:69`
  _Today:_ `createPage.test.mjs` asserts the capability gate and the rail wiring thoroughly — 15 tests over `visibleModels`, `selectedModel`, `tallies`, `emptyReason`, `emptyShelfLine`, `legalSeconds`, `takesFirstFrame` and the Rust command registrations, several of them already source assertions read through the `read` helper at line 35. `jobProgress.test.mjs` covers `jobMeter` in isolation. Nothing asserts that the surfaces which DRAW job state actually call it, and `jobMeter`'s only production caller is AiPane.tsx:945 — which is how CreatePage.tsx:527 came to compute its own percentage.
  _Problem:_ The rule exists, is documented at length in jobProgress.ts:1-16, is tested as a pure function, and is then bypassed on the one page where a paid, minutes-long job is watched. A pure-function test cannot catch a caller that never calls it.
  _Do:_ Add a source assertion to createPage.test.mjs in the file's existing style: `read("src/workspace/create/CreatePage.tsx")` and assert it contains no `role="progressbar"` and no `Math.round(live.` — i.e. the running-job card states the job's own label and nothing it computed itself. Pair it with the same assertion over `Sidebar.tsx`'s CreationsNav once that row stops printing `${p.done}/${p.total}` (see the separate finding).


### Every viewer, judged as one family (23)


- **[high·ux-defect]** A .txt and a PDF each draw two reading-progress strokes
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:1172`
  _Today:_ There are two complete implementations of reading progress with the same hook name: `useReadingProgress`+`ReadingProgress`+`.doc-progress` (ReaderShell.tsx:88-139, viewer-formats.css:64-88) and `useReadingProgress`+`.rdr-progress` (ProseView.tsx:76-117, viewer.css:279-311). ViewerPane.tsx:483 calls the first and 1172 draws it for every kind in READER_KINDS, which includes 'prose' and 'pdf' — and ProseView.tsx:43-51 and PdfView.tsx:876-885 each draw the second for themselves. A plain-text file and a PDF both paint two 3px accent strokes at the top of the pane, from two separately-measured numbers.
  _Problem:_ Two hooks with the same name in two files, two CSS classes, and on two of the commonest reading formats they are visibly stacked. On a PDF they can also disagree: PdfView's stroke is `curPage/numPages` (PdfView.tsx:881), ReaderShell's is scroll offset (ReaderShell.tsx:98-99), so at the top of a tall final page they read differently.
  _Do:_ Delete ReaderShell's `useReadingProgress` (88-121), `ReadingProgress` (131-139) and the `.doc-progress`/`.doc-progress-ink` rules (viewer-formats.css:64-88); drop the `reading` call at ViewerPane.tsx:483, the render at 1172 and the `ref={reading.ref}` at 1185. Keep ProseView's hook as the single implementation — it walks up to the real scroller (ProseView.tsx:120-127), so it is correct whether the viewer owns the scroll or `.viewer-body` does — and call it from MarkdownView, TextView, EmailView and DocxView, the four reader kinds that would otherwise lose the stroke. One hook, one class, ~60 fewer lines.

- **[high·accessibility]** A Hebrew note reads correctly in chat and backwards as a file
  `src/viewers/MarkdownView.tsx:93`
  _Today:_ ProseView (53), TextView (21), EmailView (22, 38, 54), SubtitleView (83, 94), SlidesView (206) and the Text readings of Html/Book/OfficeDoc (HtmlView.tsx:170, BookView.tsx:242, OfficeDocView.tsx:111) all carry `dir="auto"`. MarkdownView does not: `<div className="md-body">` at line 93 sets no direction and markdown.css declares none. JsonView (`.json-leaf` at 29, `.json-key` at 51 and 71), LogView's `.log-text` (106), ArchiveView's `.zip-name` (58, 72) and CloudView's `.cloudview-text` payload preview (117) are the same. chat.css:426 sets `unicode-bidi: plaintext` on `.msg-content`, but that property is not inherited, so the `<p>`/`<li>` blocks ReactMarkdown generates inside it do not get it.
  _Problem:_ MarkdownView renders both .md files and every AI answer (viewer.css:133 says so in as many words; ChatPane.tsx:623 and 950 are the calls), so this is the app's most-used renderer. A Hebrew or Arabic note lays out left-to-right: final punctuation lands at the wrong end, list bullets sit on the wrong side, mixed Hebrew/English lines scramble. The Cloud-payload preview is worse in kind — it exists to be believed about what leaves the Mac, and it shows that text in the wrong direction.
  _Do:_ One rule in markdown.css: `.md-body :is(p, li, h1, h2, h3, h4, h5, h6, td, th, blockquote, dd) { unicode-bidi: plaintext; }` — fixes the file view and the chat bubble together, per block, without guessing a document-level direction. Add `dir="auto"` to `.json-key` at BOTH JsonView.tsx:51 and 71 and to `.json-leaf` at 29, to `.log-text` (LogView.tsx:106), `.zip-name` (ArchiveView.tsx:58, 72) and `.cloudview-text` (CloudView.tsx:117).

- **[high·honesty-defect]** A slide that is not being drawn says "Drawing slide…" forever
  `src/viewers/SlidesView.tsx:200`
  _Today:_ The stage's fallback is `{renderError || (rendering ? "Drawing slide…" : "Drawing slide…")}` (SlidesView.tsx:200) — both branches of the ternary are the same string. So whenever `current` is undefined and no error was captured, the pane claims a render is in progress whether or not one is: after a `slidePreview` call that resolved with no image, after the effect's cleanup ran on a fast Next/Previous, and on any slide the prefetch never reached.
  _Problem:_ This is the app's own rule broken in one line: a viewer stating that work is happening when it can see that it is not. It reads exactly like the fabricated progress bar the house rules forbid, and because the two branches are identical the mistake is invisible to review — the ternary looks like it distinguishes something.
  _Do:_ Replace with two real branches: `rendering ? "Drawing slide…" : "This slide hasn't been drawn yet."` and give the second one a retry that re-runs the `api.slidePreview(fileId, at)` call at SlidesView.tsx:92-101. If a resolved-but-empty preview is a distinguishable case, say so instead: 'macOS returned no picture for this slide.'

- **[high·ux-defect]** Waiting, failing and empty are all the same dim grey line
  `src/viewers/DocxView.tsx:56`
  _Today:_ Waiting, failing and empty share one class. `.empty-hint` carries all three in 38 places across src/viewers/: 'Opening book…' (BookView.tsx:136), 'This book could not be read: …' (BookView.tsx:138, string set at BookView.tsx:61/74) and 'This archive is empty.' (ArchiveView.tsx:139) all render as muted `var(--fs-meta)` text — 13px default, 12px in compact density — with no icon and no role. DocxView.tsx:56 puts a render failure in `.viewer-status`, the same class SlidesView.tsx:172 uses for 'Slide 3 of 12'. Four viewers do mark a failure with role="alert" — PdfView.tsx:852/859, HtmlView.tsx:141, RoomMap.tsx:422, RecordingView.tsx:1915/1933 — and each does it with its own bespoke class. The waits alone are `.viewer-opening` (ViewerPane.tsx:576), Suspense's 'Loading viewer…' (ViewerRouter.tsx:99), `.viewer-status` 'Loading PDF…' (PdfView.tsx:857), `.empty-hint` 'Opening book…', `.rec-blank` 'Reading this recording…' (RecordingView.tsx:370) and `.cloudview-empty` (CloudView.tsx:72).
  _Problem:_ A user cannot tell a stall from a failure. Waiting on a 200MB PDF and a .docx whose render threw look the same, so the honest response to both is to keep waiting — under-honesty of exactly the kind this app is otherwise careful about. And the class carrying most viewers' failure text is defined in sidebar-search.css under the heading 'LIBRARY FILE ROWS', at the metadata font floor, under a comment that says instructions must never be set small.
  _Do:_ Add `src/viewers/ViewerState.tsx` with `kind: "waiting" | "failed" | "empty"`. `failed` gets role="alert" and the `nb-sem-urgent` marker tokens from paper.css:347; `waiting` keeps the muted line; `empty` renders at `--fs-lead` (what sidebar-search.css:213 already asks for and no viewer honours). Replace the 38 `.empty-hint` uses in src/viewers/ and the error-carrying `.viewer-status` uses (DocxView.tsx:56, Mermaid.tsx:78, Waveform.tsx:539, JsonView.tsx:129), and fold `.cloudview-empty` (viewer.css:936), `.pdf-failed`, `.room-map-error` and `.gate-error` into the one component. Leave `.rec-blank` alone — it is a distinct three-line waiting screen, not a duplicate.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The one format made of quotable speech cannot be quoted
  `src/workspace/quoteSelection.ts:92`
  _Today:_ QUOTABLE_KINDS (quoteSelection.ts:92-97) covers docx, pdf, prose, email, text, markdown, html, book, worddoc. 'audio', 'video' and 'recording' are absent, with the comment at 87-90 explaining that a video's selection 'means something else'. But AudioView renders a timestamped transcript (AudioView.tsx:554-571) where every row with a timestamp is a `<button>` whose whole job is seeking, so a drag across two sentences is fighting a click target as well as being refused a quote button.
  _Problem:_ 'He said X at 4:12 — what do you make of it' is the whole reason someone imports a meeting recording into a room with an assistant in it. Today the only route is Copy all text (which copies the entire transcript, provenance line included) or retyping. Meanwhile a Word document, which nobody is trying to point at mid-sentence, has the gesture.
  _Do:_ Add 'audio' and 'video' to QUOTABLE_KINDS. In AudioView.tsx:559-570 shrink the click target: make `.audio-stamp` the `<button title="Jump to this moment">` and leave the speaker and text as sibling spans, so dragging across the words selects rather than seeks. `withQuote` already stamps the file name, and the timestamp is already in the text, so a transcript quote lands with its source. Update quoteSelection.test.mjs:122 ('formats whose selection means something else are left alone'), which currently asserts audio and video are refused. Leave 'recording' out for now — RecordingView's rows are editable fields, a different fix.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Find-in-document exists in exactly one viewer
  `src/viewers/PdfView.tsx:808`
  _Today:_ PdfView claims ⌘F on the capture phase while it is hovered or focused (PdfView.tsx:808-835) and draws a real find bar with a live count and ↑/↓ stepping (954-1010). Monaco brings its own find to CodeEditor (CodeEditor.tsx:282, `actions.find`). LogView.tsx:69 has a filter box, which is a different act — it removes lines rather than finding one. Everywhere else — a 300-page epub in BookView, a .docx in DocxView, a saved article in HtmlView, a .txt in ProseView, a note in MarkdownView, a message in EmailView, extracted text in TextView — ⌘F falls through to effects.ts:894, which opens the room-wide search overlay.
  _Problem:_ 'Where does it say that' is the commonest thing a reader does inside a long document, and in this family it works on PDFs and code only. The room-wide search answers a different question (which file), so the reader gets a list of files while looking at the one file they meant.
  _Do:_ Lift PdfView's bar into `src/viewers/FindInDocument.tsx` — same capture-phase ⌘F claim, same count readout — over the DOM-rendered readers: ProseView, MarkdownView, TextView, EmailView, JsonView's tree, and the Text readings of HtmlView/BookView/OfficeDocView. Do NOT claim it over a `roomdoc://` frame: the app cannot read a match position out of an opaque origin, which is the same limit BookView already states at BookView.tsx:198-201 about its own progress stroke. In Page mode, ⌘F should switch the `.rdr-modes` group to Text and search there, with the pressed mode button showing it moved.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Zoom into a picture and the zoom controls scroll away
  `src/styles/viewer-formats.css:559`
  _Today:_ `.pdf-zoombar` is `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 5` (settings-annotations.css:71-77). ImageView reuses that class at ImageView.tsx:357 and then cancels it: `.img-zoombar { position: static; margin-bottom: 0 }` (viewer-formats.css:559-562). 'image' is also absent from FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS, so `.img-scroll { flex: 1; min-height: 0; overflow: auto }` (548-552) never receives a definite height and the scrolling happens in `.viewer-body` instead — carrying the bar off the top. PdfView additionally binds ⌘+ / ⌘− / ⌘0 (PdfView.tsx:808-826) and says so in its button titles (894, 906, 914); ImageView binds no keys at all and its titles name none.
  _Problem:_ Zooming a scan to 400% to read a stamp is the exact moment you need the zoom control, and it is the moment it leaves the screen. Two viewers with the same pill, the same glyphs and the same job behave differently for no reason a user could infer.
  _Do:_ Delete the `position: static` override at viewer-formats.css:560 so the bar inherits sticky, give the image row `fill: true` per the registry item above (or add 'image' to FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS), and extract PdfView's ⌘+/⌘−/⌘0 handler into `src/viewers/useZoomKeys.ts` called by both. Update ImageView.tsx:365/379/389 titles to name the keys, matching PdfView.tsx:894/906/914.

- **[medium·consistency]** Four different controls that all mean "show me the raw form"
  `src/viewers/SvgView.tsx:29`
  _Today:_ HtmlView (111-124, modes defined at 38-42: Page / Text / Source), OfficeDocView (74-93: Page / Text) and BookView (157-176: Page / Text) use `.rdr-modes` — aria-pressed toggle buttons with the marker underline, labelled by state. JsonView.tsx:152-160 uses an `.nb-btn` labelled by destination ('Raw' takes you to raw, 'Tree' takes you back), explicitly justified at 147-150 as 'which is why it is a button and not an aria-pressed toggle'. SvgView.tsx:29-31 uses a `.subtle` link labelled by destination (Source / Picture), plus a second `.subtle` for the backdrop (33-40) with no pressed state. NotebookView, SubtitleView and MarkdownView offer no source reading at all.
  _Problem:_ The same act — flip this document to its underlying form — is drawn three ways and reasoned about in two contradictory ways in comments four files apart. A reader who learns the Page/Text/Source strip on a saved article gets a differently-shaped, differently-labelled control on a JSON file and a third one on an SVG.
  _Do:_ Standardise on `.rdr-modes` with aria-pressed. JsonView becomes Tree | Raw (delete the comment at 147-150 and the inline `style={{ marginLeft: "auto" }}` at 155); SvgView becomes Picture | Source, and its backdrop toggle moves to `.rdr-bar-end` with aria-pressed, since it is a viewing condition and not a reading. Delete `.svg-bar`'s and `.json-bar`'s bespoke button styling.

- **[medium·view-quality]** The words OCR read off a picture are rendered as source code
  `src/styles/viewer-formats.css:627`
  _Today:_ ImageView.tsx:462 renders the recognised text as `<pre className="img-ocr-text" dir="auto">`. `.img-ocr-text` (viewer-formats.css:627-636) sets no font-family, so `.viewer-body pre { font-family: var(--mono) }` (viewer.css:114-121) applies. Its `font-size: 13px` (line 633) is a single-class (0,1,0) rule losing to `.viewer-body pre`'s (0,1,1) `font-size: var(--fs-meta)` — dead in the default theme and actively wrong in compact density, where --fs-meta drops to 12px (tokens.css:460).
  _Problem:_ This is the same specificity trap written up at viewer.css:108-113 and email.css:90-93 ('every saved message has in fact been rendering in 13px monospace'), still live one file over. OCR output is a document's prose — a scanned letter, a receipt, a Hebrew page — drawn in the face reserved for wire formats, at a size the file thinks it controls and does not.
  _Do:_ Rewrite as `.image-view .img-ocr-text { font-family: var(--sans); font-size: var(--fs-body); line-height: 1.6; }` at two classes, and replace the hardcoded 12px/13px in `.img-ocr-note` (624) and `.img-ocr > summary` (615) with tokens. While there, check the remaining single-class `<pre>` rules in src/viewers/*.css against `.viewer-body pre` — `.svg-source` (svg.css:52) is the same shape and survives only because it happens to want mono.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Copy all text pastes a model instruction into the clipboard
  `src/workspace/chatActions.ts:961`
  _Today:_ `copyAllText` (chatActions.ts:961-968) writes `s.openFile?.content.text` to the clipboard verbatim. For a picture that string begins with OCR_PREFIX — the literal `(text recognized from scan)` stamped on by `run_ocr_job` (util.ts:23) so the model knows the words are a machine reading. ImageView strips it with `ocrBody` (util.ts:34) precisely because, in that file's words, it is 'an instruction to the model, not something to put on screen under someone's photograph.' A recording or imported media file carries a '(transcribed from recording)' first line for the same reason (stt_cmds.rs:316). The menu item (ViewerPane.tsx:904-916) is offered for any file whose `content.text` is non-empty.
  _Problem:_ The one place these provenance prefixes must not appear is a person's clipboard, and that is the only place they still do. The same call also hands back raw markup for an .html file whose reader is showing the Text mode (registry.tsx:248 passes `c.text` as `source`), and a full SRT with cue numbers and `-->` timecodes for a subtitle file (registry.tsx:282).
  _Do:_ Route `copyAllText` through the reader-facing text rather than the stored text: `ocrBody()` for image, the transcript body minus the provenance line for audio/video/recording, `textOf()` for html, the cue text for subtitle, `content.text` otherwise. Put that mapping on the registry row as `copyText?: (c: FileContent) => string` so it sits beside `render`, and rename the item 'Copy the text' — it is not 'all' of anything for a picture.

- **[medium·view-quality]** The one infinitely scalable format is the one you cannot zoom
  `src/viewers/svg.css:48`
  _Today:_ `.svg-stage img { max-width: 100%; max-height: 100% }` (svg.css:48-51) — an SVG is fitted to the pane and that is the only size it has; SvgView.tsx binds no zoom state and no keys. ImageView, which shows rasters that lose information when scaled, has 25%–400% zoom (ImageView.tsx:19-21), Actual size, Fit (386-399) and drag-to-pan (398-404). SvgView also has a dark-backdrop toggle (33-40) whose title says 'Most diagrams are drawn in black on nothing'.
  _Problem:_ A dense architecture diagram or a floor plan opens at pane size and stays there, on the one format that could be enlarged without losing a single pixel of fidelity.
  _Do:_ Give SvgView the same zoom pill: reuse the `.pdf-zoombar` markup and set `width: calc(natural * zoom)` on the stage img, with `useZoomKeys` (extracted from PdfView per the image item) covering ⌘+/⌘−/⌘0 for all three viewers. Leave the backdrop toggle where it is — it belongs to a format that is usually black-on-nothing, and adding a second one to ImageView is new surface with nothing in the tree suggesting anyone needed it.

- **[medium·copy]** Two buttons labelled Find, in the same family, doing unrelated things
  `src/viewers/ImageView.tsx:285`
  _Today:_ ImageView's `.locate-bar` submit button is labelled 'Find' with a crosshair glyph (ImageView.tsx:261-286) and asks a vision model to draw boxes on the picture. PdfView's toolbar button is labelled 'Find' (949) and searches the document's text. The image bar is always-on: a prompt field sits above the picture on every image opened, whether or not any model in the room can see. The download-offer card below it (297-351) carries `className="vision-offer"` — a class with no rule in any stylesheet — and is styled entirely by an inline object with hardcoded pixel geometry (gap 10, padding '8px 12px', borderRadius 8), which the compact density setting (tokens.css:447) cannot reach.
  _Problem:_ One word, two acts, one app — and the AI prompt outranks the picture in the layout of a picture viewer. A person opening a photo gets a text field, possibly a model-download card, and their photo third.
