  _Do:_ Rename the image action 'Mark' — the verb that component already uses in its own copy ('Nothing here can mark images yet', ImageView.tsx:319) — and collapse the bar to a single `.rdr-bar` button 'Mark something…' that reveals the field, so the picture leads. Move the vision-helper offer into that revealed state: it is an answer to pressing Mark, not a standing notice. Replace its inline style object with `.rdr-note` (viewer.css:252-266), which is exactly this — a compact technical notice in the chrome — and drop the dead `vision-offer` class name.

- **[medium·test-gap]** Nothing fails the build when a new format falls out of the fill, reader or quotable set
  `e2e/page-script/registry.test.mjs:46`
  _Today:_ registry.test.mjs reads formats.rs, registry.tsx and apiTypes.ts and asserts they name the same kinds — the file's own preamble calls this 'THE DRIFT TEST for the format registry.' It parses only the top-level keys of FORMATS (`tsxKinds`, 46-56). FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS, READER_KINDS and QUOTABLE_KINDS are read by no test at all: grep finds FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS only in ViewerPane.tsx and a comment in viewer.css, and quoteSelection.test.mjs:28 textually extracts READER_KINDS purely to build a harness, asserting only that 'markdown' is in it (line 34).
  _Problem:_ Five viewers (sketch, recording, audio, video, image) are already outside a set whose absence has a visible layout cost, and the suite is green. The one guard that exists guards the one table that is hardest to forget.
  _Do:_ Once the three sets are fields on FormatEntry (see the registry item), extend `tsxKinds` to parse `fill`/`reader`/`quotable` per row and assert every kind states each explicitly — no defaults. Until then, add a second assertion to registry.test.mjs that every kind in FORMATS appears either in FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS or in an explicit `NOT_FILL` list in ViewerPane, which forces the decision to be written down where the reviewer can see it.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** One file open shows three different waiting messages from three components
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:576`
  _Today:_ Opening a book runs three waits in series, each owned by a different file. ViewerPane.tsx:573-579 draws the `.viewer-opening` pill — `position: absolute`, centred at the top of the pane (viewer.css:982-994) — reading 'Opening…' while `s.openingFileId` differs from the current file. Then ViewerRouter.tsx:99 draws Suspense's `<div className="empty-hint">Loading viewer…</div>` while the lazy chunk loads. Then BookView.tsx:136 draws `<div className="empty-hint">Opening book…</div>` while the bytes arrive.
  _Problem:_ Three strings, three type treatments, three positions, for one act the user thinks of as 'open the file'. The middle one is also the least meaningful thing on the list — 'Loading viewer…' names an implementation detail (a JS chunk) that the reader has no model of, and it appears only on the first open of each format, so the same file opens differently the second time.
  _Do:_ Delete the Suspense fallback string: pass `fallback={null}` at ViewerRouter.tsx:99 and let the `.viewer-opening` pill, which is already on screen and already positioned, cover the whole open. The viewer's own first line ('Opening book…') then follows it as the only in-pane wait. Two states instead of three, one fewer string to keep consistent across 18 viewers.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Where the mouse happens to be decides what ⌘F does
  `src/viewers/PdfView.tsx:812`
  _Today:_ PdfView's capture-phase key handler treats the viewer as active if `hoverRef.current || root.contains(document.activeElement)` (PdfView.tsx:812-814), where `hoverRef` is set by onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave on the root (843-849). effects.ts:894-901 opens the room-wide search on ⌘F but stands down whenever the event is already `defaultPrevented`, and PdfView calls preventDefault before opening its own bar (823-825).
  _Problem:_ Keyboard routing decided by pointer position. With a PDF open and the pointer resting anywhere over it — which is where the pointer sits after you scrolled — ⌘F typed while the caret is in the chat composer opens the PDF's find bar and silently suppresses the room-wide search. The same handler makes ⌘+ and ⌘0 zoom the PDF while you are typing a message. Nothing tells the user which of the two ⌘F meanings they are about to get.
  _Do:_ Drop `hoverRef` and gate on focus alone: `const active = root ? root.contains(document.activeElement) : false`. Delete the ref, the onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave handlers (843-849) and the two assignments — the viewer root is already `tabIndex`-reachable via its scroller, and clicking a page focuses it. Keyboard shortcuts should follow the caret, which is the rule every other ⌘ binding in effects.ts already follows.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The cloud-payload view is full-height or not depending on which file you opened it from
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:1180`
  _Today:_ `.cloudview` asks for `display:flex; flex-direction:column; height:100%; min-height:0` (viewer.css:843-848). But the `fill` class on `.viewer-body` is computed from the underlying file's kind — `FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS.has(openFile.content.kind)` at ViewerPane.tsx:1180 — and the cloud view is swapped in at 1194 without touching that decision. So the same privacy screen gets a definite height over a .json or .csv (both in the fill set) and resolves to auto over a .md, .txt, .pdf or .docx (none of them are), where `.cloudview-head`, the payload `<pre>` and the badge lay out against content height inside a padded scroller.
  _Problem:_ This is the screen whose whole job is to be believed about what would leave the Mac, and its layout is decided by a set that knows nothing about it. The header and badge that say 'Protected cloud payload' / 'Raw cloud payload' (CloudView.tsx:79-82) are the part that most needs to stay pinned while the payload scrolls.
  _Do:_ At ViewerPane.tsx:1180, make the class depend on the view actually being rendered: `cloudView || FILL_HEIGHT_KINDS.has(openFile.content.kind) || …`. One condition, and the cloud view stops inheriting a decision about a file it is not showing.

- **[medium·honesty-defect]** The subtitle editor says "Saved" about a file nobody has saved, and its save failure is silent to a screen reader
  `src/viewers/SubtitleView.tsx:68`
  _Today:_ SubtitleView's save button renders `{saving ? "Saving…" : dirty ? "Save" : "Saved"}` (SubtitleView.tsx:68). On a freshly opened .srt, `dirty` is false and nothing has been written, so the button reads 'Saved'. The save failure two lines down is `{error && <div className="gate-error">{error}</div>}` (71) — the same class HtmlView.tsx:141 uses, but without the `role="alert"` HtmlView gives it.
  _Problem:_ 'Saved' is a claim about an act that has not happened — the app asserting completed work on open, which is the one thing its honesty rule singles out. And when a real save does fail, the message appears in a div with no role, so a screen-reader user gets no announcement and the button has already flipped back to 'Saved'.
  _Do:_ Make the idle label 'Save' (disabled) and show 'Saved' only after `save()` resolves, clearing it on the next edit — the state already exists in `dirty`/`saving`, it needs a third `justSaved` flag or simply no third label at all. Add `role="alert"` to line 71 to match HtmlView.tsx:141.

- **[medium·accessibility]** An SVG document is marked decorative and never named
  `src/viewers/SvgView.tsx:47`
  _Today:_ SvgView renders the entire document as `<img src={src} alt="" />` (SvgView.tsx:47). The registry row passes only the text — `render: ({ content: c }) => <SvgView text={c.text ?? ""} />` (registry.tsx:250-254) — so the component has no name to use even if it wanted one. ImageView, by contrast, receives `name` and sets `alt={name}` (ImageView.tsx:418), and SlidesView sets `alt={outline[at]?.title ?? \`Slide ${at + 1}\`}` (SlidesView.tsx:197).
  _Problem:_ `alt=""` means 'this image carries no information' — asserted about the file that IS the document. Unlike QuickLookView, which at least pairs its empty alt with a figcaption (QuickLookView.tsx:64-68), there is no accompanying text at all: a screen-reader user lands in a viewer whose main region is empty, with only a Source button to prove anything is there.
  _Do:_ Pass `name={c.name}` from registry.tsx:253 and set `alt={name}` on SvgView.tsx:47, matching ImageView.tsx:418. One prop, one attribute; no claim is made about the drawing's content beyond the file name, which is what the other two viewers already do.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** Only the e-book reader can change text size
  `src/viewers/BookView.tsx:177`
  _Today:_ BookView has an A− / A+ pair stepping five sizes (FONT_STEPS at BookView.tsx:10, buttons at 177-193). PdfView has a zoom pill. Every other reader is fixed: ProseView, MarkdownView, TextView, EmailView, DocxView, OfficeDocView and the Text readings render at `var(--fs-lead)` (viewer-formats.css:439 for `.text-extract`, and the per-element rules for the rest) with no control. The one app-wide lever is the Interface density setting (InterfaceSection.tsx:82), which moves --fs-lead 16px→15px in compact (tokens.css:457) — a density preference for the whole shell, not a reading size for a document.
  _Problem:_ Text size is the control readers reach for first, and here it is a per-viewer control on the format most people open least. A long .txt or a dense .docx at 16px on a 27" display has no answer except the global density setting, which only makes everything smaller.
  _Do:_ Move A−/A+ out of BookView into the shared `.rdr-bar` as `<TextSize>`, writing `--doc-scale` on `.viewer-body.is-reader`, and multiply by it wherever a document sheet sets its size — viewer-formats.css:439 (`.viewer-body .text-extract`), the `.md-body`/`.prose-view`/`.eml-body` font-size rules — leaving the shell alone so it does not fight the density setting. BookView keeps no second control: its chapter is staged markup with the size baked in by `chapterHtml` (BookView.tsx:93), so pass the same scale into that call.

- **[medium·view-quality]** A deck's slide rail promises thumbnails and draws a numbered list
  `src/viewers/SlidesView.tsx:209`
  _Today:_ `.sl-rail` is `role="tablist"` (SlidesView.tsx:209) with one `role="tab"` per slide (211-223); each `.sl-thumb` contains `.sl-thumb-n` (the number) and `.sl-thumb-t` (the first line of text) and nothing else — even though `images[i]` already holds a rendered PNG for every slide visited and the prefetch effect (108-121) fetches the next one on a 350ms timer. The tablist has no arrow-key handling anywhere in the file and there is no `role="tabpanel"` on the stage.
  _Problem:_ Scanning a deck is a visual act — you find the slide with the chart on it. A rail of numbers and truncated titles cannot do that, so the only way through a 40-slide deck is Next 40 times. And a tablist that does not answer arrow keys is a promise to a screen-reader user that the app does not keep.
  _Do:_ Render `images[i]` as the thumb when it is cached and the numbered placeholder when it is not (honest: an un-rendered slide has no picture yet). Do NOT walk the whole deck in the background — each `slidePreview` is an IPC round trip and a full page render, so bound the prefetch to the rail's visible window plus one either side, at the existing 350ms cadence. Either implement arrow-key roving on the rail or drop role="tablist"/"tab" for a plain `<nav>` with `aria-current`, which SheetView.tsx:525-535 already chose for this exact reason and documents.

- **[medium·view-quality]** An HTML-only notebook output renders as the words "[HTML output — not rendered]"
  `src/viewers/NotebookView.tsx:69`
  _Today:_ `Output` prefers png, jpeg, svg, then any text/plain repr; if only `text/html` exists it returns `<pre className="nb-out nb-muted">[HTML output — not rendered]</pre>` (NotebookView.tsx:69-71). That is the shape of a plotly figure, an ipywidget and a styled DataFrame (`df.style`), none of which ship a text/plain twin. The comment points at the SVG note above it (55-57): untrusted markup must not be injected.
  _Problem:_ The refusal is right and the outcome is still a hole. This app already has the machinery to render untrusted markup safely and uses it three times — `api.stagePreviewHtml` (api.ts:328) plus a `roomdoc://` frame with `sandbox=""` and `default-src 'none'`, in HtmlView.tsx:69, BookView.tsx:93 and OfficeDocView.tsx:55. The notebook viewer is the one place that reaches for a placeholder instead.
  _Do:_ Render `text/html` outputs through the same staged frame OfficeDocView uses (OfficeDocView.tsx:94-104): `sandbox=""`, no allow-scripts, height sized to content with a max. Stage lazily — one call when the cell scrolls into view, not one per output on mount, or a notebook with thirty styled tables stages thirty documents. Keep the `[HTML output]` line for the case where staging fails and say why in that line. A plotly figure stays a static picture, which is the honest limit of a scriptless frame — worth saying in the caption rather than by omission.

- **[low·consistency]** The empty-state rule contradicts the comment directly above it
  `src/styles/sidebar-search.css:215`
  _Today:_ sidebar-search.css:213-214 says of `.empty-hint`: 'Never set small: it is the only thing on screen and it is usually an instruction.' The rule at 215-220 then sets `font-size: var(--fs-meta)` — 13px, which tokens.css:271 labels 'metadata floor', and which drops to 12px under compact density (tokens.css:460). Thirty-eight empty, waiting and failure states across src/viewers/ inherit it, including multi-sentence instructions like AudioView.tsx:596-600 and NotebookView.tsx:96-101.
  _Problem:_ The file states the rule and breaks it in the next declaration, so every viewer's only-thing-on-screen text is set at the size reserved for metadata. Where that text is a paragraph of instructions ('Converting it to a common format (M4A, MP3, WAV or MP4) and importing it again usually fixes this'), it is a page of 13px muted grey in the middle of an empty pane.
  _Do:_ If the ViewerState component is not built, the one-line version is `.viewer-body .empty-hint { font-size: var(--fs-body); }` in viewer.css — two classes, so it beats the sidebar rule without changing the sidebar's own file rows, where 13px is genuinely metadata. Either way the comment at 213-214 and the rule under it must stop disagreeing.

- **[low·accessibility]** role="status" is on the captions and missing from the readouts that actually change
  `src/viewers/ImageView.tsx:356`
  _Today:_ `.viewer-status` is used both for text that changes as work happens and for static captions, and the live-region role does not follow that line. It is present on SheetView.tsx:548/574, Waveform.tsx:539/544 and PdfView.tsx:1036 (the scan strip), and absent from the two genuinely changing readouts — ImageView.tsx:356 `{status && <div className="viewer-status">{status}</div>}` and PdfView.tsx:1054, both of which carry the running result of a model call. Meanwhile the class also dresses things that never change during a session: QuickLookView.tsx:64 (a figcaption), SubtitleView.tsx:63 (the cue count) and SlidesView.tsx:172 ('Slide 3 of 12', explicitly described at 169-171 as 'a count pencilled beside the deck').
  _Problem:_ A screen-reader user is announced the waveform's caption and the sheet's read-only note, and is told nothing when the vision model finishes marking a picture or a PDF text scan reports back — the two places where the string exists precisely because something changed while they were not looking.
  _Do:_ Apply one rule and write it in viewer.css beside the `.viewer-status` declaration (809): role="status" exactly where the string is replaced as a result of work. Add it to ImageView.tsx:356 and PdfView.tsx:1054; leave QuickLookView.tsx:64, SubtitleView.tsx:63 and SlidesView.tsx:172 as plain captions, which is what they already are.


### Settings: 25 sections, judged for merges and deletions (21)


- **[high·ux-defect]** Dragging out of the sheet closes Settings — the backdrop closes on click, not mousedown
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/Settings.tsx:345`
  _Today:_ The backdrop is <div className="settings-backdrop" data-agent-blocked onClick={requestClose}> and the sheet stops propagation (358). A click event fires on the nearest common ancestor of mousedown and mouseup, so a drag that starts inside the sheet and ends over the backdrop targets the backdrop and closes the modal. The sheet is min(1040px, 94vw) wide and min(900px, 90vh) tall (src/styles/settings.css:164-177), so the backdrop is a thin margin the pointer crosses easily. The app's other two sheets do it correctly: CustomizeSidebar.tsx:146-151 and Overlays.tsx:367-371 both use onMouseDown with an e.target === e.currentTarget guard.
  _Problem:_ Selecting text in the custom-instructions textarea, dragging the creativity/reverb/distortion sliders, or selecting the readonly MCP config string and releasing past the sheet edge dismisses Settings. On a clean page it closes silently; on a dirty one it raises the unsaved strip for something the user never asked for.
  _Do:_ Replace onClick={requestClose} on .settings-backdrop with onMouseDown={(e) => { if (e.target === e.currentTarget) requestClose(); }}, copying CustomizeSidebar.tsx:146-151, and delete the now-unnecessary onClick={(e) => e.stopPropagation()} at Settings.tsx:358. Same change updates the assertion in e2e/page-script/settingsPages.test.mjs that currently reads /className="settings-backdrop"[^>]*onClick=\{requestClose\}/ — point it at onMouseDown, or the suite fails on a correct fix.

- **[high·ux-defect]** 'Reset to Arcelle defaults' does not reset the open room's panes, and says it does
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/InterfaceSection.tsx:122`
  _Today:_ The Reset copy reads 'Puts the sidebar, the density, the texture and every room's saved pane layout back to how Arcelle ships. It changes nothing inside your rooms…' (InterfaceSection.tsx:122-126). resetAll (useInterfaceSettings.ts:119-124) sets density and texture, calls resetNavPrefs() — both live stores, so the sidebar, density and texture change instantly — and calls forgetSavedLayouts(), which only deletes prLayout:* keys from localStorage (useLayout.ts:187-197). The live room's useLayout holds ratios/hidden/railExpanded in React state seeded once at mount (useLayout.ts:243-268) and re-persists them on every change (useLayout.ts:330-341).
  _Problem:_ Three of the four things the sentence promises happen in front of the user; the fourth does not, and it is the one on screen. The panes sit exactly where they were, and the record the button just deleted is written back by the next splitter drag. The honest reading of a button where three effects land and one silently does not is 'this button is broken'.
  _Do:_ Preferred: thread the workspace's layout reset into InterfaceSection the same way onApplyPreset is already threaded (types.ts:54-58 → Settings.tsx:96, 625) and call it from resetAll, so the open room snaps back too. If that is not wanted, change the sentence at InterfaceSection.tsx:122-126 to 'Puts the sidebar, the density and the texture back to how Arcelle ships, and forgets every room's saved pane layout — rooms you already have open keep their current panes until you reopen them.'

- **[high·ux-defect]** A failed model download reports itself at the bottom of whatever page you are on
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/Settings.tsx:629`
  _Today:_ {error && <div className="gate-error">{error}</div>} is rendered inside .settings-body but outside all six .settings-page divs (Settings.tsx:629), so it sits below whichever page is showing, at the very bottom of a 900px-tall scroller. `error` is written only by model operations — stopPull, pull, removeModel, pullSpecial (useModelManagement.ts:163, 185, 199, 245) — and cleared only by starting another model operation (174, 194, 228) or by pressing Save in Behavior (useBehaviorSettings.ts:79, wired at Settings.tsx:218). Nothing clears it on a page change.
  _Problem:_ A download that fails on the AI page prints its error under six framed sections, off-screen unless the user scrolls to the very bottom. Switch to Voice or App and the same red line reappears there, attached to nothing on the page. And pressing Save in Behavior makes a model error vanish, which teaches a cause-and-effect that does not exist.
  _Do:_ Move the error to the control that produced it: pass `error` into ModelSection and render it as <div className="gate-error">{error}</div> directly under the .pull-row block (after ModelSection.tsx:200), the same way the section already renders sttErr at line 263. Pass it into HelpersSection too, or adopt item 6 and have one section. Then delete Settings.tsx:629, the clearError argument at Settings.tsx:218, and clearError from useBehaviorSettings.ts:11 and 79.

- **[high·honesty]** 'Test connection' saves the remote-AI address even when the test fails — and Settings then says it is unsaved
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/useRemoteAi.ts:41`
  _Today:_ testOllama (useRemoteAi.ts:41-54) calls api.testOllamaUrl, and the backend command SAVES first on purpose: test_ollama_url calls set_ollama_url before it tries to reach the machine (src-tauri/src/commands/moonshot/server.rs:341-346), and set_ollama_url writes remote_ollama_url into the room DB and sets the live base-URL override (318-329). When the machine is unreachable the command returns Err, so the frontend falls into its catch (50-52): it prints '✗ …' and does NOT update storedUrl. closetDirty stays true (line 65), so the Connections page keeps flying the 'Unsaved' nav flag and Escape raises 'Some changes on this page haven't been saved yet — closing now would discard them' (Settings.tsx:376-379).
  _Problem:_ The room is already pointed at that remote machine — this is the setting whose own copy says 'Set this and the room is no longer local' (RemoteAiSection.tsx:44-51) — while Settings tells the user the change is unsaved and offers to discard it. Pressing 'Discard & close' discards nothing; the address stays. That is the app claiming a state it does not have, on the control that ends the room's locality.
  _Do:_ In useRemoteAi.ts's testOllama, move setStoredUrl(closetUrl.trim()) out of the try's success path and into a finally, since the backend has persisted by then either way — or, better, have the catch set storedUrl too and append one clause to the result line: '✗ Could not reach it. The address was saved anyway — clear the field and press Save to go back to this Mac.' Either way the dirty flag must stop claiming the write did not happen.

- **[medium·copy]** Nothing says which settings belong to this room and which to this Mac
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/BehaviorSection.tsx:61`
  _Today:_ Two storage scopes are mixed with no marking. Room-scoped, encrypted in the room DB via api.setSetting: model (miscActions.ts:391), temperature, custom instructions, response style, auto-describe, memory auto-save, adaptive text, edit approval, room role, spoken voice, autolock, web access + the two agent lanes + result previews, dictation mode/translate, mic voice processing (MicSection.tsx:33), remote-AI address, room-server scope. Only two sections state their scope: AppearanceSection.tsx:53 and InterfaceSection.tsx:42-45.

A user who spends ten minutes writing custom instructions and picking a role in one room opens another and finds both gone, with nothing having said the settings were per-room. The inverse is worse: turning the theme dark 'for this room' silently changes every room.

Verify each label against the writer before shipping — api.setSetting means 'This room', localStorage or Keychain means 'This Mac'. No new panel, one span per section.

- **[medium·honesty]** AI helpers hides the true vision answer behind 'Ollama is not running'

  _Problem:_ A room running a cloud vision engine with Ollama stopped is told to start Ollama in order to add a vision helper it does not need — while the app is holding the name of the model that would actually mark the image. This is the exact failure the surrounding code was rewritten to fix ('a room on a cloud vision model was told its vision helper was missing and offered a download it had no use for', HelpersSection.tsx:54-59); the outer gate quietly re-creates it.
  _Do:_ Move the ai?.running gate inward: render the Vision helper block whenever visionInstalled or visionBlock is set, regardless of Ollama, and show 'Ollama is not running — start it to download a local helper' only in the branch that offers a download (HelpersSection.tsx:79-97) and around the Semantic search block, which really does need a local pull.

- **[medium·copy]** AI advisors points at 'Connected tools (MCP) below' — nothing is below, and a test forbids it being there
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/AdvisorsSection.tsx:67`
  _Today:_ AdvisorsSection's note reads: 'the advisor can list, search, open and edit this room's files — and drive any Connected tools (MCP) below — through a private, one-question-long local bridge' (67-73). AdvisorsSection is the last section on the AI page (Settings.tsx:488-496); connectors are a separate rail destination (src/workspace/ConnectorsView.tsx), and e2e/page-script/settingsDeadPanels.test.mjs asserts Settings must have no connector page at all.
  _Problem:_ The sentence sends the reader down the page to a list that was deliberately removed from Settings. On the one control whose whole subject is a second way for content to leave this Mac, the reference that would tell the user what is exposed points at nothing.
  _Do:_ Change 'any Connected tools (MCP) below' to 'any connector you have installed (Connectors in the sidebar)' at AdvisorsSection.tsx:69-70. One string, and it stops the last cross-reference in Settings from pointing at a deleted panel.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Behavior's Save button sits mid-section, above three checkboxes that never needed saving
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/BehaviorSection.tsx:109`
  _Today:_ BehaviorSection renders the creativity slider, response style, custom instructions, then a <div className="settings-actions"> holding the Save button (109-113) — and then three checkboxes (114-138) and the edit-approval select (143-156), all of which persist on change (useBehaviorSettings.ts:96-115). tuningDirty covers only the slider and the textarea (126-128).
  _Problem:_ A Save button placed above four controls reads as governing them. Someone who ticks 'Save suggested memories automatically' and closes without pressing the Save button above it has every reason to think the tick was dropped — and someone who presses Save expects it to have committed the checkbox, which it never touched. The visual grouping contradicts the save model in both directions.
  _Do:_ Move the settings-actions block from line 109 to the end of the section, after the edit-approval hint (161), so the only controls above the Save button are the two it actually saves; or adopt item 1 and delete the button, which removes the ambiguity outright.

- **[medium·copy]** Two buttons labelled 'Test' write the room's settings before testing anything
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/OnlineSection.tsx:160`
  _Today:_ 'Test search' (OnlineSection.tsx:154-161) calls testWebSearch, which runs saveWebAccess() first — persisting web_provider and the three sub-switches — before performing one search (useOnlineSearch.ts:79-92). 'Test connection' (RemoteAiSection.tsx:55-57) reaches a backend command that likewise saves the address before trying it (src-tauri/src/commands/moonshot/server.rs:341-346). Both sections also carry a separate Save button, and OnlineSection carries a 'Not saved yet — press Save, or these changes are discarded when you close Settings' note (172-184).
  _Problem:_ On the page where the user decides whether this room may reach the internet, a button that promises to test also commits. Ticking 'Let this room reach the internet' and pressing 'Test search' to see what happens turns internet access on for the room, permanently, while the note two lines down is still saying the changes are unsaved until you press Save. Save-before-test is the right engineering choice — testing what is not active would be a lie of a different kind — but the labels hide it.
  _Do:_ Rename the two buttons to say what they do: 'Save & test search' at OnlineSection.tsx:160 and 'Save & test connection' at RemoteAiSection.tsx:56 (with 'Testing…' unchanged as the busy label). If item 1 lands, the labels can go back to 'Test' because there will be nothing left to commit.

- **[low·consistency]** The delete-a-model confirm disarms itself after three seconds; no other confirm in Settings does
  `/Users/benreich/private-room/src/settings/useModelManagement.ts:204`
  _Today:_ askRemoveModel arms the inline Delete? / ✓ / ✕ confirm and starts a 3000ms timer that clears it (useModelManagement.ts:203-208), rendered at ModelSection.tsx:119-138. Every other destructive confirm on this surface waits indefinitely: compact arms until Confirm or Cancel (PrivacySection.tsx:281-311), checkpoint delete and rollback use inline confirm rows with no timer (CheckpointsSection.tsx:112-153), and disconnecting a provider uses a native dialog (AiProvidersSection.tsx:53-61).
  _Problem:_ The question vanishes while the user is reading it. Anyone who pauses to check which model they are about to delete looks back at a trash icon, and the click they had already committed to lands on the icon and merely re-arms — the same gesture means 'ask' and 'confirm' depending on a timer the user cannot see. It is also the only place in Settings where a destructive question expires.
  _Do:_ Delete the timer: remove confirmTimer and the window.setTimeout from askRemoveModel (useModelManagement.ts:203-208) and the clearTimeout from cancelRemoveModel (210-214), leaving ✕ and the existing cancelRemoveModel as the only ways out. Less state, and the four destructive confirms in Settings then behave identically.


### Chat, composer, agent graph, token budget and the AI-in-context surfaces (19)


- **[high·ux-defect]** The Memory page never says memories are sent to the model
  `src/workspace/MemoryView.tsx:187`
  _Today:_ `.mem-lead` promises "Everything the AI remembers about you — visible, editable, and used only when relevant." `select_memories` (retrieval.rs:347), called from agent.rs:1032, appends the matching memories to the turn's user message under "Notes to remember for this room:" on every question. The page has no trust chip, no cloud icon and no mention of the route — `trustState` and `isCloudRoute` are never imported here.
  _Problem:_ "Used only when relevant" is the only thing said about use, and it reads as a restraint promise while the actual behaviour is transmission. On a cloud route the user's standing notes about themselves — exactly the content this page invites them to write — leave the Mac with every relevant question, redacted only as far as the privacy door recognises them. The page that asks for that content is the one page that never says where it goes.
  _Do:_ Add one sans line under `.mem-lead` in `.mem-masthead`, derived from `trustState(isCloudRoute(s.model, s.ai), s.privacyOn).tone` (markup.ts:38, 57) so it can never drift from the top bar, status bar and chat strip: `good` → "Memories are read on this Mac."; `warn` → "On a cloud model, memories relevant to a question are sent with it — private details are replaced first."; `danger` → the same sentence without the second clause. No new component, no new state.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The AI-action dialog can send the whole room to a cloud model without saying so
  `src/workspace/AiActionModal.tsx:55`
  _Today:_ `.studio-prompt-title` states the scope ("this file" / "this folder" / "whole room", lines 55-62) and `.studio-prompt-hint` explains the prompt field. The file imports nothing from markup.ts — no `isCloudRoute`, no `trustState` — and nothing anywhere in its 239 lines names the route. It is opened via `a.openAiAction` (studioActions.ts:218) from StudioShelf.tsx:92 with a folder-or-room scope and from the file-row menu at Overlays.tsx:904.
  _Problem:_ Every other place a user commits content to a model says where it goes: the composer draws `.cloud-strip` ("This will leave your Mac.", ComposerPane.tsx:163-176) and the Studio tab draws `.studio-note` (AiPane.tsx:384). This dialog is the widest-scoped send in the product — an action over the entire room — and it is the one that says nothing. The user presses Run believing the scope line is the whole disclosure.
  _Do:_ Lift the composer's `.cloud-strip` markup (ComposerPane.tsx:161-176) verbatim into AiActionModal between `.studio-prompt-hint` (line 63) and the prompt field, gated on `isCloudRoute(s.model, s.ai)` ALONE — drop the composer's `s.question.trim().length > 0` half, since this dialog's prompt is prefilled and would otherwise never show the strip. Fold the scope word into the sentence ("The whole room will leave your Mac."). Reusing the markup rather than writing new copy keeps the two identical.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The token meter and Hand off disappear on restart, though the numbers were saved
  `src/workspace/effects.ts:755`
  _Today:_ `TokenBudgetBar` early-returns `if (!usage) return null` (TokenBudgetBar.tsx:66), and `usage` is `usageOf(usageByChat, activeChatId)` (state.ts:255) — a `useState` map filled only by the live `ask-token-usage` event (effects.ts:222) and by a handoff marker (chatActions.ts:781). The chat-open effect at effects.ts:753-761 loads `api.getMessages` into `setMessages` and seeds nothing else. Meanwhile each turn's snapshot IS persisted on the message: `effects_json` writes the `usage` key (agent.rs:1437-1439) and `MessageEffects.usage` is typed at apiTypes.ts:457. Nothing in the frontend ever reads a stored `usage` except the handoff marker line.
  _Problem:_ Quit and reopen a long conversation and the whole row is gone: no fill, no `12,043 / 16,000`, no `Near limit` tape — and no "Hand off" button, because the component returns null before drawing it. The one turn where a person most needs to compact the context is the first turn after reopening a conversation that was already near the limit, and that is exactly the turn where the meter and its remedy are both absent. The reading then reappears after the next answer, at which point it is too late to have acted on it. The data was never lost — it is sitting on the last assistant message, unread.
  _Do:_ In the chat-open effect at effects.ts:753-761, after `setMessages`, seed the reading from what was saved: walk the loaded messages backwards for the newest one with `m.effects?.usage` and call `s.setChatUsage(chatId, that)`. It is the same setter the live event uses (effects.ts:222) and the same shape, so nothing downstream changes — the meter simply stops forgetting a number the room already wrote down.

- **[high·ux-defect]** A failed specialist reads as a clean one once the turn is reloaded
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:603`
  _Today:_ The persisted roster strip renders `m.effects.agents.map(...)` as `<span className="agent-chip past" title={p.instruction}>{p.label}</span>` (ChatPane.tsx:600-616) — label and tooltip only. `AskPlanStep.status` (apiTypes.ts:346, values pending/running/done/failed) rides in that same stored array: the sidecar sets `entry["status"] = "failed"` at graph.py:1809, 1844, 1847, 1859 and 2979, every `plan` event is a complete snapshot (apiTypes.ts:341-345), and sidecar.rs:1191-1192 keeps `last_plan` which agent.rs:1442 persists under the `agents` key. composer.css:870-880 already defines `.agent-chip.done` / failed styling, and AgentGraph's single-agent path renders a failure glyph with the comment "Never state by colour alone" (AgentGraph.tsx:216-224).
  _Problem:_ Once the live graph is gone — a restart, or simply the tab switch that item 4 describes — the flat strip is the entire account of a delegating turn, and it renders a specialist that failed exactly like one that finished. A turn whose File agent errored out and whose answer is therefore partial is, in the transcript, indistinguishable from a turn where everything worked. The status was recorded, sent, and stored; only the renderer drops it.
  _Do:_ In ChatPane.tsx:610-615 give the past chip its stored state: `className={`agent-chip past ${p.status ?? ""}`}` so the existing `.agent-chip.done` / `.failed` rules apply, and render the failure glyph plus `role="img" aria-label` beside the label the same way AgentGraph.tsx:216-224 already does for the single-agent chip. No new component and no new data — one class and one glyph.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The transcript never records which model wrote an answer
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:569`
  _Today:_ `Message` (apiTypes.ts:247) carries id/role/content/sources/createdAt/effects/kind and `MessageEffects` (apiTypes.ts:446) carries boxes/annotation/edits/usage/agents — nothing names the engine. The message label row (ChatPane.tsx:569-581) renders `speakerName(m.role)` — the string "Room AI" for every assistant turn (markup.ts:280) — plus the clock. The only engine readouts are the top-bar pill `a.engineLabelOf(model)` (TopBar.tsx:266) and the trust badge beside it (TopBar.tsx:303), both of which describe the model selected right now.
  _Problem:_ Switch models mid-conversation and the whole transcript re-labels itself retroactively. An answer produced on a raw cloud route reads, after switching back, as if it had been produced under "Local only"; an answer from a 4B local model is indistinguishable from a frontier cloud one. For an app whose core value is never implying privacy it did not deliver, where each answer actually went is the one fact the transcript does not keep.
  _Do:_ Persist the engine label into the message's `effects` map — `effects_json` at src-tauri/src/commands/agent.rs:1420 already builds that map key by key beside `usage` and `agents`, so this is one more `map.insert("engine", …)` plus one field on `MessageEffects` — and render it in `.msg-label` (ChatPane.tsx:569) next to `<time className="msg-when">`, in the same mono metadata face. Show it only when it differs from the previous assistant message in the list, so an unchanged conversation gains one first-turn stamp and nothing else.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Switching to Studio and back destroys the agent diagram and every clock
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:203`
  _Today:_ `graphByMsg` is plain `useState` in ChatPane (line 203) and `liveTimings` is a ChatPane `useRef` (line 192). AiPane renders the chat conditionally — `{s.aiTab === "chat" && (<>…<ChatPane/></>)}` (AiPane.tsx:188-248) — so clicking Studio or Activity unmounts ChatPane and discards both.
  _Problem:_ The diagram is described in-code as "the only record of which helpers ran and for how long" (ChatPane.tsx:189). Reading a finished turn, clicking Activity to check a job, and coming back leaves that answer with only the flat `agent-strip past` chips — the per-node reports, which are the sole place a failed specialist explains itself (AgentGraph.tsx:694-717), and every duration are gone. The same switch mid-turn empties the live timing store, so the running graph shows no elapsed time for the rest of the turn. The file's own comment at lines 116-122 already identified remounting as this hazard and used a ref to survive it for the privacy receipt; these two were missed.
  _Do:_ Move `graphByMsg` and `liveTimings` out of ChatPane into `WSState` (state.ts), keyed by message id exactly as `undoByMsg` already is (state.ts:161), and keep passing the timing store down to AgentGraph as the `timings` prop it already takes (AgentGraph.tsx:145). No visual change — the diagram simply stops depending on a tab it has nothing to do with.

- **[high·ux-defect]** An answer that rewrote files leaves no durable record of it
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:835`
  _Today:_ `s.undoByMsg[m.id]` renders "Undo 2 edits" (ChatPane.tsx:835-847) and is the only in-transcript sign a turn wrote to the room — and it is session state (state.ts:161) populated from `editedRef` at turn end (chatActions.ts:225-231), so it is gone after a restart. `MessageEffects.edits` IS persisted per message (agent.rs:1428 writes it; edit_file/edit_files/write_file push `{tool, outcome, n}` / `{tool, outcome, files, n}` at agent.rs:3167-3231) and apiTypes.ts:454 states "Telemetry only — the UI renders nothing from it".
  _Problem:_ "Ask before AI edits files" is off by owner decision, so the transcript is where a person is supposed to discover that an answer changed the room. The transient step chip is, per the sidecar's own label table (labels.py:50-52), "often the only place a user sees that a deletion happened at all" — and it evaporates when the turn ends. Reopen the room the next day and an answer that rewrote three files is indistinguishable from one that only talked.
  _Do:_ Render a persistent change line on the assistant message, above `.msg-footer` (ChatPane.tsx:784), built from `m.effects.edits`: "Changed 3 files in this room" / "Rewrote 1 file", summing the `n`/`files` keys and taking verbs from `tool`. Count only entries whose `outcome` records a landed write — the same array also carries declines and errors with `n: 0` (agent.rs:3184, 3195, 3230), and counting those would be the app claiming an edit it did not make. Style it like `.undo-edit` but never faded, because it is a report and not an action, and keep the session-only Undo button beside it while `undoByMsg` still holds the ids.

- **[medium·consistency]** Regenerate silently asks with less evidence than the turn it repeats
  `src/workspace/chatActions.ts:790`
  _Today:_ `regenerate` (line 790) re-parses `@`-mentions out of the saved text and passes `parsed.refIds` alone (line 820). Paperclip attachments and the turn scope's `fileIds` are not re-sent; the button's `title` at ChatPane.tsx:849 admits it in a parenthetical. Sixty lines earlier `askAgainWithRealDetails` does the opposite and re-attaches deliberately, including `currentTurnScope().fileIds` (chatActions.ts:226-232).
  _Problem:_ Two retry paths give two different answers to the same question, and the one a user reaches most often is the weaker one. The comment on the other path already states the consequence — a retry that carries "less evidence than the question it is retrying" — and that is precisely what Regenerate does, disclosed only in a tooltip nobody reads before clicking.
  _Do:_ Give `regenerate` the identical attachment block `askAgainWithRealDetails` uses — `new Set([...s.attachments.map(f=>f.id), ...parsed.refIds, ...currentTurnScope().fileIds])` — for both the `runCommand` and `askOnce` branches, and delete the parenthetical from the button title at ChatPane.tsx:849, which then stops being true. One rule for both retries.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Regenerate destroys the previous answer before it has a replacement
  `src/workspace/chatActions.ts:804`
  _Today:_ `regenerate` calls `api.deleteMessage(assistantId)` (line 804) and reloads the transcript (line 809) before running the new turn. The lost-reply strip's "Try again" (ChatPane.tsx:776-782) routes through the same function.
  _Problem:_ If the new turn is stopped, fails, or simply comes back worse, the original answer is unrecoverable — and on a local 4B model "came back worse" is exactly the case in which someone presses Regenerate. The user gambles a good answer to get a second one, with nothing on screen saying so.
  _Do:_ Capture `s.messages[idx].content` before the delete, and on the delete succeeding push the app's existing action toast: `s.pushToast("info", "Asking again — the previous answer was removed.", { label: "Restore it", run: … })`, re-inserting the stored text. `pushToast` already takes `{label, run}` and is used this way for the memory Forget undo at chatActions.ts:205. Skip the toast when the row was a lost-reply notice (`lostReplyNotice(content) !== null`) — there is nothing there worth offering back.

- **[medium·copy]** "Hand off" names an action nobody can guess
  `src/workspace/TokenBudgetBar.tsx:179`
  _Today:_ A composer chip reads "Hand off" ("Summarizing…" while busy, TokenBudgetBar.tsx:166-179). It appears once this chat has a usage snapshot — the component early-returns `if (!usage) return null` at line 66 — and is not gated on the meter's 70% threshold. Its `title` explains it ("Summarize this conversation and continue with a smaller context") and pressing it produces a divider reading "Context summarized, continuing" (`HandoffMarker`, line 193).
  _Problem:_ "Hand off" reads as handing the conversation to a person or another agent, which is a real thing this app does elsewhere (`*specialist`, "Asked the File agent"). The control, its tooltip and its own result use three different vocabularies for one operation, and the visible label is the one that misleads.
  _Do:_ Relabel the button "Summarize & continue" (TokenBudgetBar.tsx:179), matching the marker its own press produces. Keep the existing tooltip and the "Summarizing…" busy label. Button text only — no identifier renames.

- **[medium·accessibility]** The AI surfaces sit below the design system's own type floor
  `src/styles/shell.css:1007`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:272 declares `--fs-micro: 12px; /* the smallest size allowed anywhere */`, restated at tokens.css:442 as "The metadata floor does NOT move". Four surfaces in this lens hardcode below it: `.context-strip` 11px (shell.css:1007), `.step-chip` 11px at `--text-dim` (composer.css:941), `.agent-chip` 11px (composer.css:857), `.agent-strip-caption` 10px (composer.css:907).
  _Problem:_ The 11px line is the one that states what the AI will answer from and whether the page's text is about to leave the Mac — the smallest type in the pane carries the most consequential sentence in it. Both status-bar rules record that 10px there was raised for exactly this reason (shell.css:23-25 and 1068-1071), so the floor is a decided policy these four rules quietly opt out of.
  _Do:_ Replace all four hardcoded values with `var(--fs-micro)`. If `.context-strip` then overflows at a narrow pane, let it — `.context-label` already truncates with an ellipsis (shell.css:1012-1017) and `.local-mini` is a fixed-size item, so the trust pill keeps its place and the scope label is the correct casualty.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The scope control is two different things wearing one appearance
  `src/workspace/AiPane.tsx:196`
  _Today:_ When `view.available.length > 1` the strip renders `<select className="context-scope">` (AiPane.tsx:196-211); otherwise a `<button className="context-count">` that runs `s.setLibraryTab("sources")` and `layout.showPane("library")` (lines 212-221). shell.css:1018-1040 styles both identically — accent colour, `background: none`, `border: 0`, inherited font — the only difference being the native caret the select draws.
  _Problem:_ The same slot sometimes changes what the turn will read and sometimes navigates away to another pane, and a user cannot tell which before clicking. Worse, in the select state the sources picker becomes unreachable from the strip entirely — even though the label can read "“Portfolio map” + 2 attached" (browserScope.ts:141-145) and those attachments are exactly what someone reading that label might want to change.
  _Do:_ Always render the `<select>`. Give it a final, visually separated option — "Choose files…" — whose `onChange` branch calls `s.setLibraryTab("sources"); layout.showPane("library")` and leaves `chosen` untouched. Delete the `.context-count` button branch and its rule at shell.css:1018-1028. One control, one affordance, and the sources list reachable from every scope.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Every message in the transcript is stamped with a time and no day
  `src/workspace/markup.ts:347`
  _Today:_ `messageClock` (markup.ts:347-352) returns `d.toLocaleTimeString(undefined, { hour: "2-digit", minute: "2-digit" })` — hours and minutes only. ChatPane.tsx:576-580 renders it as `<time className="msg-when" dateTime={m.createdAt}>`, so the full ISO reaches assistive technology but the visible stamp is bare. There is no day divider anywhere in the list: the render path is `shownMessages.map(...)` at ChatPane.tsx:522 with no date grouping, and no `toLocaleDateString` call exists in the file.
  _Problem:_ A conversation is a long-lived object here — chats persist, `Show earlier messages` pages back through hundreds of turns — so a transcript routinely spans weeks. Every one of those turns reads "09:14", with nothing on screen distinguishing this morning from three weeks ago. Scrolling up to find what the room said about a file last month, the reader has no anchor at all; the clock actively suggests recency it cannot support.
  _Do:_ Extend `messageClock` (markup.ts:347) to fold the day in when the message is not from today, reusing the shape `dayLabelOf` already established at AiPane.tsx:825-838: today → `14:32` unchanged; otherwise `Aug 3, 14:32`, with the year appended when it differs from the current one. One pure function, already unit-testable, and no new element in the row.

- **[medium·accessibility]** The AI pane's tabs announce a tablist that has no panels
  `src/workspace/AiPane.tsx:104`
  _Today:_ `.assistant-header` carries `role="tablist"` (AiPane.tsx:104-107) and its three buttons carry `role="tab"` with `aria-selected` (lines 109-163). None has an `id` or `aria-controls`, none manages `tabIndex`, and there is no arrow-key handler. The three panels are plain conditional siblings — `{s.aiTab === "chat" && …}` (line 188), `{s.aiTab === "studio" && <StudioView …>}` (line 250), `{s.aiTab === "activity" && <ActivityPanel …>}` (line 252) — with no `role="tabpanel"` and no `aria-labelledby`.
  _Problem:_ The markup promises the tab pattern and delivers none of its behaviour. A screen-reader user is told "tab, 1 of 3, selected" and then has no association to the content it controls, so the relationship the role exists to express is missing. A keyboard user gets Tab-through-every-button instead of the Left/Right arrow navigation a tablist announces. This is the entry point to the chat, so it is the first thing assistive technology meets in the pane.
  _Do:_ Give each tab an `id` (`ai-tab-chat` / `-studio` / `-activity`) and `aria-controls` pointing at its panel; wrap each of the three conditional blocks (AiPane.tsx:188, 250, 252) in a `<div role="tabpanel" id=… aria-labelledby=…>`; set `tabIndex={s.aiTab === … ? 0 : -1}` on the buttons and add one `onKeyDown` on `.assistant-header` moving `s.setAiTab` on ArrowLeft/ArrowRight/Home/End. Attributes and one handler — no new control, no visual change.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The conversation's name is the only thing in the chat header that shrinks
  `src/styles/chat.css:64`
  _Today:_ `.chat-head` is `display: flex` with no `flex-wrap` (chat.css:64-83), and `.chat-head .chat-select` is its only `flex: 1; min-width: 0` item (chat.css:88-90). Beside it sit six fixed-width controls: rename, "＋ New", "Copy chat", auto-speak, hands-free and a `DeleteControl` (ChatPane.tsx:289-345). There is no `@container` rule anywhere in chat.css or composer.css.
  _Problem:_ At the pane widths AgentGraph.tsx:303-306 documents (~290px), the chat title collapses toward zero while every button holds its size — so the control that tells you which conversation you are in, and the control you use to switch conversations, is the first thing to disappear and the last thing that should.
  _Do:_ Keep the select, rename and "＋ New" inline; move "Copy chat", auto-speak and hands-free into `QuickActionsMenu` (workspace/QuickActions.tsx) with `buttonLabel="Chat actions"`, a `buttonIcon`, `inlineMax={0}`, no footer, and a `useState` in ChatPane for its required `open`/`onOpenChange` pair. Leave `DeleteControl` inline: a `QuickAction` exposes only `onRun` (QuickActions.tsx:5-12), so folding delete into the menu would drop its two-step confirm. The menu brings its own arrow-key and Escape handling (QuickActions.tsx:80-97) and renders nothing when empty.

- **[low·consistency]** Hands-free can re-arm the mic for answers nobody hears
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:322`
  _Today:_ `toggleHandsFree` (voiceActions.ts:34) only flips `handsFree` and writes the `voice_handsfree` setting; it never touches `autoSpeak`. The two are independent icon-only toggles side by side in the chat header (ChatPane.tsx:309-334), distinguished by icon and tooltip alone.
  _Problem:_ Hands-free on with auto-speak off produces a spoken conversation in which the room listens, thinks and answers in silence, then re-opens the mic — a state nobody chooses deliberately, reachable by pressing the more obviously-named of the two buttons first.
  _Do:_ In `toggleHandsFree` (voiceActions.ts:34), when switching hands-free ON while `s.autoSpeak` is false, also call `toggleAutoSpeak()` — both live in the same factory — and say so in the button's on-state tooltip at ChatPane.tsx:324: "Hands-free — the mic re-arms after each answer, and answers are read aloud." Switching hands-free off leaves auto-speak wherever the user last put it.

- **[low·accessibility]** The token breakdown opens an unnamed dialog and never moves focus
  `src/workspace/TokenBudgetBar.tsx:133`
  _Today:_ The meter button declares `aria-haspopup="dialog"` (line 81); the popover carries `role="dialog"` (line 133) with no `aria-label`, no `aria-modal`, and no focus move. Escape closes it (lines 55-64) and the backdrop click works (line 132).
  _Problem:_ A screen-reader user is told a dialog opened and is then left standing on the button, with the five category rows reachable only by continuing to read forward past it. The exit is handled; the entrance is not.
  _Do:_ Add `aria-label="Token usage breakdown"` and `tabIndex={-1}` to the `.token-breakdown-pop` div (line 133), focus it on open via a ref, and return focus to the meter button when `open` goes false. No visual change.

- **[low·copy]** The token meter says "this turn" about the turn before
  `src/workspace/TokenBudgetBar.tsx:85`
  _Today:_ The meter button's tooltip reads `${total} / ${max} tokens used this turn — click for a breakdown` (TokenBudgetBar.tsx:85). `usage` is the LATEST snapshot stored for the chat (state.ts:241-255, "the latest turn's usage snapshot FOR EACH CHAT"), so between turns — which is most of the time the tooltip can be read, since it takes a hover — it describes the turn that already finished, not one in progress.
  _Problem:_ It is a small false present tense in the one readout whose whole job is to be exact about a number. Read while composing the next question, "used this turn" invites the reading that the box you are typing into has already consumed 12,043 tokens. The breakdown popover directly below is careful to label its own estimates ("Breakdown is estimated…", line 156); the tooltip above it is the only sentence in the component that misstates what it is counting.
  _Do:_ Change the tooltip at TokenBudgetBar.tsx:85 to `${total} / ${max} tokens in the last answer — click for a breakdown`. Text only; the figures, the fill and the `Near limit` tape are unchanged.

- **[low·accessibility]** Closing the quick-actions menu drops keyboard focus onto nothing
  `src/workspace/QuickActions.tsx:93`
  _Today:_ `QuickActionsMenu` moves focus INTO the menu on open (QuickActions.tsx:71-75, focusing `[role="menuitem"]` at `focusIdx`) and closes on Escape (lines 93-96), on item activation (line 172) and on backdrop mousedown (line 144). None of those paths restores focus: the menu unmounts while a menu item holds focus, so the browser falls back to `<body>` and the next Tab restarts from the top of the document.
  _Problem:_ The component otherwise implements the menu pattern carefully — roving focus, arrow keys, Home/End, an `aria-label` added specifically because it "opened as an unnamed list of items" (lines 152-155). The exit is the one half missing, and it is the half a keyboard user hits every time: press Escape and you are ejected to the start of the page rather than returned to the button you opened. This is the component item 15 proposes to put in the chat header, so the gap would be inherited by the chat.
  _Do:_ Hold a ref on the trigger button (QuickActions.tsx:128-140) and, in an effect that watches `open` going true→false, call `triggerRef.current?.focus()`. Same fix and same shape as the focus-return proposed for the token breakdown popover, so the two menus behave identically.


### Skills, Scripts, Workflows, Connectors — the configuration pages (19)


- **[high·ux-defect]** Editing a skill file silently throws away unsaved SKILL.md edits
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:496`
  _Today:_ `load(id)` (SkillsView.tsx:204-222) re-fetches the bundle, re-seeds `draft` from the server and calls `setDirty(false)`. Three resource actions call it unconditionally — `addResource` (:496), `saveResource` (:507), `removeResource` (:527) — and `confirmDiscard("resource")` (:439-441) only tests `resourceDirty`, never `dirty`. The `useEffect` on `selected` (:224-233) does the same whenever the contextual sidebar's `openSkill` (skillActions.ts:17-21) switches skills, and that path asks nothing at all. So: type a new Description, then add `references/policy.md` — the description reverts to the saved value and Save greys out (`disabled={!dirty…}`, :784) with nothing said.
  _Problem:_ The one editor in this area with no version history — its own delete dialog says so at :520 — loses typed work on the most ordinary sequence there is: write the trigger sentence, then attach the reference it names. Four independent paths hit it, and the guard that exists deliberately looks at the wrong flag.
  _Do:_ Give `load` a second parameter — `load(id, { keepDraft: true })` — and pass it from `addResource`, `saveResource` and `removeResource`, which only need `bundle.resources` refreshed, not the SKILL.md fields re-seeded. Widen `confirmDiscard("resource")` to include `dirty` on the paths that reload, or save the metadata first the way `toggleEnabled` already does at :411. For the sidebar path, have `openSkill` run the same confirm before switching (the file editor's `editorDirtyRef`, state.ts:428, is the existing pattern for a cross-component dirty flag).

- **[high·ux-defect]** A draft workflow's card promises a run the scheduler will never make
  `src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowLibrary.tsx:318`
  _Today:_ `cadenceOf` (cadence.tsx:42-80) reads only the schedule and the binding — never `w.status`. The card draws `<CadenceNote countdown={sc?.enabled ? countdown(sc.nextRunAt, now) : ""}/>` (WorkflowLibrary.tsx:316-319) beside a `Draft` badge (:321). The scheduler's query requires `w.status = 'active'` (src-tauri/src/db/workflows.rs:375). And this is the default first-run state: `save_workflow` always creates a draft (workflow.rs:3002) yet still calls `apply_schedule`, which computes a real `next_run_at` (workflow.rs:2969-2976); `instantiateTemplate` (workflowActions.ts:106-117) passes the template's schedule straight through, and three of the four built-in templates ship one (workflow.rs:4143, 4167, 4183).
  _Problem:_ Press "From template → Morning digest" and the library immediately reads "Draft · every day at 08:00 · in 5h". Nothing will happen at 08:00. The app is fabricating a future event on the first workflow most people create — the exact class of claim the product forbids.
  _Do:_ Pass status into the cadence: `cadenceOf(sc, w.binding, w.status)` returns `{ mark: "", note: "schedule waiting — activate it", exact: null }` when `status !== "active"` and an enabled schedule exists, and `WorkflowLibrary` passes `countdown=""` for anything not active. In `SchedulePopover`, the preview at :148-152 is the natural place: when the workflow is a draft, print one line under it — "This workflow is a draft — the schedule is stored but won't fire until you activate it." (the popover would need the status passed in alongside `disabled`).

- **[high·ux-defect]** You cannot test-run a workflow before activating it, and nothing says why
  `src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:348`
  _Today:_ `<button className="subtle btn-ic" disabled={workflow.status !== "active"} onClick={() => void a.runWorkflowNow(workflow.id)}>Run now</button>` — no `title`, no `aria-disabled`, no reason, in a header where the Activate button beside it goes to real trouble to explain its own disabled state (:361-375). The backend has no such rule: `run_workflow` (workflow.rs:3310) and `start_workflow_run` (:2718) never look at status, and the agent tool `test_workflow` (:4122) exists precisely to run drafts to completion. The draft banner says "Draft — activate to run on schedule" (:442), which reads as though manual runs are already fine.
  _Problem:_ The loop after "Compose with AI" is: read the draft, try it, fix it. The first step of that loop is a dead button with nothing to say. The only way to see what a composed pipeline does is to activate it — which also arms its schedule — i.e. to make the commitment you wanted to make after testing, not before.
  _Do:_ Enable Run now for drafts whenever `valid` is true, and label it "Test run" while `workflow.status === "draft"`. Keep it disabled only while `!valid`, with the same treatment Activate uses at :365-371 (`title={checking ? "Checking this workflow…" : "Can't run yet — " + errors[0]}`). Change the draft banner (:442) to "Draft — test runs work; activate it to run on a schedule or from a file's Actions menu."

- **[high·ux-defect]** Run now is enabled on a file-scoped workflow and is guaranteed to fail
  `src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:348`
  _Today:_ Run now's only gate is `disabled={workflow.status !== "active"}` (:348); it calls `a.runWorkflowNow(workflow.id)` with no file id (workflowActions.ts:37-47). For a binding of `scope: "file"` the backend refuses outright — `start_workflow_run` returns "This workflow runs on a chosen file — start it from a file's Actions menu." (workflow.rs:2745-2750) — which `tryToast` surfaces as an error toast. The component already computes `isFileScoped` at :177 and uses it to hide Pin (:380) and to render SchedulePopover's refusal (:429 → SchedulePopover.tsx:62-74).
  _Problem:_ The built-in "Deep read" template is file-scoped (workflow.rs ~4196). Activate it — which is what its own draft banner tells you to do — and the most prominent button in the header is one that can only ever produce an error. Two neighbouring controls already explain this exact constraint in words; the one that fails is the one that says nothing.
  _Do:_ Add `|| isFileScoped` to Run now's `disabled` and give the wrapper SchedulePopover's own sentence as the title: "This workflow runs on a chosen file — start it from that file's Actions menu." `isFileScoped` is already in scope at :177, and the string already exists at SchedulePopover.tsx:66-67, so this is a two-line change that deletes a failure rather than adding a control.

- **[high·ux-defect]** A half-built pipeline cannot be saved at all, so leaving the page destroys it
  `src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:377`
  _Today:_ Save is `disabled={!dirty || !valid}` (:377), mirroring the backend: `update_workflow` runs `validate_workflow_inner` and returns `Err` for any invalid definition (workflow.rs:3076-3079). `leave()` (:258-269) can therefore only offer to discard: "This workflow has unsaved changes, and they can't be saved until the listed problems are fixed. Leave and lose them?" The form lives entirely in component state (`def`, `name`, `emoji`, `binding`, :74-79).
  _Problem:_ Building a branching pipeline passes through many invalid intermediate states — a route with one label wired, a step with no target. During all of them the work exists only in React state. Close the pane, click another workflow in the sidebar, or reach for something else and ten minutes of canvas work is gone behind a yes/no dialog. The validator is the gate for ACTIVATION; it should not be the gate for remembering what you typed.
  _Do:_ Let a draft save unvalidated: in `update_workflow` (workflow.rs:3066-3080) skip the `validate_workflow_inner` bail when the target row's `status == "draft"` — activation, scheduling and running each validate independently (`set_workflow_status` → `validate_runnable` :3143, `apply_schedule` :2966, `compile_workflow` :952). Then change the button to `disabled={!dirty}` and leave the errors panel (:447-472) as the thing that blocks Activate. `leave()` then only has to ask about genuinely unsaved edits.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The install drawer says "Installed ✓" without knowing whether the connector started
  `src/settings/McpMarketplace.tsx:811`
  _Today:_ `doInstall` (:534-546) awaits `installServer`, ignores the `McpServerStatus[]` it returns (useMcpConfig.ts:78-80 has them) and sets `done = true`; the footer then renders `<CircleCheckIcon/> Installed` (:811-813). `mcp_apply_config` (mcp_cmds.rs:12-24) writes the config, records the approval fingerprint, calls `start_mcp_connections` — which spawns — and returns the statuses as they stand at that instant. The drawer sits over a scrim (:601) covering the Installed list, the only place the real state (`connected` / `connecting…` / `failed` + error) is shown (ConnectorsView.tsx:336-342).
  _Problem:_ The confirm step promises "Arcelle will run `uvx …` as soon as you confirm" (:796-799) and then shows a green tick for having written JSON. If the program is missing or the endpoint refuses, the user sees success, closes the drawer and finds a red dot with no explanation — the same failure the runtime pre-check at :762-780 was added to prevent, one step later.
  _Do:_ Pass the live status down: add `statusOf?: (name: string) => McpServerStatus | null` to `Props` and supply `(n) => mcpStatuses.find(s => s.name === n) ?? null` from ConnectorsView.tsx:556. In the drawer, label the button "Added to this room" once `done`, and under it render `statusOf(entry.name)` in the connector's own words — "Connecting…", "Connected · 12 tools", or the failure text in the `gate-error` treatment already used at :781. No spinner and no invented progress: only the state the backend is reporting.

- **[high·ux-improvement]** Skills is the one place with no way to try the thing you just made
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:778`
  _Today:_ The editor header offers Export folder and Save SKILL.md (:783-785); the grid card is a single button that opens the editor (:734-738). Scripts have Run and Runs (ScriptRow.tsx), workflows have Run now and Run history (WorkflowDetail.tsx:348, :587-588). A skill's only feedback is the word "Enabled", whose own `why` defines it as "Its description is offered to the assistant" (:82) — a statement about advertising, not use. Nothing records that a skill was ever read: `read_skill` (agent.rs:4324) bumps no counter.
  _Problem:_ "Did it work?" has no answer on this page. The user builds a skill, enables it, asks a question in chat, gets an answer, and cannot tell whether the skill was chosen, ignored, or invisible because it is bound to a specialist the main assistant never sees (agent.rs:747-750 filters the per-turn skill list to unowned skills).
  _Do:_ Two parts. (1) Small and immediate: in the editor header beside Export folder, add "Try it in chat" — `s.setQuestion("/" + draft.name + " ")` then `a.focusComposer()` (miscActions.ts:358; the layout arg is optional). Render it only when `bundle.skill.enabled`, because the backend refuses `/name` for a disabled draft (agent.rs:757-761) and chatActions.ts:449-453 already says so. (2) Record one real fact, locally: set `last_used_at` on the skills row inside the `read_skill` arm (agent.rs:4324) and the explicit-`/name` path, and show it on the card as "Last used 2 days ago" or the honest "Never used yet". A timestamp of a tool call that actually happened — no inference, nothing leaving the Mac.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Selecting a step in the pipeline scrolls nothing, so the click reads as a no-op
  `src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:484`
  _Today:_ `PipelineCanvas` (:474-482) sits above `NodeParamSheet`, which renders in normal flow at :484-494 and is followed by the binding editor at :497 — a second `<div className="node-param-sheet">`, visually identical. `onSelect={setSelected}` (:478) sets state and moves nothing. The only named section heading in the body is "Run history" (:587).
  _Problem:_ On a pipeline of more than a few steps the inspector opens below the fold: the node takes a selected outline and the panel that appeared is off-screen. When it is visible it is indistinguishable from the binding editor stacked under it — two identical bordered panels, the second about a different subject entirely.
  _Do:_ In `onSelect`, after `setSelected(id)`, call `scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest" })` on the sheet via a ref — a scroll that keeps a just-revealed panel in view is a state change the user needs, not motion for its own sake. Add `<h3 className="wf-sec-head">Step: {selectedNode.label}</h3>` above the inspector and `<h3 className="wf-sec-head">Where this workflow appears</h3>` above the binding editor, matching the existing "Run history" heading at :587, so the body reads as three named sections rather than two identical boxes.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** Enabling a skill means opening it; there is no toggle where the skills are listed
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:733`
  _Today:_ The grid card (:733-764) shows the name, flags, description, resource count and owner, and its whole surface is one button that opens the editor. The only enable control is the checkbox in the editor head (:809-813). The sidebar rows (Sidebar.tsx:1085-1099) likewise only open. Import deliberately lands a skill as a disabled draft (:388) and `compose` produces a draft too (:356).
  _Problem:_ Every path into this page produces drafts, and the page's own stamp counts them ("None enabled yet", :557). Turning on four imported skills is four navigations, four scrolls to the toggle and four "← All skills". The list knows the state; it just cannot change it.
  _Do:_ Put the switch on the card: replace the read-only `Enabled`/`Draft` tape (:742-748) with the same `.sk-enable` switch used in the editor, calling `api.setSkillEnabled` + `a.refreshSkills()` and stopping propagation so the rest of the card still opens the editor. Render it disabled, with the flag's `why` as the title, when the skill carries the `incomplete` or `owner` flag — a skill with no description cannot be chosen however enabled it looks, which `skillFlags` already knows at :91-108. (This also needs the card to be something other than a single `<button>`, since a control cannot nest inside one.)

- **[medium·consistency]** Saving a new skill fails through a toast; workflows validate inline
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:784`
  _Today:_ "Save SKILL.md" is enabled the moment the editor opens (`startNew` sets `dirty` true at :310) with an empty name and empty description. Pressing it calls `createSkill`, whose backend refuses with "Give the skill a name." (skills.rs:202) or "Describe what the skill does and when the assistant should use it." (skills.rs:222), arriving as `s.pushToast("error", String(e))` (:337) and then disappearing. The name is also silently normalised — `validate_skill_name` lowercases and turns spaces and underscores into hyphens (skills.rs:200), so "Review Contracts" is stored as `review-contracts` and the field only shows that after a reload. WorkflowDetail, by contrast, keeps a persistent, clickable error list (:447-472) and puts the first error in the button's tooltip (:365-371).
  _Problem:_ The first save of the first skill is the most likely one to fail, and it fails into a transient message that names a rule without pointing at the field, on a form the user is still looking at.
  _Do:_ Mirror the workflow pattern locally: derive `problems` in the component (empty name, empty description, a name that does not match `/^[a-z0-9-]+$/` after trimming), disable Save while `problems.length > 0` with `title={problems[0]}`, and render the list under the head row in the existing `.wf-errors` idiom. Normalise the name inside `patchDraft("name", …)` exactly as `validate_skill_name` does (lowercase, spaces and underscores to hyphens) so the field always shows what will be stored.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "New script" throws you into the file editor with no way back to Scripts
  `src/workspace/fileActions.ts:769`
  _Today:_ `createNewScript` (:768-792) saves a file called literally `New script.py`, calls `viewFile` (which sets `openFile`, and the ViewerPane chain tests `openFile` before the Scripts branch at :637 vs :1230, so the page is replaced), flips on edit mode, and toasts "New script created — edit it, then run it from the Scripts page." with no action attached — while `runScript`'s toast does carry one (`{ label: "Scripts", run: openScripts }`, scriptActions.ts:37). `save_generated_impl` does not de-duplicate names (files.rs:1008-1035), so pressing it twice produces two rows both reading "New script.py".
  _Problem:_ The page's primary action navigates out of the page, and the only thing that knows the way back is a sentence that isn't clickable. Two starter scripts are then indistinguishable in the list, in the top-bar shortcut menu and on the run-consent card.
  _Do:_ Attach the same action this app already uses: `{ label: "Scripts", run: a.openScripts }`. Mint a unique name the way `createNewNote` does two functions away (fileActions.ts:678-688) — or simply check `s.scripts` for "New script N.py" before calling `saveGeneratedFile`. Better still, since the Scripts page has the file list already, open the new script in place and leave the destination alone.

- **[medium·copy]** The Scripts empty state teaches the manifest but not what a run is allowed to do
  `src/workspace/scripts/ScriptsPage.tsx:37`
  _Today:_ The page-level caution — `SCRIPT_POWERS` + `SCRIPT_WORKSPACE_NOTE`, the app's single source for this wording (scriptTrust.ts:18-25) — renders only `{s.scripts.length > 0 && …}` (:37-43), on the stated reasoning that a standing caution over "No scripts yet" warns about something that cannot happen. But the empty state right below it contains a "New script" button (:52) and a manifest example whose caption ends "saves its outputs back into the room as versioned files" (:63-66).
  _Problem:_ The screen where someone decides to write their first script is the screen that omits the fact that running one gets the internet, no sandbox, and read/write access to their whole home folder. They meet that sentence for the first time on the consent card — after the script is written, which is when it is hardest to say no.
  _Do:_ Render the same `script-caution-page` paragraph in the empty state too, directly under the "No scripts yet" heading and above the New script button, with its lead changed to "Before you write one: a script here is a real program — …". Same constants, same component, one condition removed.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** A run tells you which files it produced but will not open them
  `src/workspace/workflows/RunHistory.tsx:96`
  _Today:_ `StepBody` renders `Imported {n} file(s): {names}` as a plain caption (:92-97). The local `ScriptReport` type declares `imported?: { name?: string }[]` (:20-26), but the artifact is a serialized `ScriptRunReport` whose `imported` is `Vec<FileMeta>` (script_run.rs:928-934) and `FileMeta` carries `id` (commands.rs:597-598) — the ids are in the JSON and dropped by the parse. `RunHistory` takes only `runs`, `nodeCount` and `nodes` (:6-12).
  _Problem:_ The output is the point of the run. The one surface that knows exactly which files a run created is the one surface you cannot reach them from — you learn the name and then go hunting in the Library.
  _Do:_ Widen the type to `imported?: { id?: string; name?: string }[]`, add an optional `onOpenFile?: (id: string) => void` prop to `RunHistory` and `StepBody`, and render each imported name as a `subtle` button calling it (falling back to plain text when a legacy artifact has no id). Pass `a.viewFile` from both call sites — WorkflowDetail.tsx:588 and ScriptRow's `<RunHistory runs={runs} nodeCount={1} />`.

- **[medium·accessibility]** A skill's state is legible; the reason for it is tooltip-only
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:744`
  _Today:_ `skillFlags` pairs each state with a `why` sentence (:68-110) — including the two that mean the skill is inert: "Incomplete" ("A skill needs a description to be chosen and instructions to follow…") and "Unknown owner" ("Offered to \"x\", which is not a specialist this app has, so nothing can use it"). Both are rendered only as `title={f.why}` on a non-interactive `<span>` — on the card at :744 and in the editor at :801 — so they are not focusable, and a `title` on a plain span is not reliably announced.
  _Problem:_ The two flags that mean "this skill can never fire" are drawn in urgent red with a one-word label, and the sentence saying what to do about it is reachable only by hovering with a mouse. Colour plus one word is the whole message for everyone else.
  _Do:_ Leave the card's tape as it is. In the editor, where there is room, render the `why` of any non-`on` flag as visible text under the flag row (:798-806): `{flags.filter(f => f.key !== "on").map(f => <p key={f.key} className="sk-flag-why">{f.why}</p>)}`. That puts the fault and its fix in the DOM, in the accessibility tree, on the screen where it gets fixed.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The Skills sidebar hides the two states that mean a skill can never fire
  `src/workspace/Sidebar.tsx:1094`
  _Today:_ `SkillsNav`'s row copy is `{skill.enabled ? "Enabled" : "Disabled draft"} · N resources` (:1094-1096), and its tooltip is `title={skill.description}` (:1090) — empty when there is no description. Meanwhile `skillFlags` (SkillsView.tsx:91-108) derives "Incomplete" for a missing description and "Unknown owner" for an agent the roster does not have, both marked `nb-sem-urgent`. The sidebar computes neither.
  _Problem:_ A skill that can never be chosen reads "Enabled · 0 resources" in the column you navigate skills from, identical to a working one, with an empty tooltip. The card two panes over says otherwise. This is the same list a user scans after importing several folders.
  _Do:_ In the row copy (:1094-1096), when `!skill.description.trim()`, print "Needs a description — can't be chosen" instead of the Enabled/Draft line. `SkillSummary` already carries `description` (apiTypes.ts:1704-1717), so no new data crosses the wire. Leave the owner check to the card and editor, which have the agent roster.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** A connector's API key is masked as you type it and shown in clear text below
  `src/workspace/ConnectorsView.tsx:574`
  _Today:_ The install drawer masks secrets with `type="password"` and says why in a comment: the Connectors screen is not fenced from the screen-driving agent, so a key shown in the clear is a key it can read (McpMarketplace.tsx:728-743). `specToEntry` (:21-39) then writes those values into the `mcpServers` entry, `installServer` puts the whole JSON into `mcpConfig` (useMcpConfig.ts:73-79), and the Advanced disclosure renders it verbatim in `<textarea className="mcp-config">` (ConnectorsView.tsx:574-581) — env values and Authorization headers included. The disclosure's warning note (:568-573) talks only about connectors reaching the internet.
  _Problem:_ A field is masked for a stated reason, and the same value is displayed unmasked a few inches below it behind a one-click disclosure that does not mention keys at all. Whichever treatment is right, the page currently makes both claims at once.
