  _Do:_ Add one clause to the existing warning note at :569-572: "This box also shows any API key or token you gave a connector, in clear text." Copy only, in the note that is already there, no new control and no change to how the config is stored.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** A failed connector states the error and no way to retry it
  `src/workspace/ConnectorsView.tsx:341`
  _Today:_ A failed connector renders `{s.error ?? "failed"}` in `.conn-state` (:341) and offers exactly two controls: the on/off switch (:351-366) and Remove (:394-401). Toggling the switch off and on is in fact a reconnect — `mcp_set_server_enabled` rewrites the config and calls `apply_edited_config` (mcp_cmds.rs:1546-1553) — and Advanced → Save & Connect restarts everything (:583-585). Neither is stated anywhere on the card.
  _Problem:_ The one state that needs a next action is the only one with no action offered. The path a user will find on their own — Advanced → Save & Connect — also restarts every other enabled connector in the room, the side effect the marketplace's own confirm step warns about at McpMarketplace.tsx:801-802.
  _Do:_ After the error text on a `failed` card, add one sentence: "Turn it off and on again to try connecting once more, or fix its entry under Advanced." It is true of the code as it stands, it needs no new control, and it names the cheaper of the two recoveries first.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "New skill" then "← All skills" always asks to discard work that does not exist
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:310`
  _Today:_ `startNew` sets `setDirty(true)` (:310) before a character is typed. `leaveEditor` (:479) calls `confirmDiscard("editor")`, whose `pending` is `resourceDirty || dirty` (:440), so an untouched new-skill form raises the native dialog "Discard your unsaved changes to SKILL.md?" (:443). `patchDraft` (:315-318) already raises `dirty` on the first real edit.
  _Problem:_ The first thing "New skill, actually never mind" does is warn about work that does not exist — and it is the same dialog that guards the genuine data loss in the resource-reload defect. Spending it on a false alarm is how a user learns to click straight through it.
  _Do:_ Have `startNew` leave `dirty` false and let `patchDraft` raise it (:317), which it already does. Save's empty-form problem is then handled by the derived `problems` list proposed for the inline-validation item, not by a stale dirty flag.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The skill card can never show "Incomplete" for an empty SKILL.md body
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:91`
  _Today:_ `skillFlags` raises "Incomplete" when the description OR the instructions are empty, but guards the second half with `skill.instructions !== undefined` (:91) because the grid only has `SkillSummary`, which has no instructions field (apiTypes.ts:1704-1717; db/skills.rs:12-25). `validate_skill_fields` (skills.rs:216-234) checks the name and the description but never refuses an empty body. So a skill saved with a description and an empty SKILL.md reads "Enabled" on the card (:742-748) and "Enabled + Incomplete" in the editor (:798-806) — contradicting the comment at :794-797, which says the editor shows "the same four states the card shows, so a skill does not change its story when you open it".
  _Problem:_ The flags exist to say "this skill is inert". For one of the two causes of inertness they only say it after you have already opened the skill — and the code states the opposite intention in writing.
  _Do:_ Pick one and close the gap. Smaller: make an empty body a save-time refusal in `validate_skill_fields` (skills.rs:216-234) beside the empty-description one — the state stops existing, so nothing has to report it. Otherwise add `has_instructions: bool` to `SkillSummary` (db/skills.rs:12-25, apiTypes.ts:1704-1717) and feed it into `skillFlags` from the card.


### Loading, error, empty, progress, toasts, and copy honesty across the whole app (24)


- **[high·ux-defect]** "N jobs running" counts jobs that are not running
  `src/shell/StatusBar.tsx:148`
  _Today:_ StatusBar.tsx:139-150 renders `{runningJobs} job{s} running`. `runningJobCount` (src/shell/activity.ts:33-42) is `groupActivity(jobs).active.length + summaryStarting + saving`, and `groupActivity` (activity.ts:82) puts **queued** jobs into `active` alongside running ones. It also adds `summaryStarting` (an optimistic flag set before any job exists, studioActions.ts:60-63) and a recording save, neither of which is a job.
  _Problem:_ AiPane's own JobRow renders a queued job as "Waiting" with a **Remove** button rather than Stop (AiPane.tsx:1050-1056), so the Activity list and the status bar describe the same rows differently. With one job executing and two queued behind the single work slot, the footer says "3 jobs running" while Activity one click away shows one running and two waiting — and the number is exactly the one that would tell the user whether to start a fourth.
  _Do:_ Split the counter in src/shell/activity.ts: keep `runningJobCount` as `active.filter(j => j.status === "running").length + summaryStarting + saving`, and export `queuedJobCount(s)` = `active.filter(j => j.status === "queued").length`. Pass both into StatusBar and render one button: "{n} running" plus, only when non-zero, " · {q} waiting". Leave the title at StatusBar.tsx:142 alone — it already says "open Activity".

- **[high·ux-defect]** The empty viewer promises privacy from a hardcoded string that reads no state
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:1302`
  _Today:_ ViewerPane.tsx:1298-1303 renders `.viewer-empty-note` — a lock icon plus the fixed sentence "**Encrypted on your Mac.** Your data is encrypted and never leaves this file unless you choose a cloud model." It is a literal; the component reads no trust state to produce it.
  _Problem:_ The sentence is false whenever online search is on, whenever an MCP connector is connected, whenever the browser is used, and whenever Settings → the Closet points Ollama at another machine — the last of which is precisely the case `isCloudRoute` (markup.ts:38-40) was written to catch and which this string does not consult. It also sits on the empty-workspace screen, so it is the first privacy claim a new user reads.
  _Do:_ Replace the literal with the state that already exists: import `trustState` in ViewerPane and render `trust.title` here — the same sentence the status-bar chip is showing at that moment, so the empty viewer can no longer disagree with the footer below it. If a fixed clause is still wanted, keep only the part that is unconditionally true and reuse the seal's existing wording: "This room is an encrypted file on this Mac" (StatusBar.tsx:76-77).

- **[high·ux-defect]** The crash card asserts that nothing was written, which a render boundary cannot know
  `src/shell/ErrorBoundary.tsx:43`
  _Today:_ ErrorBoundary.tsx:40-44 tells the user "Your room is untouched — it is an encrypted file on this Mac, and nothing was written by this failure." The boundary sees a render exception and nothing else (componentDidCatch, lines 22-26, only logs to console).
  _Problem:_ A crash in a re-render triggered by a resolved `api.updateFileContent`, `api.trashFile` or a job start has already written. The user is told to stop worrying at the exact moment they should go and check. The chat layer models this correctly — `lostReplyAdvice` (markup.ts:172-180) refuses to say "nothing happened" when a write landed or a job is live, and carries three different sentences to avoid it — so the crash card is the one place still making the claim the rest of the app was rewritten to stop making.
  _Do:_ Cut the unknowable clause from ErrorBoundary.tsx:40-44. Keep "Arcelle hit an unexpected error while drawing the window. Your room is an encrypted file on this Mac and is still there." and add only what the code can stand behind: "Anything already saved is saved; unsaved work in the editor is gone." No new state, one fewer claim.

- **[high·ux-defect]** A failed script read is presented as "this podcast is an old format — generate it again"
  `src/workspace/PodcastPanel.tsx:65`
  _Today:_ PodcastPanel.tsx:64-66: `catch { if (alive) setScript(null); }`. A thrown `api.getPodcast` collapses into the same `null` as a genuinely script-less page, and PodcastPanel.tsx:181-192 then explains that null with a confident cause: "This page has no script attached… Podcast scripts made before voices existed are pages only — generate the podcast again from the Studio shelf and the new one will have voices."
  _Problem:_ A transient read failure instructs the user to regenerate a podcast that is intact — a minutes-long local-model run that produces a duplicate and may overwrite a cast they had already assigned. The error is not merely swallowed, it is replaced with a wrong diagnosis, which is worse than showing nothing.
  _Do:_ Add a third state beside `script`/`loading`, exactly as the same file already does for the voice catalog: `const [readError, setReadError] = useState("")`, set from the catch at line 64-66. Render it before the legacy branch as "This script couldn't be read just now ({message}). Nothing was changed — reopen the page to try again." The legacy explanation at 181-192 then only appears when `getPodcast` actually resolved to null, which is the only case it describes.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Edit & resend destroys the rest of the conversation with the count only in a tooltip
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:719`
  _Today:_ chatActions.ts:886-892 deletes every message from the edited one to the end of the chat. The only warning is a `title` tooltip on the button that OPENS the editor (ChatPane.tsx:747-751, "…everything after it is removed"). Once the editor is open the card shows "Send again" / "Cancel" (ChatPane.tsx:717-726) with no mention of the tail, and plain Enter fires it (ChatPane.tsx:706-709).
  _Problem:_ Deleting twenty messages — including answers whose Undo-edit buttons (ChatPane.tsx:835-844) were the only route back to a file change — is one unconfirmed keystroke, and the warning lives on a tooltip the user dismissed by hovering away. Every other destructive path in the app arms first through DeleteControl (trash, versions, folders, chats, skills, workflows); a hover tooltip is not consent.
  _Do:_ Put the count on the control that does it, no dialog and no new component. In ChatPane.tsx compute `const tail = s.messages.length - s.messages.findIndex(m => m.id === editDraft.id) - 1` and label the button at line 718-720 "Send again" when `tail === 0`, otherwise "Send again — deletes the {tail} messages below". This is the same technique DeleteControl's `question` prop uses for multi-select (Sidebar.tsx:588-589 names the count).

- **[high·copy]** The gate's lead sentence promises "never leaves this computer" for every room, including the ones the same file refuses to classify
  `src/screens/StartScreen.tsx:56`
  _Today:_ StartScreen.tsx:54-57 opens the app with "Your files, links, chats and AI — sealed inside one encrypted file that never leaves this computer", followed by the assurance list "Offline by default" (line 62). Directly above it, `roomPlace` (StartScreen.tsx:23-32) returns a location badge for exactly two paths — `/Library/Mobile Documents/` ("iCloud Drive", marked pending) and `/Volumes/` ("External volume") — and `null` for everything else, on purpose: its comment at lines 17-22 says the removed fallback badge "affirmatively labelled local" a room in ~/Dropbox or ~/Google Drive and that "saying nothing is the only honest answer for a path we cannot read".
  _Problem:_ The badge was made honest and the sentence above it was not. A room in ~/Dropbox gets no badge — and the unconditional lead copy then supplies the claim the badge deliberately withheld, on the first screen, before the user has opened anything. The file's own comment names this exact scenario as the reason the fallback was deleted, so the fix stopped one line short.
  _Do:_ Reword the lead at StartScreen.tsx:54-57 to what is true of every path: "Your files, links, chats and AI — sealed inside one encrypted file on your Mac." That keeps the whole promise (encrypted, single file, yours) and drops the one clause a file path can falsify, leaving `roomPlace`'s badge as the only thing on the screen that speaks about where a room lives. No new state, one fewer claim.

- **[high·ux-defect]** "Thinking locally…" is the same model-only privacy claim as the trust chip, on the surface the user is actually watching
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:966`
  _Today:_ While a turn streams, ChatPane.tsx:959-967 picks between two lines with `isCloudRoute(model, s.ai)`: the cloud branch says "Asking your cloud AI — content leaves this Mac…", the else branch says "Thinking locally…". `isCloudRoute` (markup.ts:38-40) is engine + relay only; nothing here reads `s.webOn` or `s.mcpTools`.
  _Problem:_ This is the same blind spot as the status-bar trust chip but on the surface the user is looking at while it happens. A local model that reaches for online search or an MCP connector during the turn sends the question, or text derived from it, off the machine — while the pane says it is thinking locally. The comment two lines above (ChatPane.tsx:959-962) states the principle the code then half-applies: "Where this question is going is a privacy consequence".
  _Do:_ Feed this line from the same widened trust function proposed for the status bar: when the engine is local but `s.webOn` or `s.mcpTools.length > 0`, render the middle case — "Thinking on this Mac — online search and connected tools may send parts of this out…" — in the `chat-route-cloud` style, not the quiet one. One rule, three surfaces (chip, empty viewer, streaming line), no new component.

- **[high·ux-defect]** The trust chip says "nothing leaves the device" while the chip next to it says "Internet tools on"
  `src/shell/StatusBar.tsx:88`
  _Today:_ `trustState(cloud, protectedOn)` (markup.ts:57-78) takes only the engine route and the privacy door. Workspace.tsx:871 supplies `cloud` as `isCloudRoute(s.model, s.ai)`, so a LAN-relayed Ollama is already handled — but nothing about outbound tools reaches it. With a local model it returns label "Local only", title "The AI runs on this Mac — nothing leaves the device.", rendered as the chip at StatusBar.tsx:83-89. Immediately to its right, StatusBar.tsx:93-111 renders a separate chip from `webOn`/`mcpToolCount`: "Internet tools on" when either is set, "No external tools" otherwise.
  _Problem:_ With a local model plus online search or a connected MCP tool, the status bar makes two contradictory claims a centimetre apart, and the one with the strongest wording ("nothing leaves the device") is the false one — search queries derived from the user's question go to one of the room's engines and MCP arguments go to the connector. markup.ts:49-56 declares trustState the single source for "whether their content leaves this Mac", so the app's designated authority answers with two thirds of the evidence.
  _Do:_ Make outbound transport, not just the engine, an input to the one trust function. Change `trustState` in src/workspace/markup.ts to `trustState(cloud, protectedOn, outbound: { web: boolean; tools: number })`, and when `!cloud && (web || tools > 0)` return a fourth state: tone "warn", label "Local + internet tools", title "The AI runs on this Mac, but online search and connected tools send what it asks for off this Mac." Pass `webOn`/`mcpToolCount` into the call at StatusBar.tsx:68 and delete BOTH arms of the chip at StatusBar.tsx:93-111 — the warn arm's content is now inside the trust chip, and the "No external tools" arm is now exactly what "Local only" means. The status bar loses a cell and the trust chip keeps its existing click-through to Settings → Cloud privacy.

- **[medium·copy]** "See the card in the sidebar" points at a pane that has no such card
  `src/workspace/studioActions.ts:55`
  _Today:_ studioActions.ts:55 pushes `"Already summarizing — see the card in the sidebar."`, and the function doc-comment at studioActions.ts:37-38 repeats "The sidebar card shows progress". Sidebar.tsx reads `s.jobs` exactly once, at line 1417, filtered to `j.kind === "create"`, so a `deep_summary` job never renders there. Its card is the JobRow in the assistant pane's Activity section (AiPane.tsx:672-675).
  _Problem:_ The one toast whose whole job is to redirect a click sends the user to the wrong pane. They press "Summarize room", are told it is already running and to look left, find nothing, and reasonably conclude it is not running — then press again. The toast is the only report on this path, so being wrong about the location costs the whole message.
  _Do:_ Change the string at studioActions.ts:55 to "Already summarizing — it's in Activity." and attach the action the toast system already supports: `{ label: "Show Activity", run: onShowActivity }`, the same callback Workspace.tsx:882 hands StatusBar. Per toastStack.ts:72-73 an action with no `about` waits to be dismissed rather than expiring, which is right for an offer. Fix the doc-comments at studioActions.ts:37-38 and 119-121 in the same edit, since both make the same wrong promise.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The updater draws a progress track that stays at 0% for the whole download
  `src/updater.ts:140`
  _Today:_ updater.ts:136 reads `contentLength`; when the server omits it, `total` stays 0 and updater.ts:140 calls `progress.set(null)`. `set(null)` (updater.ts:190-197) restores the label to "Downloading Arcelle X…" but still executes `fill.style.width = `${pct ?? 0}%``, leaving the track drawn and empty.
  _Problem:_ An empty track that never moves for a multi-hundred-megabyte download reads as a stalled update rather than an unmeasured one, and there is no cancel path in this code, so the user's only recourse is force-quitting mid-install. The app knows better in two places already: ModelSection.tsx:201-204 renders its bar only when `pullPercent != null`, and jobProgress.ts exists entirely to keep "running, position unknown" apart from "0% done" (jobProgress.ts:56-58). The launch banner is the one progress surface that ignores both.
  _Do:_ In `showDownloadProgress` (updater.ts:186-197), when `pct === null` set `host.dataset.unknown = "1"` (and clear it otherwise), and add `.update-progress[data-unknown] .update-progress-track { display: none }` to the stylesheet, so an unmeasurable download shows the label alone — the same shape jobMeter's `indeterminate` branch produces. Do not add a Cancel button: `update.downloadAndInstall` is awaited with no abort handle and no backend cancel exists, so the button would be a control that cannot do what it says.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Three viewers end in a "Reading…" state that nothing ever clears
  `src/viewers/SlidesView.tsx:158`
  _Today:_ SlidesView.tsx:158 `if (!slideCount) return <div className="empty-hint">Reading slides…</div>` is the FINAL branch — after `loading` (146), `readError` (147), `parseError` (148) and `renderError` (149-157) have all been ruled out. BookView.tsx:139 (`if (!book)`) and ArchiveView.tsx:138 (`if (!tree || !entries)`) are the identical terminal shape.
  _Problem:_ A deck that parses cleanly to zero slides, a book with no detectable chapters, or an archive whose index came back empty renders an ellipsis promising work in progress when nothing is running. There is no timeout, no retry and no error text, so the file looks permanently stuck rather than permanently unreadable — and the user waits instead of reaching for Export, which is the thing that would get them their content.
  _Do:_ Turn each terminal branch from a progress claim into a finding, reusing the sentence pattern SlidesView.tsx:151-155 already uses for renderError. SlidesView.tsx:158 → "This presentation has no slides in it. Its text is still stored, and **Export** saves the original out unchanged." BookView.tsx:139 → "This book couldn't be opened — no chapters were found. **Export** saves the original out unchanged." ArchiveView.tsx:138 → "This archive's index couldn't be read." Each keeps the existing Export escape hatch so the state is not a dead end.

- **[medium·consistency]** Four verbs for one phase: Opening / Reading / Loading / Preparing
  `src/viewers/PdfView.tsx:857`
  _Today:_ The same act — decoding a stored file before it can be shown — is announced with four verbs: "Opening archive…" (ArchiveView.tsx:135), "Opening book…" (BookView.tsx:136), "Opening document…" (DocxView.tsx:55, OfficeDocView.tsx:125), "Opening spreadsheet…" (SheetView.tsx:474), "Opening presentation…" (SlidesView.tsx:146), "Loading PDF…" (PdfView.tsx:857), "Loading the script…" (PodcastPanel.tsx:177), "Loading viewer…" (ViewerRouter.tsx:99), "Preparing the cloud view…" (CloudView.tsx:72), "Reading this recording…" (RecordingView.tsx:371). They also sit in four different wrappers: `.empty-hint`, `.viewer-status`, `.cloudview-empty`, and an `<h3 class="rec-blank-title">`.
  _Problem:_ "Reading" then names a *different, later* phase in three of the same viewers ("Reading archive…" ArchiveView.tsx:138, "Reading book…" BookView.tsx:139, "Reading slides…" SlidesView.tsx:158), so the word is simultaneously a synonym for and a successor to "Opening" depending on which file was double-clicked. A user opening a mixed folder gets a different sentence per file for identical behaviour. "Loading PDF" is also the only one that shouts a file format where the others name the object.
  _Do:_ Settle on "Opening {object}…" for the byte-fetch phase and delete the second verb. Export a STRING helper from the existing src/viewers/util.ts — `export const opening = (object: string) => \`Opening ${object}…\`` — and use it at all ten sites without touching their wrappers, so `.viewer-status`, `.cloudview-empty` and the recording panel keep their own layout. PdfView.tsx:857 becomes "Opening document…" like DocxView; ViewerRouter.tsx:99 stays "Loading viewer…" (it is the code-split chunk, not the file, and is honestly a different thing). The former "Reading X…" branches become the findings described in the previous item, leaving "Reading" to mean only what OCR and the browser use it for.

- **[medium·consistency]** Two ways back in time, four names between them
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:943`
  _Today:_ Two features, five names. The per-file one is the menu item **"History"** (ViewerPane.tsx:943), whose rows are **"saved version"** (ViewerPane.tsx:1082, 1092), whose container class is `time-machine` (ViewerPane.tsx:975), which ChatPane calls **"version history"** (ChatPane.tsx:837), which ViewerPane.tsx:1141 describes as "every overwrite **snapshots** to History", and whose empty state says "No earlier **versions** yet" (ViewerPane.tsx:986). The room-wide one is **"Checkpoint"** in TopBar (TopBar.tsx:422-435) and in CheckpointsSection's own heading (line 66), but a **"room-wide recovery point"** in the command palette (Overlays.tsx:282) — and the Settings tab that contains it is labelled **"History & storage"** (Settings.tsx:75).
  _Problem:_ The user cannot tell from the words whether the thing that will save them is per-file or room-wide, which is the only question that matters when something has just gone wrong — and Settings makes it worse by filing Checkpoints under a tab named History, the name the *other* feature uses. App.tsx:61 makes it concrete: a damaged room is told to "Try a checkpoint", a word that person may only have met as "recovery point" in the palette, and looking for it under a "History" tab is now ambiguous rather than helpful.
  _Do:_ Two concepts, two words, no synonyms: **History** for the per-file version list, **Checkpoint** for the whole-room copy. Rename the Settings group at Settings.tsx:75 from "History & storage" to "Checkpoints & storage" — the section inside is already called Checkpoints. Delete "recovery point" from Overlays.tsx:282; the hint becomes "A copy of the whole room you can roll back to". Change ChatPane.tsx:837 "reversible via version history" → "reversible from History", and ViewerPane.tsx:1141 "snapshots to History" → "saves the old copy into History". Leave "saved version" as the name of a row *inside* History.

- **[medium·consistency]** "Workspace" names both the middle pane and the whole app
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:612`
  _Today:_ The centre pane's controls call it the workspace: "Give the workspace pane the full width" (ViewerPane.tsx:612), "Collapse the workspace pane" (ViewerPane.tsx:620), "Focus the workspace" (Overlays.tsx:276 and src/workspace/LayoutMenu.tsx:91), and `paneName` reports focusing it as "Workspace focus" (useLayout.ts:673-678). The status strip spanning the entire window is `aria-label="Workspace status"` (StatusBar.tsx:71), and the layout presets describe the whole three-pane arrangement as "Sidebar, workspace, and the assistant" (useLayout.ts:117) — where "workspace" is again only the middle third.
  _Problem:_ One word denotes both the container and one third of its contents, in labels a few pixels apart. A screen-reader user hears "Workspace status" for the app and "workspace pane" for one column of it with no way to resolve which is meant; a sighted user reading "Sidebar, workspace, and the assistant" has to infer that the middle item is not the sum of the other two. useLayout.ts:670-672 already recognised this exact class of error for the sidebar and stopped one name short.
  _Do:_ Reserve "workspace" for the window and give the centre pane the name its own content already uses (PodcastPanel.tsx:182 calls an open file "this page"): **"the page"**. ViewerPane.tsx:612/620 → "Give the page the full width" / "Collapse the page"; Overlays.tsx:276 and src/workspace/LayoutMenu.tsx:91 → "Focus the page"; useLayout.ts:674 `paneName` → "Page"; useLayout.ts:117 hint → "Sidebar, page, and the assistant". StatusBar.tsx:71 keeps "Workspace status", now unambiguous.

- **[medium·ux-improvement]** Moving a file to the trash offers no Undo, though the toast machinery and the restore call both exist
  `src/workspace/fileActions.ts:371`
  _Today:_ `removeFile` (fileActions.ts:358-375) ends with `pushToast("success", 'Moved "X" to the trash.')` — no `action`, no `about`. `restoreFile` sits at fileActions.ts:377-388 and does exactly the undo. The bulk path `removeFiles` (fileActions.ts:548-565) ends at `reportBulk(report, "moved to the trash")`, whose toast is built at fileActions.ts:437-442, also with neither.
  _Problem:_ `stackToast`/`toastLifeMs` already model this case — a toast with an `about` and an `action` is an offer and gets 12 seconds instead of 5 (toastStack.ts:25, 72-73) — and the comment at toastStack.ts:22-23 says that window exists so someone can "read it, decide it was wrong, and reach Undo". Parsing every pushToast call in src/, the Library link (fileActions.ts:736) is the ONLY non-error toast in the app that uses it: the mechanism is spent on the cheapest reversible act and withheld from the most consequential one. The user's route back is instead: notice the sidebar, switch to Trash, find the row, press Restore.
  _Do:_ Give `removeFile` the offer shape the machinery was built for: `pushToast("success", 'Moved "X" to the trash.', { label: "Undo", run: () => void restoreFile(id) }, \`file:${id}\`)`. The `about` key is required — without it toastLifeMs returns null (toastStack.ts:72) and the toast would never expire. For the bulk path, give `reportBulk` two optional parameters (`action`, `about`) and pass them through to its success toast at fileActions.ts:437-442, then call it from `removeFiles` with `{ label: "Undo", run: () => void restoreFiles(ids) }` and `about: "file-bulk"`, so a second bulk removal replaces the first rather than stacking two Undos that mean different things.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The status bar counts the room summary twice while it starts — the Activity list already guards against showing it twice
  `src/shell/activity.ts:41`
  _Today:_ `runningJobCount` (activity.ts:33-42) returns `groupActivity(s.jobs).active.length + (s.summaryStarting ? 1 : 0) + (saving ? 1 : 0)` — `summaryStarting` is added unconditionally. `startDeepSummary` (studioActions.ts:60-77) runs through `runGuarded` with `begin: setSummaryStarting(true)`, a body that awaits `api.startDeepSummary()` and then `refreshJobs()` (which puts the new queued job into `s.jobs`), and `finish: setSummaryStarting(false)` afterwards. AiPane.tsx:623-624 handles the overlap explicitly: the optimistic "Room summary / Starting…" card renders only `s.summaryStarting && !s.jobs.some(j => j.status === "running" || j.status === "queued")`.
  _Problem:_ Between `refreshJobs` resolving and `finish` running there is at least one render in which both the flag and the job are true, so the footer reads "2 jobs running" for a single summary. The Activity list one click away shows one card, because AiPane applies a suppression that the shared counter — the function whose header comment says it exists so these surfaces "can never disagree" (activity.ts:3-7) — does not.
  _Do:_ Move AiPane's suppression into the shared rule, where the comment already says it belongs: in activity.ts:33-42 count the optimistic flag only when nothing it could be describing exists yet — `(s.summaryStarting && !jobs.some(j => j.status === "running" || j.status === "queued") ? 1 : 0)` — and delete the now-duplicated test at AiPane.tsx:623-624 in favour of a small exported helper so there is one copy of the judgement.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The sidebar's Creations rows print a raw done/total pair, bypassing the rule written to stop "0 of 0"
  `src/workspace/Sidebar.tsx:1469`
  _Today:_ CreationsNav renders each running create job's status as `{p ? `${p.label} ${p.done}/${p.total}` : j.status === "queued" ? "Queued" : "Working…"}` (Sidebar.tsx:1466-1469), reading `s.jobProgress[j.id]` directly. `jobMeter` (src/workspace/jobProgress.ts:34-66) is not called. Its comment at lines 44-52 exists for precisely this: "a job that had started but not yet planned its steps rendered as '0 / 1': one step, none of them done, both invented", and its `known = total > 0` test returns `figure: null` in that case.
  _Problem:_ A live `job-progress` event that carries a label before a total is planned (`total: 0`) prints "Rendering 0/0" here, and any over-count prints "13/12" — both of which jobMeter refuses to emit for the identical data in AiPane's JobRow. The comment sitting two lines above this code correctly says the app has "ruled out" invented numbers, and then the line below invents them anyway.
  _Do:_ Call the existing rule instead of re-deriving it: `const meter = jobMeter(j.status, 0, 0, p)` and render `meter.figure ? \`${p.label} ${meter.figure.done}/${meter.figure.total}\` : (p?.label ?? (j.status === "queued" ? "Queued" : "Working…"))`. The label alone is the honest output when there is no quantity, which is what jobProgress.ts was written to produce.

- **[medium·copy]** The bug-report sheet offers "the errors shown this session" — the error log is fed only by toasts, so crashes are never in it
  `src/workspace/FeedbackModal.tsx:197`
  _Today:_ FeedbackModal.tsx:191-199 offers a checkbox: "Append the {n} error message{s} shown this session", filled from `s.errorLogRef` (FeedbackModal.tsx:40) and appended under the heading "### Error messages seen this session" (line 52). `errorLogRef` is written in exactly one place — inside `pushToast`, when `kind === "error"` (state.ts:645-650). `ErrorBoundary.componentDidCatch` (src/shell/ErrorBoundary.tsx:22-26) pushes no toast; it only calls `console.error`, and its comment says "The app captures no logs, so the console is the only record a support conversation can reach for."
  _Problem:_ The pane-scoped boundary leaves the rest of the app working, so a user whose chat pane just crashed can open the feedback sheet — and the sheet, whose stated purpose is answering "what did it say?", lists every trivial toast and omits the crash. The one failure worth reporting is the one failure the report cannot see, while the label claims completeness.
  _Do:_ Pick one and make the sentence true. Cheapest: change FeedbackModal.tsx:197 and the heading at line 52 to "the {n} error message{s} shown as pop-ups this session", which is exactly what the ref holds. Better, and still small: give ErrorBoundary an optional `onCrash?: (text: string) => void` prop wired at the pane call sites to a store method that appends `{ at, text: \`${scope} render failed: ${detail}\` }` to `errorLogRef` without raising a toast — the card on screen is already the user-facing report, so this adds a record, not a second notification.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The toast stack's wrapper aria-live downgrades the error toasts the file says are interrupting
  `src/workspace/Toasts.tsx:42`
  _Today:_ Toasts.tsx:42 wraps the stack in `<div className="toast-stack" aria-live="polite" aria-relevant="additions">`, and each toast inside carries `role={t.kind === "error" ? "alert" : "status"}` (line 47). The file's own doc-comment at lines 33-38 states the intent: "the stack is a live region, and an error is an `alert` (interrupting) while successes and notices are polite `status` messages. Without this a blind user was told nothing at all when an action failed."
  _Problem:_ Toasts are inserted as children of a container that is already a polite live region, so the politeness that applies to the insertion is the container's — the child's `role="alert"` does not raise it back to assertive. The interrupting behaviour the comment promises is not what ships, and errors are the one thing this stack exists to announce (toastStack.ts:60-64 makes them the only messages that never expire, for the same reason).
  _Do:_ Delete `aria-live="polite"` and `aria-relevant="additions"` from the wrapper at Toasts.tsx:42. Each toast already carries `role="alert"` or `role="status"`, both of which are live regions in their own right with the correct politeness, and they are inserted into the DOM after the container exists — which is the shape assistive technology handles. One attribute pair removed, and the code finally does what its comment says.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "Summarize the room" reports itself as Working when any unrelated job is running
  `src/workspace/AiPane.tsx:381`
  _Today:_ AiPane.tsx:312-315 computes `const jobRunning = s.jobs.some(j => j.status === "running" || j.status === "queued")` and `const working = s.summaryStarting || jobRunning`. The "Summarize the room" row then renders `{working ? "Working…" : "Create"}` in a taped `is-working` state (AiPane.tsx:379-382). The button itself is not disabled, so it still fires `startDeepSummary`. Eleven lines away in the same feature, `startDeepSummary` (studioActions.ts:50-52) is careful to test `j.kind === "deep_summary" && j.status !== "done"` before deciding anything.
  _Problem:_ An image being generated, a file pass started by the assistant, or a Studio deck rendering all make this row claim the user's own summary is in progress. Pressing it anyway either starts a real second summary or produces the "Already summarizing — see the card in the sidebar" toast about a job that does not exist — so the label is wrong in both directions, and the state it shows belongs to a different piece of work entirely.
  _Do:_ Give the row the same kind filter its own action already uses: `const summaryRunning = s.jobs.some(j => j.kind === "deep_summary" && (j.status === "running" || j.status === "queued"))`, and set `working = s.summaryStarting || summaryRunning` at AiPane.tsx:315. Nothing is added — one predicate is narrowed to match studioActions.ts:50-52, and the tape then means what it says.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** "Everything in here is actionable" — five of the six row types in that section have no control
  `src/workspace/AiPane.tsx:580`
  _Today:_ The live section's user-visible heading is "Running now" (AiPane.tsx:552) under aria-label "Work happening now" (line 545); the sentence "Everything in here is actionable — Stop, Remove, Resume, Retry" is a JSX comment at lines 543-544 that nothing renders. Only `JobRow` (AiPane.tsx:1050-1069) has Stop/Remove/Resume/Retry. The OCR row (557-571), the Studio step row (580-597), the import row (601-627) and the summary-starting row (623-633) each render an indeterminate bar and no control; the recording-save row (636-670) offers only "Open". The Studio step row is a duplicate: `runStudio` (studioActions.ts:129-137) starts a real job, so the same run also appears as a JobRow — with Stop.
  _Problem:_ Two costs. The comment misleads whoever maintains the section (it is why an audit reads a Stop into rows that have none). And the Studio run is shown twice in the same list: a step row with no controls, directly alongside its own JobRow that has Stop and a jobMeter — so the card carrying the information is not the card carrying the button, and the user scanning for a way out of a minutes-long local run has to work out that two cards are one run.
  _Do:_ Subtractive. Delete the standalone Studio row at AiPane.tsx:580-597 and route `s.studioStep.text` into that job's JobRow, which already renders `live?.label ?? "Working…"` at AiPane.tsx:1033 — one card per run, and the Stop lands on the card that describes the work. Then correct the comment at AiPane.tsx:543-544 to what the section actually contains: "Jobs here can be stopped and resumed; OCR, imports and the recording drain finish on their own." Do not wire studioActions.ts:265-276 to anything here — it cancels a chat turn, not a Studio job.

- **[medium·consistency]** Four names for the one actor the user talks to
  `src/workspace/markup.ts:281`
  _Today:_ `speakerName` (markup.ts:280-281) labels every answer **"Room AI"** — a string that appears nowhere else in src/ except its own comment on line 278, and on no control. The pane is toggled by a pill labelled **"Assistant"** with aria-label "Show the assistant (⌘2)" (TopBar.tsx:348-360) and preset hint "Sidebar, workspace, and the assistant" (useLayout.ts:117, Overlays.tsx:278), while the pane's own aria-labels call it the **"AI pane"** (AiPane.tsx:172, 180). The same actor is **"the agent"** in the browser (BrowserView.tsx:881, 918), in workflows (src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:443 and WorkflowLibrary.tsx:323, both "Drafted by the agent"; kinds.ts:12 "Ask the agent") and in the agent graph (AgentGraph.tsx:713); and **"the AI"** in Skills (src/shell/navPrefs.tsx:50, Overlays.tsx:270), the Studio (StudioModal.tsx:53) and Trash provenance, where a row reads "by the AI" (TrashPanel.tsx:24).
  _Problem:_ Four names for one thing force the user to work out, every time, whether "the agent" that took over the browser is the same thing as "the AI" that trashed their file and the same thing as the "Room AI" answering in the transcript. It matters most on the provenance and consent surfaces, where deciding whether to approve or restore depends on knowing who acted. "Room AI" is the worst offender because it is shown most often — on every answer and in every copied transcript (chatActions.ts:949) — and labels no control anywhere.
  _Do:_ Pick "the assistant" — what the pill, the ⌘2 shortcut and the layout presets already say. Change markup.ts:281 to return "Assistant": that single edit fixes the on-screen label and the copied transcript together, which is why the function exists (chatActions.ts:947-949 says so). Then sweep "the agent" → "the assistant" at BrowserView.tsx:881/918, src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:443, WorkflowLibrary.tsx:323, kinds.ts:12, AgentGraph.tsx:713; "the AI" → "the assistant" at src/shell/navPrefs.tsx:50, Overlays.tsx:270, StudioModal.tsx:53, TrashPanel.tsx:24; and rename the aria-labels at AiPane.tsx:172/180 to "the assistant pane". Keep "the AI" only where it means the *model* (ModelSection's capability copy).

- **[low·copy]** Clipboard failures surface a DOMException string as the whole message
  `src/workspace/chatActions.ts:928`
  _Today:_ Of 240 `pushToast` calls in src/, 74 are exactly `pushToast("error", String(e))`. Most are fine — the Rust commands throw written sentences ("That file is no longer in this room.", "No room is open."). The exceptions are the browser APIs: `copyMessage` (chatActions.ts:924-930), `copyConversation` (chatActions.ts:956-959), `copyAllText` (chatActions.ts:962-968), `copyReceipt` (miscActions.ts:186-189) and `copyIssue` (FeedbackModal.tsx:109) all reject with a DOMException.
  _Problem:_ A denied clipboard write shows "NotAllowedError: Write permission denied." as the entire toast — no subject, no verb the user recognises, and no statement of which of the three Copy buttons in the message footer failed. It is also the one error class here whose cause is fully known at the call site (the clipboard, refused by macOS), so printing the exception class is a choice rather than a fallback.
  _Do:_ These five share one shape, so give them one helper — `copyToClipboard(s, text, doneMsg)` — that performs the write and on failure pushes "Couldn't copy — macOS refused clipboard access to Arcelle." Put it beside the other shared workspace helpers rather than in guard.ts, which is the Ollama-down module. Replace the five inline `navigator.clipboard.writeText(...).then(ok, e => pushToast("error", String(e)))` blocks with calls to it: five sites collapse to one, and the message names the act that failed instead of the exception class.

- **[low·ux-defect]** Any job finishing wipes the Studio's step line, even when the Studio run it belongs to is still going
  `src/workspace/effects.ts:272`
  _Today:_ The `onJobProgress` listener clears the Studio step on every terminal tick, without checking whose it is: `if (p.finished || p.paused || p.failed) { …; s.setStudioStep({ text: "", local: true }); … }` (effects.ts:263-273). `p.jobId` is used two lines earlier to delete that job's entry from `s.jobProgress`, but not here. The step is set by a separate `onStudioStep` event (effects.ts:318-320), which carries no job id either.
  _Problem:_ `s.studioStep.text` is one of the six conditions that raise the "Running now" heading (AiPane.tsx:546-552) and the sole condition for the Studio card (AiPane.tsx:580). When any other job ends — a paused summary, a failed create, a dismissed pass — the Studio card and its privacy step ("your cloud AI is writing (content leaves this Mac)", the line effects.ts:315-317 says was the whole point) vanish mid-run, while the Studio job keeps going. The comment at effects.ts:269-271 is right that the line must not outlive its run; it just does not check that the run ending is the one that owns it.
  _Do:_ Make the clear belong to the run. Have `onStudioStep` store the owning job id alongside the text (`{ text, local, jobId }` — the backend already emits per-job progress with an id, and startStudioJob returns one), and at effects.ts:272 clear only when `p.jobId === s.studioStep.jobId`. If plumbing the id is more than this is worth, the smaller honest fix is to clear the step only on `p.finished` for a job whose kind is a Studio kind, taken from the refreshed `s.jobs` list.


### Visual system consistency: tokens, type, spacing, colour, radii, dark mode (16)


- **[high·accessibility]** Every selected row puts --faint on a ground it was never solved against
  `src/styles/shell.css:1461`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:62-68 solves --ink-muted against --hover because '--hover is the lightest ground in dark mode' and records '4.54:1 worst case now' — which is exactly what --faint measures on plain --hover. But --selected (tokens.css:178, 20% berry dark / tokens.css:402, 24% light) is a translucent wash that lands lighter than --hover, and it is what every current row is painted with: shell.css:1445-1447 (.area-nav-row.is-current), 1318-1320 (.source-row.is-current), 1948-1951 and sidebar-search.css:241-244 (.file-row.selected). The secondary line inside those rows stays --faint at 10px — .area-nav-copy (shell.css:1459) and .area-nav-state (shell.css:1466), .source-meta (shell.css:1371), .file-description and .file-size (sidebar-search.css:370, 378, at --fs-micro). Recomputed against the real ground (.pane-library = color-mix(--surface 55%, transparent) over --page, shell.css:718-720): 4.32:1 dark, 4.49:1 light; on --raised 3.64:1; on --hover 3.39:1; --selected-strong on --hover 2.82:1.
  _Problem:_ The row the user's eye rests on longest is the one whose metadata is furthest below the floor, and tokens.css states a worst case that this ground disproves. No value of --ink-muted fixes it, because --selected is translucent: its resulting ground changes with whatever surface is behind the row.
  _Do:_ (1) Step the secondary line up one ink tier when the row is current, using the class names that actually exist: `.area-nav-row.is-current .area-nav-copy, .area-nav-row.is-current .area-nav-state, .source-row.is-current .source-meta { color: var(--muted); }` in shell.css, and `.file-row.selected .file-description, .file-row.selected .file-size { color: var(--muted); }` in sidebar-search.css (NOT `.file-meta` — no such class; FileRow.tsx:96/128 emit .file-description and .file-size). --muted measures 5.43:1 dark / 5.39:1 light on that ground. (2) Correct tokens.css:62-68: --hover is not the floor, --selected over --hover is, and add 'a selected row's metadata steps to --muted; --faint is never legible on --selected or --selected-strong.'

- **[high·ux-defect]** var(--radius-md) is not defined — four Create-page surfaces render with square corners
  `src/styles/create.css:343`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:307-310 defines exactly four radius rungs: --radius-xs 6px, --radius-sm 8px, --radius 10px, --radius-lg 12px. --radius-md does not exist anywhere in the tree. create.css:343 (.cr-tile), 932 (.cr-hero-form), 990 (.cr-shot) and 1308 (.cr-split) each write `border-radius: var(--radius-md)` with no fallback, so the declaration is invalid at computed-value time and, since border-radius is not inherited, all four compute to 0.
  _Problem:_ The image tile, the hero form, the shot card and the split preview — four of the largest bordered surfaces on the Create page — are the only hard-cornered boxes in an app where every other box reads a radius token. On the newest page it reads as a rendering fault.
  _Do:_ Replace all four with `var(--radius)` (10px, the rung between sm and lg that the name was reaching for). Do not add a --radius-md token: four rungs is the scale, and adding a fifth to satisfy four typos moves the wrong way. Note this is one instance of a wider class — see the added item on --sp-5, --sp-7 and --ink-soft, which fail the same way and would be caught by the same lint.

- **[high·copy]** The Density hint names the wrong pages as the rough ones
  `src/settings/InterfaceSection.tsx:96`
  _Today:_ InterfaceSection.tsx:95-99 reads 'Compact tightens spacing and type across the app. The densest pages — Settings, Connectors, Workflows — are tuned for Comfortable and give back less room than the rest.' tokens.css:435-441 says the same. Measured, settings.css is the opposite of what the copy claims: 0 of its 57 font-size declarations are raw px. The two sheets that ignore the scales hardest are shell.css (36 of 76 font sizes tokenised, 20 of 82 paddings) and create.css (23 of 73 font sizes) — neither mentioned. shell.css is the sidebar, tab strip and status bar.
  _Problem:_ The app tells the user which surfaces will disappoint them and names the wrong ones. Someone who switches to Compact to fit more rows in the sidebar gets nothing there, having just been told the sidebar was fine. It is a small untruth in a product whose whole argument is that it does not tell them.
  _Do:_ Copy first, because it is wrong today: change InterfaceSection.tsx:96-98 to 'Compact tightens spacing and type across the app. The sidebar, the tab strip and the Create page are tuned for Comfortable and give back less room than the rest.' and update tokens.css:435-441 to match. Then do the shell.css sweep (the sub-floor item above, plus the 12.5px item added below, which is the change that actually gives Compact something in the sidebar). Put Settings back in the sentence only if a later measurement earns it.

- **[high·test-gap]** Nothing checks the design system — no lint would have caught any of the above
  `scripts/preflight.sh:113`
  _Today:_ package.json's scripts run tsc, the Vite build, a page-script contract suite, cargo test, pytest, ruff and clippy; scripts/preflight.sh:113-120 is the suite block. There is no stylelint, no CSS-aware step and no devDependency for one. qa/check-mock-coverage.mjs (invoked at preflight.sh:99) is the precedent for a bespoke node checker. As a result nothing prevented four undefined `var(--radius-md)` from shipping, nothing catches the eight further undefined-token declarations (--sp-5, --sp-7, --ink-soft), nothing flags a font-size below --fs-micro, and nothing notices when a paper.css class loses its last consumer.
  _Problem:_ Every fix in this review is a one-time sweep that will silt back up. tokens.css carries pages of hard-won invariants — the 12px floor, the contrast solve, symmetric radii, 'a mark is drawn and a box corner is not' — and not one of them is enforced by anything but a reader's memory. Two of the highest-impact findings here are literally undefined variables, which is the cheapest possible thing to check.
  _Do:_ Add `qa/check-tokens.mjs`, modelled on qa/check-mock-coverage.mjs, asserting four things over src/**/*.css: (a) every `var(--name)` is defined in tokens.css or carries an explicit fallback — this alone catches --radius-md ×4, --sp-5 ×4, --sp-7 ×1 and --ink-soft ×7 today; (b) no `font-size` literal below 12px; (c) no raw hex outside a per-file allowlist — derive it from the 16 declarations that actually exist (misc-moonshot-cards.css's print block, slides.css, officedoc.css, svg.css, viewer-formats.css, composer.css, agent/driver.css), not from a guess, and drop browser.css and agentGraph.css from it since they have none; (d) every `.nb-*` class defined in paper.css appears at least once in src/**/*.tsx. Wire it as `suite "Design tokens" node qa/check-tokens.mjs` at preflight.sh:114.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Three more tokens are referenced and never defined — 12 declarations silently dropped
  `src/styles/create.css:328`
  _Today:_ --radius-md is not the only one. I diffed every `var(--name)` referenced in src against every `--name:` defined, and four names are referenced with no definition and no fallback. --sp-5: create.css:328 (`padding: var(--sp-7) var(--sp-5)`), create.css:771, create.css:1142, create.css:1400, browser.css:922 (`margin-top`). --sp-7: create.css:328. --ink-soft: recording.css:1020, 1037, 1040, 1043, 1946 (all `color:`) and waveform.css:294 (`border-inline-start: 1px dashed var(--ink-soft)`). tokens.css defines --sp-1, -2, -3, -4, -6, -8 only, and no --ink-soft in either theme block. Each of these is invalid at computed-value time: the padding/margin declarations fall to their initial 0; `color:` is inherited, so those five rules set nothing and the text takes its parent's colour; and waveform.css:294 is a shorthand, so the whole border-inline-start is dropped — border-style falls to `none` and the chapter divider does not draw at all. (--mk-text at paper.css:406 and --nb-val/--nb-step/--nb-trace-col/--mono-h are the correct pattern by contrast: all carry explicit fallbacks.)
  _Problem:_ Five Create-page and browser surfaces are padded to zero on one or both axes, five recording metadata lines lost their intended ink tier and quietly inherit whatever is above them, and the waveform's chapter divider — a 1px dashed rule marking where a chapter starts — is invisible. All of it looks like sloppy layout rather than a typo, so nobody has traced it back to four missing token names.
  _Do:_ No new tokens. Replace `var(--sp-7) var(--sp-5)` at create.css:328 with `var(--sp-6) var(--sp-4)` and the three lone `var(--sp-5)` at create.css:771, 1142, 1400 plus browser.css:922 with `var(--sp-4)` (16px — the rung between the 12px and 24px they sit among). Replace all seven `var(--ink-soft)` with `var(--faint)`: recording.css:1037/1040/1043/1946 and waveform.css:294 are metadata and a decorative rule, and recording.css:1020 (.rec-found-at, a button) can take `var(--muted)` since it is interactive. Then the lint proposed in the review's test-gap item earns its keep on day one: rule (a) catches all 12 of these plus the four --radius-md sites.

- **[high·consistency]** 86 font sizes sit below the floor the type scale declares, and none of them respond to Density
  `src/styles/shell.css:1967`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:272 defines --fs-micro: 12px as 'the smallest size allowed anywhere', and the compact block deliberately leaves it unchanged (tokens.css:461, '--fs-micro deliberately unchanged: it is the floor'). 86 declarations write a smaller literal anyway: 46 at 11px, 19 at 10px, 10 at 11.5px, 5 at 10.5px, 4 at 9.5px, 2 at 9px. They cluster in shell.css (28) and create.css (28), then composer.css (12) and workspace.css (6). The smallest are .settings-nav-heading (shell.css:1967, 9.5px), .nav-chip (shell.css:2645, 9.5px), .activity-history-note (shell.css:2158, 9.5px) and .cr-attached-role (create.css:1257, 9px, uppercase mono in --faint). Overall 145 of 930 font-size declarations are raw px across 24 distinct values, against a scale that defines 8 rungs. Because they are literals, Compact moves nothing in the sidebar, the tab strip, the Create page or the composer.
  _Problem:_ The always-visible chrome is set below the app's own legibility floor, and the setting meant to tighten the app cannot reach the densest surface in it. A 9.5px uppercase heading in --faint is the hardest thing in the product to read, and it is the label above the Settings nav.
  _Do:_ Sweep in two passes, ordered by what the user sees most. Pass 1, shell.css only: 11px/11.5px → var(--fs-micro); 10px/10.5px/9.5px → var(--fs-micro) as well, since there is no rung below it and that is the point. Pass 1 loses 10 of shell.css's 28 for free if the dead Studio/Activity band goes (item below). Pass 2, create.css then composer.css, same mapping. Where the result genuinely no longer fits — .nav-chip (shell.css:2643-2650) and .settings-nav-heading (shell.css:1964-1970) are the two likely cases — shorten the copy or drop the label rather than the size.

- **[medium·consistency]** Six destination titles, three sizes, four weights — two of them a weight the font does not have
  `src/styles/create.css:39`
  _Today:_ Four rail destinations agree on --fs-page / --display / 600 / -0.02em: .rh-title (workspace.css:506), .mem-title (skills.css:1154), .sk-title (skills.css:106) and — apart from weight — .rec-home-title (recording.css:1754), which is 700. Two do not: .cr-title (create.css:39-46) is --fs-section / 800 / -0.015em and .conn-title (marketplace.css:95) is --fs-section / 600, the latter with a comment at marketplace.css:94 explaining the choice. Separately, Space Grotesk is bundled as a variable face with range 300-700 (fonts.css:53, :62), so the `font-weight: 800` at create.css:42 and gate.css:153 cannot render — it clamps to 700, and the stylesheet says something the browser does not do. Two --display headings inside viewers declare no weight at all: .sk-gallery-head h1 (sketch.css:291-295) inherits the UA h1 bold, .eml-subject (email.css:8-15) inherits body 400.
  _Problem:_ Two real problems, of different sizes. The 800s are a stylesheet lie: the value cannot render on the bundled face, so a reader tuning the title weight is editing a number with no effect. The size split is smaller than it looks — one of the two --fs-section titles is a recorded decision — but .cr-title is a genuine outlier with nothing written down beside it, so Create's masthead sits 18px shorter than the front page's for no stated reason.
  _Do:_ Two edits, not six. (1) Replace `font-weight: 800` with 700 at create.css:42 and gate.css:153 so the declaration matches what Space Grotesk can render. (2) Bring .cr-title (create.css:40-43) onto the majority rung — --fs-page, weight 600, -0.02em — and check .cr-head (create.css:32-38), a flex space-between row whose controls may need to wrap at 44px. Leave .conn-title alone or change marketplace.css:94 first: reversing a written decision silently is how the next reader loses trust in the comments. While in marketplace.css, fix that comment's stale reference — find.css has no page title rule. .rec-home-title's 700 and the two viewer headings (sketch.css:291, email.css:8) are a separate, lower-value tidy; give them an explicit 600 only if someone is already editing those files.

- **[medium·consistency]** .activity-progress is drawn twice, and the shell copy uses the hue reserved for something else
  `src/styles/shell.css:2086`
  _Today:_ shell.css:2086-2092 draws .activity-progress as a 3px track on --hover with a plain 999px radius; 2093-2099 fills it with `display: block; height: 100%; width: 0; background: var(--accent); transition: width 0.3s ease`. aiPane.css:442-462 draws the same class as an 8px transparent track with an inset --rule hairline and a wobbled radius, filled with a four-stop --sem-linked-fill gradient on --dur-slow/--ease-pen. The .pane-ai version wins everywhere, since .activity-progress renders only inside .pane-ai (AiPane.tsx:569, 595, 630, 1014) — but it inherits display/height/width from shell's rule, and restates none of them.
  _Problem:_ A reader opening shell.css finds a progress bar that is berry, 3px and eased on a browser default curve, with no way to know it never paints — and berry is the hue tokens.css:129-143 reserves for saved/AI attribution, not for an in-flight job. The one that does paint is the correct one, which makes the dead copy actively misleading about the app's progress language.
  _Do:_ Fold into the shell.css deletion above, with the geometry preserved: move `display: block; height: 100%; width: 0` from shell.css:2094-2096 into aiPane.css's `.pane-ai .activity-progress span` (450) and drop the rest of shell.css:2086-2099, including `transition: width 0.3s ease` — aiPane.css:461 already specifies --dur-slow/--ease-pen and that is the app's motion. Do not delete the span rule without moving those three declarations first.

- **[medium·accessibility]** Three text fields drop the app's focus ring for a colour-only signal
  `src/styles/create.css:297`
  _Today:_ base.css:75-82 establishes one focus indicator — `outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: 2px` on button/input/textarea/select/[tabindex]:focus-visible. Three rules remove it and put nothing equivalent back. (1) create.css:293-301 sets `outline: none` in the base `.cr-search input` rule; that selector is (0,1,1), the same weight as `input:focus-visible`, and create.css imports at App.css:40 against base.css at App.css:13, so it wins the tie — the only remaining signal is `.cr-search:focus-within { border-color: var(--accent) }` (create.css:290-292), a 1px hue change. (2) create.css:497-501 `.cr-field:focus` sets `outline: none` plus `box-shadow: 0 0 0 3px var(--accent-soft)`; --accent-soft is berry at 18% (tokens.css:186), barely above the field's own ground. (3) aiPane.css:728-731 `.pane-ai .pod-host-name:focus` sets `outline: none` and changes only border-bottom-color. One more substitutes a lighter ring: shell.css:1185-1189 `.file-sort select:focus-visible` sets `outline: none` with `box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px var(--accent)` — half the app's weight, and inset rather than offset. `.search-field:focus-within` (shell.css:1202-1205) is NOT in this class: it suppresses nothing, and the input inside it keeps base.css's 2px ring.
  _Problem:_ tokens.css:41-43 states the app's own rule — 'status is never signalled by colour alone' — and these four controls signal keyboard focus by colour alone or by a ring half the app's weight. A keyboard user tabbing through the Create page's search field, a Create text field, or the AI pane's host name cannot tell where they are. Every other suppression in the tree (composer.css:335, browser.css:349, paper.css:996) is documented and paired with a :focus-within ring on the wrapper that fires in the same state; these three are not.
  _Do:_ Delete `outline: none` at create.css:297 and create.css:498 and let base.css draw the ring; drop the --accent-soft glow at create.css:500, which is not legible enough to be doing work. At aiPane.css:729 delete `outline: none` and keep the border-bottom accent as reinforcement. At shell.css:1188 change `0 0 0 1px` to `0 0 0 2px` so the substituted ring matches the app's weight (inset is right — the control sits in a clipped strip). Leave shell.css:1202-1205 alone.

- **[medium·consistency]** .file-row.selected is written twice with different spine widths, and the documented one loses
  `src/styles/sidebar-search.css:241`
  _Today:_ sidebar-search.css:236-244 carries a four-line comment — 'The marker spine is the shape that carries it (paper.css's .nb-edge idiom, written out here because the row's class list is built in FileRow.tsx); the wash and the strong ink reinforce it, so the state never depends on the pen being distinguishable from the paper' — over `.file-row.selected { background-color: var(--selected); box-shadow: inset 3px 0 0 var(--accent); }`. shell.css:1948-1951 declares the same selector with `background: var(--selected); box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 var(--accent);` and no comment. Both are (0,2,0); sidebar-search.css imports at App.css:19 and shell.css at App.css:42, so shell wins and the spine renders at 2px. The base `.file-row` is doubled the same way: sidebar-search.css:222 and shell.css:1944 (border-radius + min-height).
  _Problem:_ The rule that explains itself is the rule that does not render, and the one that renders says nothing. Anyone tuning the selected-row spine will edit the commented copy in sidebar-search.css, see no change, and conclude the box-shadow is being clipped — while the actual 1px difference (3px vs 2px against a --selected wash that is already the weak signal in the contrast finding above) makes the documented reasoning slightly less true than it was written to be.
  _Do:_ Delete shell.css:1943-1951 (`.file-row` and `.file-row.selected`) and fold its two unique declarations — `border-radius: var(--radius-xs)` and `min-height: 33px` — into sidebar-search.css:222, which already owns .file-row, its hover, its picked states and its selected .file-name. One definition, next to the comment that explains it, and the spine goes to the documented 3px.

- **[medium·consistency]** The paper colour is written out five times outside tokens.css
  `src/theme.ts:47`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:47 and :356 define --page as #151716 (dark) and #f4f1e8 (light). The same two literals are repeated in four more places, each with its own reason: theme.ts:47 (`const bg = theme === "light" ? "#f4f1e8" : "#151716"`, for the anti-flash <html> background), index.html:2 (the inline `style="background-color: #151716"` on <html>), index.html:30 (the pre-hydration theme script) and index.html:62 (`var(--launch-bg, #151716)`), plus viewers/monacoSetup.ts:160 and :181, which build the editor theme from a `page:` hex per mode. theme.ts:44-46 acknowledges it in a comment: 'These are the notebook's paper colours and must stay identical to --page in tokens.css and to the inline script in index.html.'
  _Problem:_ Five copies of one hue, kept in step by a comment. If the paper colour is ever retuned, four of the five will move and the fifth will show as a flash of the old paper on launch or a mismatched editor gutter — the kind of defect that appears only on cold start, which is the moment least likely to be under test.
  _Do:_ Two of the five are unavoidable (index.html:2 and :30 run before any stylesheet). Remove the other three: read `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--page')` in theme.ts:47 instead of the literal, and in monacoSetup.ts:160/181 build the Monaco theme from the same computed value rather than a second hardcoded pair. Then add the two surviving index.html literals to the token lint from the test-gap item as a fifth rule: assert index.html's two hexes equal tokens.css's --page in each theme block. That turns a comment into a check without adding a runtime dependency.

- **[medium·consistency]** The sidebar's primary row label is an off-scale 12.5px in six places, and Compact cannot move it
  `src/styles/shell.css:1457`
  _Today:_ Seven rules set `font-size: 12.5px`, six of them in shell.css: 1369 (.source-name), 1457 (.area-nav-title), 1566, 1750 (.pop-menu-item), 1976 (.settings-nav-item) and 2254, plus viewer-formats.css:232. That is the primary label of every contextual-sidebar row, every pop-menu item and every Settings nav item — the most-read text in the app's chrome after body copy. It sits between --fs-micro (12px) and --fs-body (14px), matching no rung, and because it is a literal it does not move under `data-density="compact"`. The sub-floor sweep in the review does not touch these, since 12.5px is above the floor.
  _Problem:_ The sub-floor item fixes the metadata under these rows but leaves the row titles themselves off the scale, so the sidebar stays the one region where the type ladder does not apply — and the Density setting still gives it nothing, which is precisely the untruth the Density-copy item is about. Half-sweeping this leaves the sidebar in the same half-and-half state tokens.css:290-293 says is worse than either extreme.
  _Do:_ Map all seven 12.5px declarations to `var(--fs-meta)` — 13px comfortable, 12px compact. Comfortable gets 0.5px larger (imperceptible, and it puts the label on a named rung); Compact finally shrinks the sidebar's row titles, which is the single change that makes the Density hint true for the sidebar. Do it in the same commit as the shell.css sub-floor pass so the whole file lands on the scale at once, and re-check .area-nav-row's 33px min-height and .settings-nav's 158px fixed width, which were tuned around the old size.

- **[medium·consistency]** One uppercase eyebrow label, 59 instances, 27 different type recipes
  `src/styles/shell.css:1964`
  _Today:_ 59 rules set `text-transform: uppercase`, across roughly 27 distinct combinations of size, tracking and weight. Only about 30 fall in the top four recipes (--fs-micro with 0.12em/600, 0.06em/600, 0.04em/600, 0.075em/600). The rest are singletons: .settings-nav-heading (shell.css:1964-1970) at 9.5px/0.075em/500, .studio-section-title (shell.css:1824-1829) at 10px/0.07em with no weight declared, .group-heading (shell.css:1249) at 10px, and one-offs at 0.03em, 0.05em, 0.08em, 0.09em, 0.1em, 0.14em and 0.18em with weights 500, 600, 650 and 700 mixed through. tokens.css's type section (255-278) stops at family, size and line-height: there is no tracking token and no weight scale anywhere in the file.
  _Problem:_ The section label is the app's most repeated typographic unit and its least specified. Scrolling Settings, the sidebar, Activity and the marketplace shows the same kind of label at four weights and seven trackings, which is what makes a page read as assembled rather than designed.
  _Do:_ Add `--fs-eyebrow: var(--fs-micro)` and `--track-eyebrow: 0.08em` at tokens.css:278, plus one class in paper.css: `.nb-eyebrow { font-size: var(--fs-eyebrow); font-weight: 600; letter-spacing: var(--track-eyebrow); text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--faint); }`. Convert the 27 recipes onto it — the ~22 already at --fs-micro/600 are a find-and-replace, and the five hardcoded 9.5-11px ones get promoted to the floor in the same edit, overlapping the sub-floor sweep. Do not add a full weight scale: 600 for eyebrows and labels, 400 for body, 700 for display titles is the whole vocabulary the app needs, and it belongs in tokens.css as three sentences rather than three tokens.

- **[medium·consistency]** The spacing scale reaches under half the app's padding and gaps
  `src/styles/shell.css:1419`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:311-316 defines --sp-1 through --sp-8 on a 4px grid, and tokens.css:432-434 states 'roughly 500 rules resolve through these, so the whole app tightens from one place'. Measured: 322 of 714 padding declarations read a --sp token (45%). Of 696 gap declarations the raw px values are mostly off-grid: 6px ×72, 2px ×39, 5px ×31, 4px ×22, 3px ×22, 7px ×19, 1px ×19, 9px ×7. Padding literals follow the same shape — '3px 10px', '2px 9px', '1px 7px', '7px 10px', '6px 10px', '5px 9px'. shell.css is worst at 20 of 82 paddings tokenised; .area-nav-row (shell.css:1419-1423) is representative at `padding: 6px 7px; gap: 6px`.
  _Problem:_ Half the app's rhythm is set on a 1px grid rather than a 4px one, which is why a sidebar row never quite lines up with the panel header above it, and why Compact gives the sidebar nothing. It is the largest item here by volume and the slowest to land, which is why it ranks below the sweeps that touch one file each.
  _Do:_ Do not attempt a global sweep. Add the one rung the app has already converged on — `--sp-1half: 6px` at tokens.css:312 AND a compact value (4px) in the compact block at tokens.css:445-452, or it becomes the single rung density cannot move. Then convert file by file in the order the user meets them: shell.css, then sidebar-search.css, then composer.css. Map 5-7px → var(--sp-1half), 9-11px → var(--sp-3), 3px → var(--sp-1). Stop there and measure. The pages tokens.css:437-441 already concedes are hardcoded (marketplace, workflows, browser) are not worth the churn until someone is editing them anyway.

- **[low·consistency]** seal.css re-declares the motion system on :root
  `src/seal.css:23`
  _Today:_ tokens.css:318-322 defines the motion vocabulary — --dur-fast 160ms, --dur 200ms, --dur-slow 260ms, --ease-pen cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.72, 0.3, 1) — and calls the 160-260ms band the spec. seal.css:22-26 opens a second `:root` block with --seal-ease: cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1) (Material Design's standard curve, not the pen curve), --seal-unlock-dur 0.52s and --seal-lock-dur 0.46s, consumed at seal.css:39, 59, 74, 136, 157, 172 and 284. seal.css is imported from App.tsx:37 after App.css, so it wins. Its header comment at line 4 still calls --accent 'the pink pen', which the berry repalette superseded (the same stale phrase survives at base.css:141).
  _Problem:_ The app's one signature moment — the lock and unlock ritual — is the only motion in the product that does not move on the app's own curve. The pen curve decelerates late, the Material curve early; next to every panel and menu in the app the seal feels borrowed.
  _Do:_ Delete `--seal-ease` (seal.css:23) and replace its seven uses with var(--ease-pen). Keep --seal-unlock-dur and --seal-lock-dur but move them into tokens.css beside --dur-slow with one line recording that the seal is the deliberate exception to the 160-260ms band — so the exception lives where the rule is rather than 300 lines away in another file. Note rooms/constants.ts:21 holds the matching JS timeouts and must stay in step. Fix 'pink pen' → 'berry pen' at seal.css:4 and base.css:141 while there.

- **[low·consistency]** Seven stale blue --accent fallbacks in the sketch viewer
  `src/viewers/sketch.css:472`
  _Today:_ sketch.css writes `var(--accent, #7aa2ff)` at lines 472, 480, 491, 493, 502, 583 and 624, plus `var(--page, #fff)` at 479. #7aa2ff is a cornflower blue from a palette the app no longer has — tokens.css:182 sets --accent to --mk-berry-ink, and tokens.css:214-224 gives blue a reserved meaning ('linked, informational, active, connected'). These eight are the only var()-with-hex-fallback sites in the entire tree, and none of them can fire: --accent and --page are always defined on :root.
  _Problem:_ Not a rendering bug, but a trap: the fallback documents a wrong default, and anyone copying one of these lines into a context where --accent is scoped away — an injected overlay, a future iframe'd surface — gets a blue selection handle in a berry app, on a hue the token file has reserved for a different meaning.
  _Do:_ Strip the fallbacks at all eight sites: plain `var(--accent)` and `var(--page)`. The token lint's rule (a) then keeps new ones from appearing, and this is the right shape for that rule — a fallback is exactly what hides the staleness.


### Accessibility and keyboard operation across every page (21)


- **[high·ux-defect]** The expanded agent graph is unreachable by keyboard
  `src/workspace/AgentGraph.tsx:329`
  _Today:_ Expand (AgentGraph.tsx:284-291) portals the overlay to `document.body` (createPortal at 320) as `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` (329-331) with no focus management of any kind. Escape closes it via a window listener (188-196). The comment above the portal asserts 'a modal at the end of the body is where the focus order wants it anyway'.
  _Problem:_ That comment is the bug. Focus stays on the Expand button, which lives inside the chat pane; the modal is the last element in `<body>`. Tab from Expand therefore continues into the chat pane behind the backdrop, and reaching the dialog means tabbing through the rest of the application. In practice only Escape does anything, so the roomy graph, its node inspector and the per-agent reports are mouse-only — and unlike the other portalled layers this one is not even backed by a focus trap.
  _Do:_ Use `useFocusTrap` here as well (see the previous item): `const { modalRef, onModalKeyDown } = useFocusTrap(() => setExpanded(false))`, then `ref={modalRef} tabIndex={-1} onKeyDown={onModalKeyDown}` on `.agraph-modal` (AgentGraph.tsx:327-331), and delete the bespoke window Escape effect at 188-196. The hook's mount effect focuses the first control inside (the modal's Close button) and its cleanup returns focus to Expand.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Escape is a dead key in the three Create-page sheets
  `src/workspace/create/PicturePicker.tsx:53`
  _Today:_ PicturePicker.tsx:53, DocumentPicker.tsx:54 and FilmReview.tsx:78 all render `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` scrims with no keydown handler anywhere in the file. The only dismissal is the `cr-pick-x` ✕ button, or in FilmReview its own action buttons. Neither PicturePicker nor FilmReview moves focus into the sheet on open (DocumentPicker's filter input is the only autoFocus of the three).
  _Problem:_ Every other sheet in this app closes on Escape and the shortcuts sheet teaches it as a rule ('Esc — Close the menu, sheet, or open file', Overlays.tsx:313). Here Escape either does nothing — when focus is in the picker's filter input, effects.ts:876-880 treats it as typing and returns — or, from any other focus with a file tab open, falls through to effects.ts:880 and closes the FILE underneath while the picker stays on screen. FilmReview is the sheet that stands between the user and a paid generation run, so the missing escape hatch lands on the most consequential dialog on the page.
  _Do:_ Wrap all three with `useFocusTrap` (same fix as the previous item): `ref={modalRef} tabIndex={-1} onKeyDown={onModalKeyDown}` on the `.cr-pick-scrim` / `.cr-review-back` div, with `e.stopPropagation()` on Escape as ApproveCard does (Overlays.tsx:203-207) so the app-level handler never sees it. That is Escape, the trap and focus-on-open in one line each. Additionally change FilmReview.tsx:78 from `role="dialog"` to `role="alertdialog"`, matching UnsavedEditsDialog.tsx:85, since it is a confirm gate rather than a picker.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Two popovers let Escape close the file behind them
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:873`
  _Today:_ The viewer's overflow menu (`role="menu" aria-label="More actions on this file"`, ViewerPane.tsx:874) is dismissed only by the `menu-backdrop` onMouseDown at 867-870; the file contains no Escape handling for it at all (the only `Escape` in ViewerPane.tsx is the rename input's, line 667). The workflow icon picker at src/workspace/workflows/WorkflowDetail.tsx:311 is identical: a `menu-backdrop` onMouseDown at 310 and nothing else.
  _Problem:_ With either menu open, Escape reaches effects.ts:880 and closes the open file — the user asked to dismiss a menu and lost the document instead, with the menu still drawn over whatever replaced it. For a keyboard user the workflow icon picker has no dismissal at all: no Escape, and the backdrop is a mouse-only affordance, so choosing an icon is the only exit. TopBar solved exactly this with one shared slot (TopBar.tsx:93-108, 'One dismissal grammar for the toolbar popovers') and neither of these was brought onto it.
  _Do:_ Move ViewerPane's overflow onto the existing single slot: add `"viewer-overflow"` to the `TopMenu` union (state.ts:64-70) and drive the popover from `s.openMenu === "viewer-overflow"` / `s.setOpenMenu`, deleting the local `useState` at ViewerPane.tsx:325 and the reset `useEffect` at 326. That inherits TopBar's capture-phase Escape for free (TopBar is always mounted — Workspace.tsx:757). For WorkflowDetail.tsx:311, which is outside TopBar, adopt the exported `useMenuKeys` from the next-but-one item, which brings Escape and arrow keys together.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Arrow keys inside the sketch object list silently move the shape
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1230`
  _Today:_ The objects strip is a proper `role="listbox" aria-multiselectable="true"` (SketchView.tsx:1806-1811) whose chips are `role="option"` buttons carrying `aria-selected`, `aria-posinset` and `aria-setsize` (1818-1828) — and no key handling of their own. Separately, a window keydown registered at SketchView.tsx:1247 nudges the selection on ArrowLeft/Right/Up/Down (`const move = step[e.key]`, 1230; Shift moves a whole GRID_GAP) whenever `selectedRef.current.length` is non-zero, bailing only when `document.activeElement` is an INPUT or TEXTAREA (1177).
  _Problem:_ A listbox tells a screen-reader user 'use the arrow keys'. They Tab to an object chip, click or press Enter to select it, press ArrowRight expecting the next option — and the shape moves one unit on the canvas instead. Nothing announces the edit, the list does not advance, and ⌘Z is the only way back from a change the user never knew they made. The one control built specifically so a non-sighted user could reach the drawing is the control that damages it.
  _Do:_ Give `.sk-objects-row` (SketchView.tsx:1806) roving-tabindex arrow handling — ArrowLeft/Right move `document.activeElement` between chips via the existing `chipRefs` map, Home/End to the ends, Enter/Space to select, Shift+Enter to add to the selection — and call `e.stopPropagation()` on those keys so the window nudge at 1230 never sees them. Set `tabIndex` to 0 on the focused chip and -1 on the rest so the strip is one Tab stop instead of one per object. The canvas nudge stays exactly as it is for the canvas itself.

- **[high·ux-defect]** Sketch's window keydown deletes objects from anywhere in the app
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1242`
  _Today:_ SketchView.tsx:1247 registers a `window` keydown for the whole time a sketch is mounted, returning early only for INPUT and TEXTAREA (1177). Backspace/Delete calls `deleteSelected()` (1242-1245); a bare v/p/r/o/a/t/e switches tool through `KEY_TOOL` (1237, table at 118-126); ⌘-combinations for undo, select-all, duplicate and ordering are on the same listener.
  _Problem:_ The sketch is the centre pane; the assistant pane and the sidebar are on screen beside it with their own focusable buttons. Focus a button in the AI pane (Stop, a job's Resume, a chip) with shapes still selected, press Delete, and the drawing loses those objects from a keypress aimed at a different pane. Typing 't' while focus is on a sidebar file row silently changes the drawing tool. Sidebar's browser-page rows already bind Backspace/Delete to 'close this page' (Sidebar.tsx:1196) and call only `preventDefault()`, so in that layout one Delete closes a page AND deletes the selected shapes.
  _Do:_ Scope the listener to the sketch.
