Keep the `window` registration (the canvas SVG is not focusable) but gate every branch on `pageRef.current?.contains(document.activeElement)`, where `pageRef` is a new ref on the `.sk-page` wrapper (SketchView.tsx:1281). Nothing else changes: pointer interaction already puts focus inside the page, and the toolbar, the object chips and the label field are all descendants of it.

- **[high·accessibility]** The assistant's answer is never announced
  `src/workspace/ChatPane.tsx:903`
  _Today:_ The streaming turn at ChatPane.tsx:903-904 mounts, fills with tokens and is replaced by the stored message with no live region and no aria-busy. The pane's only role="status" is the privacy receipt (ChatPane.tsx:973), which renders only when `!s.asking && s.askPrivacy`. Exactly four aria-live attributes exist in all of src/ (Toasts.tsx:43, AiActionModal.tsx:215, BrowserReader.tsx:288, BrowserSearch.tsx:493) and none is the chat. The only thing that announces during a turn is AgentGraph's `p.agraph-sr` role="status" (AgentGraph.tsx:295) — and that renders only when `s.agentPlan` is non-empty, so an ordinary one-model answer announces nothing at all.
  _Problem:_ The primary output of the product is silent to a screen reader. Press Enter and nothing is announced: no 'working', no 'answer ready', no distinction between the model still thinking, the run being stopped, and an error. The two visible states the sighted user gets — the 'Thinking locally…' / 'Asking your cloud AI…' route line (ChatPane.tsx:962-968) and the stream cursor — are plain spans, and the cursor is deliberately aria-hidden.
  _Do:_ Do NOT make the stream a live region — token-by-token announcement is unusable. Add one polite `role="status"` sibling inside the message list of ChatPane, keyed off `s.asking`, carrying the same sentence the route line already shows: `Thinking locally…` / `Asking your cloud AI — content leaves this Mac…` while asking, then `Answer ready` (or the error text / `Answer stopped`) when the stored message replaces the streaming row. Copy the shape of AgentGraph.tsx:295 (`<p className="agraph-sr" role="status">`), which already does exactly one sentence for the agent roster. Add `aria-busy={s.asking}` to the `.msg.assistant.is-streaming` div at ChatPane.tsx:903 so a reader stops re-reading a half-written bubble.

- **[high·accessibility]** The ⌘K palette moves a selection nobody is told about
  `src/workspace/Overlays.tsx:1057`
  _Today:_ The palette input (Overlays.tsx:1056-1068) is a plain `<input className="search-input" aria-label="Search this room or run a command">` with `onKeyDown={onPaletteKey}`. `onPaletteKey` (531-542) moves `s.searchSel` on ArrowUp/ArrowDown and runs it on Enter, and the sheet even prints the contract — '↑↓ to move · Enter to run · Esc to close' (1142). But the selected row is only a CSS class: `className={`search-result action ${s.searchSel === idx ? "sel" : ""}`}` (1179) for commands, and the same `selectedIndex` comparison inside `SearchResultRows` (SearchExpanded.tsx:676-698 and the rows below it). There is no `role="combobox"`, no `aria-expanded`, no `aria-controls`, no `aria-activedescendant`, no `role="listbox"` and no `role="option"` anywhere in either file — `grep -n 'combobox\|aria-activedescendant\|role="option"' src/workspace/Overlays.tsx src/workspace/SearchExpanded.tsx` returns nothing.
  _Problem:_ ⌘K is the app's front door — files, chat messages, memories and every command in one list. A screen-reader user opens it, types, hears the result count from the `find-count` live region (1114), then presses ArrowDown and hears nothing at all: focus never leaves the input, and the row that Enter will run is marked only by a colour. So Enter is a blind commit into a list that includes 'Export all files…', 'Save a checkpoint' and 'Start a live recording'. ComposerPane solved exactly this for the @-mention palette, with a comment saying so.
  _Do:_ Copy ComposerPane's markup, which is already in the tree: on the search input at Overlays.tsx:1056 add `role="combobox" aria-expanded={...} aria-controls="palette-listbox" aria-activedescendant={`palette-opt-${s.searchSel}`} aria-autocomplete="list"` (ComposerPane.tsx:397-403), wrap the `.search-results` container at 1069 in `id="palette-listbox" role="listbox"`, and give every row — the command buttons at 1176-1186 and the rows `SearchResultRows` renders — `id={`palette-opt-${idx}`} role="option" aria-selected={idx === selectedIndex}`. The index plumbing (`selectedIndex`, `registerRowRef`) already exists; this adds no state and no new control.

- **[medium·consistency]** Four containers claim role="toolbar" and none implements it
  `src/workspace/Sidebar.tsx:553`
  _Today:_ `role="toolbar"` appears at Sidebar.tsx:553 (`{n} files selected`), TrashPanel.tsx:128, MarkdownEditor.tsx:90 (`Formatting`) and SketchView.tsx:1283 (`Drawing tools`). None of the four has a keydown handler or a roving tabindex; every child button is its own Tab stop. BrowserView.tsx:927 states the rule in a comment — 'A GROUP, not a `toolbar`: `role="toolbar"` promises arrow-key navigation between its controls, and these are plain tab stops. Claiming the role without the behaviour is a lie to assistive tech' — and is the only place it is followed.
  _Problem:_ A screen reader announces 'toolbar' and the user presses arrow keys, which do nothing. In SketchView it is worse than nothing: those same arrows fall through to the window handler at SketchView.tsx:1230 and nudge the drawing. Meanwhile the seven tool buttons (TOOLS, SketchView.tsx:73-117) plus the colour, arrange and zoom triggers are ten-odd consecutive Tab stops between the canvas and the rest of the page.
  _Do:_ Two choices, per container. For SketchView.tsx:1283, implement the promise: roving tabindex across the buttons inside `.sk-tools`, arrows to move, one Tab stop for the group — a drawing toolbar is the case the pattern exists for, and it also stops those arrows reaching the canvas nudge. For Sidebar.tsx:553, TrashPanel.tsx:128 and MarkdownEditor.tsx:90, take the promise back: change `role="toolbar"` to `role="group"`, keeping each `aria-label` exactly as it is. That is a net deletion of behaviour the app claimed and never had, in the wording BrowserView.tsx:927 already uses.

- **[medium·accessibility]** A file row's selected state is invisible to a screen reader
  `src/workspace/FileRow.tsx:31`
  _Today:_ `<div className={`file-row…`} aria-selected={picked}>` — FileRow.tsx:22-31. The div has no `role`, and no ancestor is a listbox or grid (Sidebar.tsx:1294's `role="list"` is the sketches list). The clickable part inside is a plain `<button className="file-main">` (FileRow.tsx:80). Multi-select comes from `a.clickFile(f, { meta, shift })` (82) and ⌘A (Sidebar.tsx:541-543). The comment at FileRow.tsx:29-30 asserts 'A picked row is a checkbox in every way that matters to a screen reader'.
  _Problem:_ `aria-selected` on a role-less div is dropped by every browser's accessibility mapping, so the state that makes SelectionBar's Move / Export / Remove safe ('7 selected', Sidebar.tsx:553-556) is announced on nothing. A screen-reader user can press ⌘A, hear the count, and then walk the list unable to tell which rows are in the set they are about to move or delete.
  _Do:_ Move the state onto the control that already exists: put `aria-pressed={picked}` on the `.file-main` button (FileRow.tsx:80) and delete `aria-selected` from the wrapper div (FileRow.tsx:31), correcting the comment above it. That is honest with no structural change — the row genuinely is a toggle. A real listbox would need a `role="listbox"` ancestor plus roving tabindex across hundreds of rows, which is a far larger change than this row warrants.

- **[medium·copy]** The shortcuts sheet under-reports what the keyboard actually does
  `src/workspace/Overlays.tsx:303`
  _Today:_ `SHORTCUTS` (Overlays.tsx:303-351) lists five groups: Around the room, Tabs, Panes, Writing, and 'Reading a PDF' (⌘+ / ⌘- / ⌘0). Its header comment (298-302) says 'Keep it in step with the real handlers' and names six files. Missing: the whole sketch vocabulary (v/p/r/o/a/t/e at SketchView.tsx:118-126, arrow and Shift+arrow nudge at 1224-1235, ⌘Z/⇧⌘Z at 1194, ⌘A at 1199, ⌘D at 1207, ⌘[/⌘] at 1211-1222, and the same ⌘+/⌘-/⌘0 at 1178-1191); BookView's ArrowLeft/Right and PageUp/PageDown (BookView.tsx:131-134); SheetView's cell arrows and Enter/F2 (SheetView.tsx:727-740); RecordingView's tab arrows and Home/End (1466-1477); BrowserSearch's j/k (191-192); the library's ⌘A and Esc-to-clear (Sidebar.tsx:536-543); Delete-to-close on a browser page row (Sidebar.tsx:1196-1199).
  _Problem:_ This sheet is the app's only discovery route for its own keyboard — ⌘/ opens it and nothing else teaches these keys. Under-reporting is this product's honesty rule pointed inward: the app answers a dozen shortcuts it does not admit to, so a user reasonably concludes the sketch page and the spreadsheet are mouse-only, and the sheet quietly implies ⌘+ belongs to the PDF when SketchView answers it too.
  _Do:_ Add three groups to the `SHORTCUTS` array: 'Drawing' (the sketch keys), 'Reading' (book page keys, sheet cell arrows, recording tab arrows) and 'The Library' (⌘A, Esc to clear the selection, Delete to close a browser page). Move the ⌘+/⌘-/⌘0 row out of 'Reading a PDF' into one 'Zooming' line reading 'Zoom in, out, or fit — in a PDF, an image or a drawing', and rename that group to 'Reading'. Same file, same data shape, no new surface.

- **[medium·accessibility]** What is reaching the internet is only readable with a mouse
  `src/shell/StatusBar.tsx:92`
  _Today:_ StatusBar.tsx:92-105 renders a non-focusable `<span className="status-item warn">` whose visible text is `Internet tools on` and whose `title` carries the breakdown built from `Online search is on` and `${mcpToolCount} connected tools`. The file count (90) and the offline notice (113-118) use the same title-on-a-span pattern. The trust chip at 82-88 is a real `<button>` but its detail (`${trust.title} (${engineLabel})`) is also title-only, while the visible label is just `trust.label`.
  _Problem:_ A `title` on a span is hover-only: not announced, not focusable, no keyboard route. The single readout that says which of the user's data paths are open to the network is therefore readable by a mouse user and by nobody else — in an app whose stated value is never implying privacy it does not deliver. The file's own comment at 72-73 gets this exactly right for the seal icon and then does the opposite ten lines later.
  _Do:_ Make the breakdown visible text rather than a tooltip. Build the same array the `title` uses at StatusBar.tsx:94-101 and render it as the label — `Online search on · 3 tools` — then delete the `title`. The strip already runs at 12px by deliberate policy, so the width exists. Do the same on the trust chip at 82-88: append `engineLabel` to the visible label instead of hiding it in the tooltip.

- **[medium·accessibility]** Deleted transcript words are read aloud as if they were still in the recording
  `src/viewers/RecordingView.tsx:2211`
  _Today:_ With 'Show deleted' ticked (`showDeleted`, RecordingView.tsx:580, filter at 819-820, checkbox at 2095), a soft-deleted word renders as `<span className="rec-word deleted">` (2211). The styling is careful — `color: var(--muted)`, `text-decoration: line-through`, `text-decoration-color: var(--sem-urgent)` (recording.css:1097-1103) with a comment noting 'the SHAPE says removed, the colour only reinforces it' — but `line-through` on a `<span>` is CSS only and reaches no accessibility API.
  _Problem:_ In the one view that exists to show what has been cut, a screen reader reads a cut word exactly like a kept one. The distinction that decides what playback skips (armCutSkip, RecordingView.tsx:916) and what 'Export edited copy' will drop for good is carried entirely by a decoration, so the mode is useless to the person who cannot see the strike — and the words they hear read out are the ones already removed from the recording.
  _Do:_ Render the deleted branch as `<del className="rec-word deleted">` instead of `<span>` at RecordingView.tsx:2208-2218. `<del>` maps to the deletion role and is announced; the existing `.rec-word.deleted` selector still matches, so nothing about the drawing changes and the CSS file is untouched.

- **[medium·accessibility]** The sketch canvas is declared an application but can never be focused
  `src/viewers/SketchView.tsx:1612`
  _Today:_ SketchView.tsx:1607-1614: the canvas is `<svg className="sk-canvas…" role="application" aria-label={canvasSummary}>` with pointer handlers and no `tabIndex`, and a redundant `<title>{canvasSummary}</title>` inside it at 1619. `canvasSummary` (1272-1276) reads e.g. 'Drawing canvas, 12 objects, 2 selected'.
  _Problem:_ `role="application"` tells assistive tech to stop intercepting keys and hand them to the element — which only takes effect once focus is inside it, and focus can never get inside because the element is not focusable. The role therefore does nothing except suppress the reader's own navigation model over a region it cannot enter, making the honest, useful summary harder to reach than it would be with no role at all. The duplicated name is a second, smaller problem: `aria-label` wins, so the `<title>` at 1619 is dead code.
  _Do:_ Change `role="application"` to `role="img"` at SketchView.tsx:1612 and keep `aria-label={canvasSummary}` — the canvas genuinely is a picture whose interactive equivalent is the objects listbox below it (1806). Delete the `<title>` at 1619. If the canvas should become enterable later that is `role="application"` plus `tabIndex={0}` plus a documented in-canvas key model, which is far more work than the objects strip already delivers.

- **[medium·accessibility]** The folder collapse control announces a triangle
  `src/workspace/Sidebar.tsx:746`
  _Today:_ A folder header (Sidebar.tsx:731-777) contains two buttons that do the same thing. `<button className="folder-caret-btn" title={collapsed ? "Expand" : "Collapse"} onClick={() => a.toggleFolderCollapse(folder.id)}>{collapsed ? "▸" : "▾"}</button>` (746-752) and `<button className="folder-label" onClick={() => a.toggleFolderCollapse(folder.id)}>` with the name and count (769-777). Neither carries `aria-expanded`, and neither has an `aria-label`.
  _Problem:_ Because the caret button has text content ('▸'), that content is its accessible name and the `title` is never used — it is announced as '▸ button'. The label button announces 'Projects 12 button' with no indication that it toggles anything or what state it is in, so a screen-reader user cannot tell a collapsed folder from an expanded one, and gets two adjacent Tab stops that do the identical thing on every folder row in the library.
  _Do:_ Delete the `folder-caret-btn` element (Sidebar.tsx:746-752) and move the glyph inside the `.folder-label` button as `<span className="folder-caret" aria-hidden>{collapsed ? "▸" : "▾"}</span>`, then put `aria-expanded={!collapsed}` on that button (769). One control instead of two, one Tab stop per folder instead of two, and the state is announced rather than drawn.

- **[medium·consistency]** Two more tablists promise arrow keys they do not answer
  `src/workspace/AiPane.tsx:106`
  _Today:_ `<div className="assistant-header" role="tablist" aria-label="AI tools">` (AiPane.tsx:104-107) holds three `role="tab"` buttons (111, 122, 133) with `aria-selected` and good labels — and no keydown handler, no roving tabindex, no `aria-controls`, and no `role="tabpanel"` on the views they switch. EngineModelPicker.tsx:157 is the same shape and additionally has no `aria-label` on the tablist. The app already has two correct implementations: TabStrip.tsx:102-130 ('a `role="tablist"` owes Left/Right (and Home/End)') and RecordingView.tsx:1464-1478's `onTabKey`, whose panel at 2107 is the only `role="tabpanel"` in src/.
  _Problem:_ Chat / Studio / Activity is the assistant pane's primary navigation. A screen reader announces 'tab, 1 of 3' and the user presses Right — nothing happens; each tab is instead a separate Tab stop, and there is no `aria-controls` linking a tab to what it changed, so after activating one the reader has no route to the panel. It is the same broken promise as the `role="toolbar"` item, in a place the user hits on every session.
  _Do:_ Give AiPane.tsx:104 the handler TabStrip.tsx:102-130 already contains (Left/Right wrap, Home/End, roving `tabIndex` so the strip is one Tab stop) and add `id`/`aria-controls`/`role="tabpanel"` between each tab and the view it selects, as RecordingView.tsx:2107 does. For EngineModelPicker.tsx:157, which is two mutually exclusive filters over one list rather than a set of panels, take the promise back instead: `role="group" aria-label="Where the model runs"` with `aria-pressed` on the two buttons.

- **[medium·consistency]** The token-budget breakdown calls itself a dialog and behaves like nothing
  `src/workspace/TokenBudgetBar.tsx:135`
  _Today:_ The trigger at TokenBudgetBar.tsx:79-86 declares `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded={open}`. What opens is `<div className="pop-menu token-breakdown-pop" role="dialog">` (135) containing only static rows of text; it has no `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`, no `aria-modal`, nothing focusable inside, and no key handling — the only dismissal is the `menu-backdrop` onMouseDown at 134.
  _Problem:_ An unnamed `role="dialog"` is announced as 'dialog' with no name, and a reader that jumps to it finds no focusable content and no way out; Escape falls through to the app-level handler at effects.ts:880 and closes the open file instead. It is not a dialog at all — it is a disclosure showing the same numbers the bar draws, and calling it one is the same overclaimed-role problem as the four `role="toolbar"` containers.
  _Do:_ Delete `role="dialog"` from TokenBudgetBar.tsx:135, leaving a plain `<div className="pop-menu token-breakdown-pop">`, and change the trigger at 82 from `aria-haspopup="dialog"` to nothing — `aria-expanded` alone is the correct disclosure semantics for a panel that follows its button in the DOM. Then drop the `menu-backdrop` at 134 and close on blur/second click, removing one more mouse-only div.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The skills source picker binds Escape to one input, not the sheet
  `src/workspace/skills/SkillsView.tsx:662`
  _Today:_ `<div className="sk-source-picker nb-float nb-float-draw" role="dialog" aria-label="Choose source files">` (SkillsView.tsx:662) opens over the skill composer. Its only Escape handler is on the filter input — `onKeyDown={(event) => { if (event.key === "Escape") setSourcePickerOpen(false); }}` (673-677). There is no backdrop, no focus trap, and no `aria-modal`; the file list below is a column of `<label>` checkboxes (683-697) and a 'Done' button (665).
  _Problem:_ Escape works only while focus is still in the filter field. Tab once — into the first checkbox, which is what you opened the picker to do — and Escape stops closing the sheet and instead reaches effects.ts:880, closing the open file behind it while the picker stays on screen. The user's dismissal key silently changes meaning one Tab stop in.
  _Do:_ Move the handler off the input and onto the picker div at SkillsView.tsx:662: `onKeyDown={(e) => { if (e.key === "Escape") { e.stopPropagation(); setSourcePickerOpen(false); } }}`, so it fires wherever focus is inside and never reaches the app-level handler. Since this popover is non-modal and has real focusable content in DOM order after its trigger, drop `role="dialog"` rather than adding a trap — `aria-expanded` on the trigger button (652-661) plus a labelled `<section>` is the honest description.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** The 'Add to Library' confirm is a dialog with no way back out
  `src/workspace/ViewerPane.tsx:175`
  _Today:_ The library chip's confirm popover — `<div className="pop-menu library-chip-pop" role="dialog" aria-label="Add to Library">` (ViewerPane.tsx:175) — is raised by a button declaring `aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-expanded={asking}` (163-168). It holds a sentence and Cancel / Add buttons (182-196) and is dismissed only by the `menu-backdrop` onMouseDown at 174. Nothing focuses it on open and, as established for the overflow menu in the same file, ViewerPane has no Escape handling at all.
  _Problem:_ It is announced as a dialog, but Escape does not cancel it — it falls through to effects.ts:880 and closes the file the popover is asking about, leaving the popover drawn over whatever replaced it. Focus is not moved in either, so a screen-reader user is told a dialog opened and then has to find it by tabbing. This is a consent surface: 'Add' changes where a file appears in the room.
  _Do:_ Add `onKeyDown` to the popover div at ViewerPane.tsx:175 handling Escape with `e.stopPropagation()` and `setAsking(false)`, and `autoFocus` on the Cancel button (183) so the safe choice takes focus — the same shape ApproveCard uses (Overlays.tsx:203-207). If ViewerPane's overflow moves onto the shared `openMenu` slot as proposed above, put this popover on the same slot and delete its `menu-backdrop` (174) too.

- **[medium·ux-defect]** Undo lives on a toast that expires before you can Tab to it
  `src/workspace/toastStack.ts:24`
  _Today:_ `NOTICE_MS = 5000` / `OFFER_MS = 12000` (toastStack.ts:23-24). `toastLifeMs` (70-75) gives 12s only to a toast that carries BOTH an `about` and an `action`; an action toast with no `about` waits to be dismissed. Exactly one toast in the app is about+action: the Library link/unlink Undo (fileActions.ts:736-740). The action is a `<button className="subtle accent toast-action nb-btn-go">` (Toasts.tsx:52-64) inside a `position: fixed` stack at the end of the layout (shell.css:1775; `pointer-events: none` on the container at settings.css:1582, `auto` on `.toast` at 1600). The 12s life is a bare `window.setTimeout` in state.ts:655-659 whose id is discarded, so nothing can pause or cancel it.
  _Problem:_ The toast is not focused when it appears and there is no shortcut to it, so reaching Undo means tabbing forward from wherever focus is until you arrive at a fixed element near the end of the document — inside 12 seconds. For a keyboard user that is not reliably reachable, and because the timeout id is thrown away, hovering or focusing the toast cannot buy any more time. The stack does announce itself (aria-live="polite", Toasts.tsx:43), so a screen-reader user is told an Undo exists and then cannot get to it.
  _Do:_ Keep the id: have `pushToast` store the `window.setTimeout` handle alongside the toast in state.ts:655-659, and expose a `holdToast(id)` / `resumeToast(id)` pair that Toasts.tsx calls from `onFocus` / `onBlur` on the `.toast` div (React's focus events bubble, so one pair on the toast covers its buttons). A user who reaches the toast is then not raced. Do not extend the timer — a longer-lived toast is more clutter, not more reach — and do not add a global Undo shortcut for a single toast whose inverse act is permanently available from the file's own Library chip (ViewerPane.tsx:164-171).

- **[low·accessibility]** One icon-only button in the app still has no accessible name
  `src/settings/ModelSection.tsx:140`
  _Today:_ ModelSection.tsx:140-146: `<button className="subtle btn-ic" title={m === model ? "Can't delete the active model" : "Delete model from disk"} disabled={m === model} onClick={() => askRemoveModel(m)}><TrashIcon size={14} /></button>`. Its accessible name comes only from `title` (the icon carries no text), and the name changes meaning between the enabled and disabled states. It is not the only such button — Sidebar.tsx:746's folder caret announces '▸' / '▾' because its text content wins over its title, and BookView.tsx:178/186 announce 'A−' / 'A+'.
  _Problem:_ `title` as the sole name is exposed by WebKit but is the weakest source in the accname order and is ignored by some screen-reader configurations; here it names the delete control for a downloaded model, where the name has to be unambiguous. The app's own standard everywhere else — including the confirm buttons ten lines above at ModelSection.tsx:123-137 — is `title` plus `aria-label`.
  _Do:_ Fold this into replacing the hand-rolled confirm with the shared `DeleteControl` (see the added item below): `DeleteControl` already sets `aria-label={title}` on its trigger (DeleteControl.tsx:82-90), so the label arrives by construction with `title={`Delete ${m} from disk`}`. If the confirm is left as it is, the minimum is `aria-label={`Delete ${m} from disk`}` on line 141, matching how `Close ${pageAccessibleName(p)}` and `Rename folder` are written elsewhere.

- **[low·accessibility]** The waveform's chapters and marks are mouse-only
  `src/viewers/Waveform.tsx:452`
  _Today:_ Waveform.tsx:452-461 draws each section start as a percentage-positioned `<span className="waveform-chapter" title={c.title}>` containing a visible `waveform-chapter-label`. Saved marks are `<span className="waveform-saved-mark" aria-hidden="true">` (444-451) and the selection band is aria-hidden too (462-468). Seeking happens through wavesurfer's `interaction` event (290) on a canvas with no role and no tabIndex. Nothing in the component is focusable — the file contains no `<button>` and no `tabIndex`.
  _Problem:_ On a two-hour recording the chapters are the shape of the meeting — 'a two-hour recording a shape you can read without scrubbing it', per the file's own comment at 98-99. Their names are readable but inert: the only way to jump to one is to click it. A keyboard user can still play the file (the shared `<audio controls>` is native) but cannot navigate its structure. Saved marks — the moments the user deliberately flagged — are aria-hidden, so they are not merely unreachable but absent.
  _Do:_ Do not make the canvas focusable. Change the elements that are already drawn: make each `waveform-chapter` span at Waveform.tsx:452-461 a `<button className="waveform-chapter" onClick={() => onSeek?.(c.at)} aria-label={`${c.title} at ${fmtStamp(c.at)}`}>`, keeping its existing percentage positioning, and do the same for `savedBands` at 444-451 — drop `aria-hidden` and give each `aria-label={`Mark at ${fmtStamp(b.at)}`}`. Leave the selection band aria-hidden; it is a reflection of a selection made elsewhere. This is the move RoomMap already made for its canvas ('the canvas is mouse-only by nature, so this is the keyboard / screen-reader route', RoomMap.tsx:246-248) and it adds no new panel.


### Where the checks are missing across all capabilities — grounding pass over e2e/, qa/, sidecar/tests/, #[cfg(test)] blocks in src-tauri/src/, scripts/preflight.sh and package.json scripts. Every file and line below was opened and read; verdicts distinguish "the code does what the item says" from "the file is right but the description misreads it". (18)


- **[high·test-gap]** Three source-scan tests can never fail: the needle is written in the test's own line
  `src-tauri/src/web/fetch.rs:673`
  _Today:_ `the_redirect_chain_is_bounded` (fetch.rs:671) binds `let src = include_str!("fetch.rs")` at :673 and asserts `src.contains("redirect(reqwest::redirect::Policy::none())")` at :675. `include_str!` includes line 675 itself, so the assertion matches its own text. Two more of the same shape in browser.rs: the `map_err(|_| EVAL_TIMED_OUT.to_string())` / `EVAL_LOST` pair asserted at :2470-2471 over the whole file bound at :2468, and `src.contains("window.__arcelleSuperseded = 1")` at :2571. Five sibling self-scans in the same file (browser.rs:1847, 2039, 2118, 2155, 2211, 2302) do NOT have the trap — each slices to a function body or composes its needle with `format!`.
  _Problem:_ All three assertions hold regardless of production code. Delete `.redirect(reqwest::redirect::Policy::none())` at fetch.rs:89 and reqwest auto-follows redirects — a hop resolved a second time, unpinned, past `guarded_get`'s private-address guard — and `cargo test` still says ok. Delete the superseded mark at browser.rs:1240 and the readiness probe answers from the page being navigated away from; the test still says ok. The repo already knows this class and fixes it three ways elsewhere (commands/browse.rs:1190, commands/library.rs:152, db/versions.rs:576), with commands/browse.rs:1186-1189 recording that a mutation is what proved the unsliced version could not fail. These three sites were missed.
  _Do:_ Apply the commands/browse.rs:1191 remedy at all three: slice the included source to production text before searching — `let src = include_str!("fetch.rs"); let src = &src[..src.find("\n#[cfg(test)]").unwrap()];` — in fetch.rs:673, browser.rs:2468 and browser.rs:2561. In browser.rs:2561 keep lines 2562-2569 (the READY_JS slice) exactly as they are and slice only for the :2571 assertion. Then stop the class recurring with a `#[test] fn no_self_scan_searches_its_own_test_module()` in src-tauri/src/browser.rs that scans the four self-scanning files for an `include_str!("<own file>")` binding used without a slice or a composed needle, and fails naming the line.

- **[high·test-gap]** scripts/accuracy-tests.sh is the only thing that ever transcribes audio, and nothing invokes it
  `scripts/accuracy-tests.sh:1`
  _Today:_ 13 transcription tests are `#[ignore]`d — 8 in recording.rs (:3089, :3211, :3315, :3425, :3571, :4249, :4423, :4543), 4 in stt.rs (:630, :662, :777, :807), 1 in commands/stt_cmds.rs (:968). `scripts/accuracy-tests.sh` runs all three module filters (:83-85), prints which model copy answered (:59-62) and treats a "0 passed" run as a failure (:77-80). It is invoked nowhere: not in package.json scripts, not in preflight.sh, not in RELEASING.md, not in ci.yml. The only mention in the tree is e2e/page-script/accuracyTests.test.mjs:24-38, which checks the script's MODEL_FILE/MODEL_URL stay in step with stt.rs but never runs it.
  _Problem:_ The app's most expensive claim — your meeting was transcribed on-device — has no automated check on any machine, and the machine that could run it is the one running preflight: preflight.sh:80-91 already asserts `resources/models/ggml-large-v3-turbo-q5_0.bin` is present and not a stub, then never puts a second of audio through it. CI stubs the weight on purpose (ci.yml:80-82). So the transcription pipeline is proven present and never proven working — the exact shape of the v0.15.0 regression the script's own header (:11-15) says shipped transcription dead while every local test stayed green.
  _Do:_ Add `"test:accuracy": "scripts/accuracy-tests.sh --no-download"` to package.json and invoke it from the full `scripts/preflight.sh` run only — inside the `if [[ $SUITES_ONLY -eq 0 ]]` branch that already checks the weights (preflight.sh:74-91), so `npm test` stays fast. Add one line to RELEASING.md step 3 (:131-138). `--no-download` is the right flag there: preflight has just asserted the weight is present, so a download at tag time means the weight check lied.

- **[high·test-gap]** The mock-coverage report treats a missing READ the same as a missing mutation
  `qa/check-mock-coverage.mjs:122`
  _Today:_ `const uncovered = sorted(frontend).filter((c) => !mocked.has(c))` (:122) is printed as one number at :128 and never sets `failed` — deliberate, per the header at :15-19 ("most of it is mutations, which QA does not need faked… a number to watch, not a build break"). Today that number is 120 of 294 invoked (59% faked). The list mixes mutations with loaders: office_html, slide_preview, stage_preview_html, list_stranded_checkpoints, mcp_runtime_for_command, suggest_file_meta.
  _Problem:_ The two classes are not equivalent and the repo says so elsewhere: an unfaked mutation is a control the harness never presses; an unfaked read is a pane rendering from nothing, which "looks perfectly healthy otherwise" (e2e/README.md:80-81). Lumping them into one figure hides the only class that can produce a false green inside a number nobody acts on. The file already maintains the distinction for a different purpose — `EXTRA_READS` (:101-114) exists precisely to name loaders whose prefix is not `list_`/`get_`.
  _Do:_ Split the report in check-mock-coverage.mjs: classify an uncovered command as a read when it matches `^(list|get|read|load|fetch)_` or appears in a new hand-written `READ_LIKE` set seeded with office_html, slide_preview, stage_preview_html, mcp_runtime_for_command and suggest_file_meta. Print "reads with no fixture" as its own line. Land the fixtures for those five (and list_stranded_checkpoints) in the SAME change, then set `failed = true` on a non-empty read list — a gate that is red the day it ships gets disabled, which is how this number became decorative in the first place. Mutations stay a reported figure.

- **[high·test-gap]** Touch ID has no test anywhere, including the sentence a locked-out user reads
  `src-tauri/src/biometrics.rs:44`
  _Today:_ `src-tauri/src/biometrics.rs` (163 lines) has no `#[cfg(test)]` block — one of exactly 13 files out of 155 under src-tauri/src without one. `map_err` (:44) turns an OSStatus into user-facing copy and lives inside `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")] mod imp` (:21-22) taking a `security_framework::base::Error`, so no test can call it. Its branches are cancel -128 (:46), errSecAuthFailed -25293 (:47), errSecItemNotFound (:48), errSecMissingEntitlement -34018 (:52) and a catch-all (:55-57) that appends `[code {code}]` after the password sentence. On the frontend, touchid_enable, touchid_disable and touchid_open are all unfaked in qa-mock.js, so the Settings row is never driven either. The one related check, e2e/page-script/unlockMessage.test.mjs:20-24, regex-slices `unlockMessage` out of src/App.tsx — the frontend's copy, not the host's.
  _Problem:_ This is the door to the room. The copy at :46-57 is load-bearing honesty: every branch except cancel and not-found must still tell the user "You can still unlock with your password", because a bare OSStatus reads as the room being gone. Nothing verifies that. The -34018 branch (unsigned or sandboxed run, no Secure Enclave) is the one a developer build hits most often, so a regression there gets dismissed as "works on my machine".
  _Do:_ Lift the mapping out of `mod imp` into biometrics.rs proper as `pub(crate) fn message_for(code: i32) -> String`, and have the macOS `map_err` call `message_for(e.code())`. Add `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` with one case per branch — -128, -25293, errSecItemNotFound, -34018, and an arbitrary unknown code — plus a loop asserting every branch except cancel and not-found contains "password", so no future branch can leave the user without the way back in. That also removes the `#[cfg(target_os)]` fence from the only part of the file worth testing.

- **[high·test-gap]** The deck and book parsers have no test, while their shared helper does
  `src/viewers/pptx.ts:206`
  _Today:_ `src/viewers/pptx.ts` (335 lines; `slideOrder` at :206, `parsePptx` at :236) and `src/viewers/epub.ts` (274 lines; `opfPath` at :44, `parseEpub` at :93, `chapterHtml` at :166) are named by no test in the tree. A reader showing chapter 7 as chapter 2 is a correctness failure that looks like a working feature, and registry.test.mjs cannot see it.
  _Do:_ Add two sections to `e2e/page-script/viewerparse.test.mjs` — it already has the loader and already imports zipdoc.ts, so no new file or dependency is needed. pptx: a hand-built `files` record whose rels graph disagrees with filename order (slide10 listed before slide2 in presentation.xml) must come back in rels order, not sorted order; and a presentation.xml whose sldId rels point at entries that do not exist must fall through to the filename sort rather than returning a short deck. epub: `opfPath` prefers container.xml's `full-path` over the any-`.opf` fallback at :49; `parseEpub` returns chapters in spine order for a manifest whose ids sort differently; a spine itemref with no manifest entry is dropped rather than yielding an undefined chapter.

- **[high·test-gap]** Nothing ties RULE_LIST_ID to the rules it caches, so an edited blocker ships stale
  `src-tauri/src/browser/rules.rs:246`
  _Today:_ `pub const RULE_LIST_ID: &str = "arcelle-browse-v3"` (:246) with a doc comment at :243-245: "Bump the suffix whenever the rules change, or WebKit serves the previously compiled list from its cache and edits here silently do nothing." browser.rs:1732 passes it to WebKit's rule-list store as the cache key. rules.rs has five tests (:253, :274, :293, :314, :349) and they are good ones — :293 even pins the two regex constructs WebKit refuses to compile, with :402-406 hand-rolling a matcher rather than pulling in a regex crate. Not one of them relates RULE_LIST_ID to `rules_json()`. The only check on the rule is a manual, still-unticked line in qa/UA-FEATURE-CHECKLIST.md:954.
  _Problem:_ The rule is stated in prose and enforced by memory. Edit `PRIVATE_URL_FILTERS` or a tracker filter without touching the ID and every test in rules.rs still passes — they read the freshly-generated JSON — while the shipped browser attaches the previously compiled list from WebKit's cache. The user's chip still reads "Private" because the attach succeeded; it just attached last version's rules. That is the app's honesty failure in its purest form: a true-looking claim over a stale mechanism, invisible to the whole suite.
  _Do:_ Add a `#[test] fn the_rule_list_id_moves_when_the_rules_do()` to rules.rs's existing test module: compute a stable digest of `rules_json()` (a hand-rolled FNV-1a over the bytes is enough — no new dependency, in the spirit of `simple_regex_matches` at :406) and assert it equals a constant written next to RULE_LIST_ID. Any rule edit fails that test with a message saying "bump RULE_LIST_ID and update RULES_DIGEST". Then tick or delete UA-FEATURE-CHECKLIST.md:954, which currently asks a human to do by hand what the test would do on every build.

The failure mode is in this repo's own history: the reading-view fix shipped inert (a viewport borrow taken twice, released once) past a fully green suite, and driving the app by hand found four more defects the same day. A release can be tagged with a workspace that throws into its error boundary on mount.

- **[medium·test-gap]** The embedding backfill decides what the model can find, and has no test
  `src-tauri/src/commands/retrieval/backfill.rs:145`
  _Today:_ `src-tauri/src/commands/retrieval/backfill.rs` (224 lines) has no `#[cfg(test)]` block, while retrieval.rs next door has a full test module. `spawn_embedding_backfill` (:145) bumps `state.embed_generation` (:150) and spawns `backfill_embeddings` (:165) carrying the stamp; that loop re-reads the stamp before collecting a batch (:173) and again before writing vectors back (:208), and also re-checks `room.path` (:213). Its two siblings in the same file — `spawn_reextract_backfill` (:26) and `spawn_legacy_text_repair` (:90) — instead pin `state.room_epoch()` (:37/:57, :108/:119/:135) with comments saying a rollback swapped the DB and pre-rollback bytes must not be written.
  _Problem:_ Two things, neither checked. First, nothing exercises the stamp at all: the three-way decision (stale generation → return, room closed → return, different path → return) is buried in an async loop with no test, and its visible failure is not a crash but retrieval quietly answering from the wrong corpus, or a room whose chunks keep their old vectors forever because a stale pass held the slot. Second, `backfill_embeddings` is the one pass in the file that does NOT pin the room epoch, so a Time Machine rollback that swaps the DB behind the same path at the same generation lets vectors computed from pre-rollback chunk text land on the restored room's chunk ids — exactly the hazard the comments at :35-36 and :117-118 were written about.
  _Do:_ Two small changes in one edit. (1) Add `state.room_epoch()` to the write-back guard at backfill.rs:208-215, captured alongside `path` at :171-180, matching the siblings' pattern. (2) Extract the decision into a pure `fn pass_is_current(carried_gen: u64, current_gen: u64, carried_path: &str, room_path: &str, carried_epoch: u64, room_epoch: u64) -> bool` and add `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` pinning four cases: superseded generation stops; a different room path stops; a same-path room whose epoch moved (rollback) stops; the current pass continues. No async, no Ollama. Separately, split the orphaned doc comment at :16-19 off `spawn_reextract_backfill` and move it onto `spawn_embedding_backfill` at :145, which currently has none.

- **[low·honesty]** A doc comment describing the embed pass is orphaned onto the re-extraction pass
  `src-tauri/src/commands/retrieval/backfill.rs:16`
  _Today:_ Lines :16-19 ("ADD-13: kick off the lazy background embed pass … Bumps the embed generation (so any older pass exits) and spawns exactly one loop carrying the new stamp") are immediately followed by :20-25, a second, unrelated doc comment about re-extracting files imported before an extractor improvement, and both are attached to `pub(crate) fn spawn_reextract_backfill` at :26. The function they describe first, `spawn_embedding_backfill`, is at :145 and carries no doc comment at all.
  _Problem:_ The file's only written explanation of the generation stamp — the mechanism that decides whether a background pass is allowed to write vectors — is filed on the wrong function, and the function it describes has no documentation. Anyone reading `spawn_reextract_backfill` is told it bumps a generation, which it does not (it pins the room epoch instead), and anyone reading `spawn_embedding_backfill` is told nothing.
